Structure of Massive Gauge/Gravity Scattering Amplitudes, Equivalence Theorems, and Extended Double-Copy with Compactified Warped Space
Yanfeng Hang, Wei-Wei Zhao, Hong-Jian He, Yin-Long Qiu

Abstract
We study the structure of scattering amplitudes of massive Kaluza-Klein (KK) states in the compactified 5-dimensional warped gauge and gravity theories. We present systematic formulations of the gauge theory equivalence theorem (GAET) and the gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET) for warped KK theories in R ξ R_{\xi} gauge, where the GAET connects the scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons to that of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons and the GRET connects the scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons of helicity-zero (helicity-one) to that of the corresponding gravitational scalar (vector) KK Goldstone bosons. We analyze the structure of 3-point and 4-point scattering amplitudes of massive KK gauge bosons and of massive KK gravitons as well as of the KK Goldstone bosons at tree level. We first prove the GAET and GRET explicitly for the fundamental 3-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes. We then demonstrate that the validity of the GAET and GRET for 4-point gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes can be reduced to the validity of GAET and GRET for 3-point gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes. With these, we study the double-copy construction of KK scattering amplitudes in the warped gauge/gravity theories. We newly realize the double-copy for massive 3-point full gauge/gravity amplitudes under the color-kinematics correspondence and the gauge-gravity coupling correspondence at tree level, whereas we can construct extended double-copy for 4-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes to the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion. We further demonstrate that this LO double-copy construction can be extended to N N -point KK scattering amplitudes with N ⩾ 4 N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt . JHEP 02 (2025) 001 [arXiv:2406.12713 [hep-th]].



1
Introduction

Scattering amplitude is an elementary means for studying fundamental forces in nature, and
can bridge theories with experiments. There have been substantial efforts
[
1
]
for studying
various massless scattering amplitudes over the past four decades. Studies on massive scattering amplitudes have attracted increasing interests in recent years,
including to consider some models of massive gauge bosons and gravitons. Among these, the compactified higher dimensional gauge and gravity theories
à la Kaluza-Klein (KK)
[
2
]
[
3
]
stand out as a fundamental approach
for
consistent mass generation
of the excited KK states
of gauge bosons and gravitons.
1
1

1

The KK theories also have important impacts on the experimental side
because the KK compactification predicts an infinite tower of massive KK states
for each known particle of the Standard Model (SM) and Generality Relativity (GR). The low-lying KK states in such KK theories have intrigued
great phenomenological and experimental efforts over
the past two decades
[
4
]
-
[
6
]
,
as they may provide the first signatures for the new physics beyond
the SM, ranging from the KK states of the SM particles to the spin-2 KK gravitons and possible
dark matter candidate.

Simple models of massive gauge bosons and gravitons include such as the
massive Yang-Mills (YM) gauge theory
[
7
]
and massive Fierz-Pauli (FP) gravity
[
8
]

and alike
[
9
]
, which add mass terms by hand and spoil the gauge symmetry or diffeomorphism
invariance. The former is plagued with nonrenormalizability and the latter further suffers
the van Dam-Veltman-Zakharov (vDVZ) discontinuity in the massive graviton propagator
[
10
]
[
11
]
. Moreover, such massive gauge and gravity theories generally exhibit bad high energy behaviors in their
scattering amplitudes involving longitudinally polarized gauge bosons or gravitons. The reason behind such inconsistencies is because the nonconservation of physical degrees of freedom
prohibits a smooth massless limit. In contrast, the KK gauge and gravity theories provide a truly consistent geometric “Higgs” mechanism
for mass generations of KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons whose additional mass-induced physical
degrees of freedom originate from absorbing the extra-dimensional components of the
KK gauge bosons
[
12
]
[
13
]
[
14
]

and KK gravitons
[
15
]
[
16
]
[
17
]
. Hence, this mass-generation mechanism naturally conserves physical degrees of freedom for KK gauge bosons
and KK gravitons, and thus has smooth massless limit. But this geometric mechanism for mass generations of KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons
is realized through the compactification itself and does not invoke any additional Higgs boson
of the conventional Higgs mechanism
[
18
]
.

The geometric mechanism for mass generations of KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons can be
formulated at the level of scattering
S
-matrix through the KK Equivalence Theorem (ET),
as done previously for the
flat 5d KK gauge theories
[
12
]
[
13
]
[
14
]

and the flat 5d KK gravity theories
[
16
]
[
17
]
. It is desirable to extend these KK ET formulations to
both
the KK gauge theories and
KK gravity theories with warped 5d compactification
[
19
]
[
20
]
[
21
]
,
as we briefly discussed in Refs.
[
14
]
[
17
]

(cf. also a recent related study on the warped gravity KK ET
[
22
]
). The warped 5d KK gravity of Randall and Sundrum (RS1)
[
19
]
holds a promise to resolve
the gauge hierarchy problem of the SM and to explain the vast discrepancy between the gravity force
and other three gauge forces. It also predicts distinctive KK mass spectrum and KK signatures
which are important for the on-going experimental searches.

On the other hand, it is conjectured that the scattering amplitudes of gravitons and gauge bosons
are connected by the striking relation of double-copy,
GR
\,=\,
(Gauge Theory)
2
,
which points to the common root of both the gravity force and gauge forces. It also provides a powerful means for efficiently computing the highly complex
scattering amplitudes of spin-2 gravitons. Such double-copy relation was first found in string theories by Kawai-Lewellen-Tye (KLT)
[
24
]
,
which links the scattering amplitudes of massless closed strings to the
products of scattering amplitudes of massless open strings at tree level,
and further leads to the double-copy relation between the scattering amplitudes of massless gravitons
and of massless gauge bosons in the field theory limit. With the inspiration of KLT, Bern, Carrasco and Johansson (BCJ) proposed the double-copy method
in the quantum field theory framework via color-kinematics (CK) duality
[
25
]
[
26
]
,
which constructs the massless graviton amplitudes from the (squared) scattering amplitudes
of massless gauge bosons. It is highly nontrivial to extend the massless double-copy formulation to the massive gauge
and gravity theories. In a recent series of works, the extended massive KLT-type and BCJ-type double-copies
were constructed for the compactified KK string theories
[
27
]
and for the KK
gauge/gravity field theories under toroidal compactification of flat 5d
(without or with orbifold)
[
16
]
[
17
]
[
28
]
. But, it is even more challenging to extend double-copy to the KK gauge and gravity theories with
warped extra dimension such as the RS1. This is because the double-copy for
N
-point
(
N\!\!\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt\!4
) massive KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes was proved
to directly work out only for the toroidal compactification
with flat extra dimensions
[
28
]
. In this work, we will demonstrate that extended double-copy can be realized
for the 3-point full scatttering amplitudes of warped KK gauge/gravity theories,
whereas for the
4
-point warped KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes the extended double-copy
can hold at the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion (which are just what we need to
formulate the ET in both the warped KK gauge and gravity theories).
2
2

2

In passing, the extensions of double-copy
for the topologically massive Chern-Simons (CS) gauge/gravity theories
were presented in
[
29
]
[
30
]
. There are other recent works in the literatue tried to extend
the massless double-copy formulation to the massive double-copies,
including the 4d massive YM theory versus Fierz-Pauli-like
gravity
[
31
]
[
32
]
,
the spontaneously broken YM-Einstein supergravity models
with adjoint Higgs fields
[
33
]
,
the KK-inspired effective gauge theory with extra global
U(1)
[
34
]
,
the 3d CS gauge/gravity theories
with or without supersymmetry
[
35
]
-
[
37
]
,
and the massless QCD/gravity coupled
to massive scalars/fermions/vectors
[
38
]
-
[
40
]
.

In this work, we study the structure of scattering amplitudes of massive KK states
in the compactified 5-dimensional warped gauge and gravity theories. We present systematic formulations of the gauge theory equivalence theorem (GAET) and the
gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET) for warped KK gauge/gravity theories
up to loop level and in the
R_{\xi}
gauge. The GAET connects the scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons to that
of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons, whereas the GRET connects the scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons of helicity-0 (helicity-1) to that of the corresponding gravitational
scalar (vector) KK Goldstone bosons. As a key idea of this work,
we take the GAET of KK Yang-Mills gauge theories as the
truly fundamental ET formulation,
from which we can
reconstruct the GRET
of
the corresponding KK gravity theories by using the double-copy
at leading order (LO) of high energy expansion. Then, we analyze the structure of 3-point and 4-point scattering amplitudes of massive
KK gauge bosons and of massive KK gravitons, as well as their corresponding KK Goldstone bosons. We first prove explicitly the GAET and GRET
for the fundamental 3-point massive KK gauge/gravity scattering
amplitudes. We then demonstrate that the validity of the GAET and GRET for 4-point KK gauge/gravity
scattering amplitudes can be
reduced to
the validity of GAET and GRET for 3-point KK gauge/gravity
scattering amplitudes. With these, we further study
the extended double-copy construction of the KK scattering
amplitudes in the warped gauge/gravity theories. We newly realize the extended double-copy for the
3-point full massive gauge/gravity amplitudes at tree level under proper color-kinematics correspondence
and gauge-gravity coupling correspondence without high energy expansion. Then, we construct extended double-copy for 4-point massive KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes
to the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion. We can derive the GRET from GAET by using the LO double-copy construction for
3-point and 4-point KK scattering amplitudes. Finally, we demonstrate that this LO double-copy construction can be extended
to the
N
-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes with
N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Figure 1
:
Equivalence Theorem and Double-Copy Correspondences from 3-point (3pt) scattering amplitudes
to 4-point (4pt) scattering amplitudes and from massive KK gauge scattering amplitudes to massive KK
gravitational scattering amplitudes in the warped gauge and gravity theories.

Outline and Summary

To make the presentation more transpareent for reading, we provide a summary of our main logic and key results
as shown in the schematic plot of Fig.
1
.

•

Fig.
1
shows the correspondence of the Equivalence Theorems from the GAET to the GRET,
the correspondence of Double-Copies from the 3-point KK scattering amplitudes to the 4-point KK scattering amplitudes, and the correspondence from the massive KK gauge scattering amplitudes to the massive KK gravitational scattering amplitudes.

•

In the lower-left corner of Fig.
1
, we start from analyzing the most fundamental 3-point KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes and establish the GAET between 3-point scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons to the LO of high energy expansion. This analysis will be presented in Section
3.1.1
.

•

Then, along the bottom horizontal arrow we reach the lower-right corner where we compute the
4-point scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the KK Goldstone bosons, as well as
establishing the GAET for these 4-point amplitudes at the LO of high energy exoansion. Here we use
the word “Reconstruction” or “Reduction” to indicate that the validity of the GAET for
4-point KK gauge/Goldstone boson amplitudes can be reduced to (proved from) the validity
of the GAET for 3-point KK gauge/Goldstone boson amplitudes. This analysis will be presented in Section
3.2.1
.

•

Next, in Fig.
1
, we can go from the lower-left corner to the upper-left corner by the double-copy construction
along the left vertical arrow and from the 3-point KK gauge boson amplitudes to the 3-point
KK graviton amplitudes, as well as from the LO 3-point KK Goldstone boson amplitudes to the LO
3-point gravitational KK Goldstone amplitudes. This analysis will be presented in Section
4.1
.

•

Then, along the top horizontal arrow we go from the upper-left corner for 3-point KK graviton amplitudes
to reach the upper-right corner where the 4-point KK graviton amplitudes and
gravitational KK Goldstone amplitudes are computed;
and thus we establish the GRET for these 4-point gravitational KK amplitudes. Here we use the word “Reconstruction” or “Reduction”
to indicate that along the top horizontal arrow
the validity of the GRET for 4-point KK graviton/Goldstone boson amplitudes can be reduced to (proved from)
the validity of the GRET for 3-point KK graviton/Goldstone boson amplitudes. These analyses will be presented in Sections
3.1.2
and
3.2.2
.

•

Finally, we can go from the lower-right corner to the upper-right corner by the double-copy construction
along the right vertical arrow and from the LO 4-point KK gauge (Goldstone) boson amplitudes to the LO 4-point
KK graviton (Goldstone) amplitudes. This analysis will be presented in Section
4.2
. Moreover, we will extend the LO double-copy to the
N
-point
KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes with
N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt

in Section
4.3
, and we give the general prescriptions for
the warped doubel-copy at LO in Section
4.4
.

The rest of this paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, after introducing the warped 5d compactification
with
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold (Section
2.1
),
we present the formulation of GAET in the warped KK gauge theory (Section
2.2
),
and then the formulations of the GRET of type-I,II in the warped KK gravity theory (Section
2.3
). In Section
3
, we study the structure of massive scattering amplitudes from the warped GAET and GRET. These include to first compute the fundamental 3-point scattering amplitudes
of KK gauge bosons (KK Goldstone bosons)
and of KK gravitons (KK gravitational Goldstone bosons),
and to establish the GAET and GRET for 3-point
massive KK amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion (Section
3.1
). Then, we present the 4-point scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons (KK Goldstone bosons)
and of KK gravitons (KK gravitational Goldstone bosons), and establish the GAET and GRET for 4-point
massive KK amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion (Section
3.2
). Our key point is to demonstrate that the validity of the GRET
for 4-point massive KK amplitude can be reduced to
(proved from) the validity of the GAET for basic 3-point massive KK amplitudes. In Section
4
, we study the double-copy construction of the massive KK scattering
amplitudes in the warped gauge and gravity theories. We realize the extended double-copy for the
3-point full gauge/gravity amplitudes at tree level under the proper correspondence of color-kinematics
and the gauge-gravity coupling correspondence without high energy expansion,
whereas the extended double-copy can be constructed for
the 4-point KK gauge/gravity amplitudes to the LO of high energy expansion. We further study the
N
-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes (with
\!N\!\!\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
)
at the leading order and their LO double-copy construction. Finally, we conclude in Section
5
. The four Appendices present supporting materials for our analyses in the main text. Among these, Appendix
A
provides the kinematics of three and four KK-particle scattering processes;
Appendix
B
presents the Becchi-Rouet-Stora-Tyutin (BRST) quantization
for warped 5d KK gravity theory and the detailed derivations of the GRET of type-I and type-II;
Appendix
C
derives the Feynman Rules for warped KK gauge and gravity theories
as needed for the analyses of Section
3
;
Appendix
D
proves the relevant 3-point and 4-point warped identities
for KK masses and KK couplings. Appendix
E
explicitly computes the inelastic longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitude of
nn\!\rightarrow\!mm

and demonstrates the nontrivial large energy cancellations of
E^{10}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{2}
.

2
Formulation of Gauge and Gravitational Equivalence Theorems

with Compactified Warped Space

In this section, we formulate the gauge and gravitational equivalence theorems for the
compactified 5d warped gauge and gravity theories. In section
2.1
,
we present the warped 5d compactification for RS1. In section
2.2
, we formulate the gauge theory ET (GAET) for the warped KK gauge theory. In section
2.3
, we first present the BRST quantization (Sec.
2.3.1
), and
then formulate the gravitational ET (GRET) of type-I (Sec.
2.3.3
) and the GRET of type-II
(Sec.
2.3.2
) for the warped KK gravity theory.

2.1
Warped 5d Compactification

We consider the warped 5d compactification à la Randall and Sundrum
(RS1)
[
19
]
,
where a warped background metric generates
weak scale from Planck scale through an exponential suppression. The background metric involves a five-dimensional non-factorizable warped geometry, derived from a slice of
\mathrm{AdS}_{5}
spacetime. This framework is realized with two 3-branes situated at fixed points of
the
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold compactification, where one 3-brane is the
UV brane (Planck brane) at the fixed point
\hskip 0.85358pty\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

and another 3-brane is IR brane (TeV brane) at the fixed point

\hskip 0.85358pty\!=\!\bar{L}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Here the physical coordinate of the fifth dimension
is denoted as
y
, so we have
\,y\!\in\![0,\bar{L}]
with

\bar{L}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\pi r_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{c}}\hskip 0.85358pt
denoting the size of fifth dimension
and
r_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{c}}
denoting the compactification radius.

The 5-dimensional warped background metric in the physical coordinate takes the following nonfactorizable form:

\text{d}s^{2}={e^{-2ky}\eta_{\mu\nu}}\text{d}x^{\mu}\text{d}x^{\nu}+\text{d}y^{2},

(1)

where
e^{-2ky}
is the warp factor and
\hskip 0.85358ptk\!=\!\!\sqrt{-\Lambda/6\,}\,

is the AdS curvature scale and around the order of Planck mass. (The parameter
\Lambda\,
denotes a negative bulk cosmological constant,
as will be defined in Sec.
2.3.1
.) Then, we can transform the physical coordinates
(x^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt,y)

into the conformal coordinate
\hskip 0.85358pt(x^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt,z)
by
imposing the relation

\hskip 0.85358pt\text{d}y=e^{A(z)}\text{d}z\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, we have the conformally flat 5-dimensional spacetime metric:

\text{d}s^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt=\,e^{2A(z)}\big(\eta_{\mu\nu}\text{d}x^{\mu}\text{d}x^{\nu}\!+\text{d}z^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\big)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(2)

where we have set
A(z)\!=\!-ky\hskip 0.85358pt
. By imposing the relation

\hskip 0.85358pt\text{d}y\!=\!e^{A(z)}\text{d}z\hskip 0.85358pt
, we deduce
\,y\!=\!(1\hskip-0.85358pt/k)\ln(1\!+\!kz)
. So we can further resolve the function
A(z)
in conformal coordinates as follows
[
6
]
:

A(z)=-\ln(1+kz)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(3)

In the conformal coordinates, we have

z\!\in\![0,{L}]
with
{L}\equiv[e^{\hskip 0.85358ptk\bar{L}}\!-\!1]/k\,
,
which corresponds to the physical coordinate
\,y\!\in\![0,\bar{L}]
with

\bar{L}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\pi r_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{c}}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

The Eq.(
2
) shows that the 5-dimensional warped metric tensor

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{g}_{MN}
is conformally flat and can be expressed as

\hat{g}_{MN}=\hskip 0.85358pte^{2A(z)}\hat{\eta}_{MN}\,,

(4)

where the flat 5d Minkowski metric
\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\eta}_{MN}
takes the form:

\hat{\eta}_{MN}=\hat{\eta}^{MN}=\text{diag}(-1,1,1,1,1)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(5)

For clarity of notations, we denote the 5d Lorentz indices by
uppercase Latin letters (such as
M,N\!=\!\mu,5
) and the
4d Lorentz indices by the lowercase Greek letters
(such as
\mu,\nu=0,1,2,3
).

In the next subsections, we will consider the RS1 under
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

compactification for both warped 5d Yang-Mills gauge theory and
the warped 5d GR theory. With these, we formulate the KK equivalence
theorems for the warped 5d Yang-Mills gauge theory and
the warped 5d GR theory.

2.2
Formulating GAET for Warped KK Gauge Theory

The warped bulk gauge theories in RS1 were discussed
in the literature
[
20
]
[
21
]
[
23
]
. We consider the Yang-Mills Lagrangian in warped 5d spacetime:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{YM}}\,=\,\sqrt{-\hat{g}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt\Big(\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!\frac{1}{\,4\,}\hat{g}^{MP}\hat{g}^{NQ}\hat{F}_{MN}^{a}\hat{F}_{PQ}^{a}\Big),

(6)

where the square root of the determinant of the warped
5d background metric tensor
\hat{g}_{MN}
is given by

\sqrt{-\hat{g}\,}=\exp[5A(z)]\hskip 0.85358pt
. The 5d field strength tensor
\hat{F}_{MN}^{a}
takes the form:

\hat{F}_{MN}^{a}=\partial_{M}\hat{A}_{N}^{a}-\partial_{N}\hat{A}_{M}^{a}+g_{5}f^{abc}\hat{A}_{M}^{b}\hat{A}_{N}^{c}\,,

(7)

where
(a,b,c)
denote the gauge-group indices,
f^{abc}
denotes
the gauge-group structure constant, and
g_{5}
is the 5d gauge coupling constant having mass-dimension
-\frac{1}{\,2\,}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

We expand the 5d Lagrangian (
6
) to the quadratic order
as presented in Eq.(
265
) of Appendix
B.1
. By inspecting Eq.(
265
),
we can remove the mixing term between the 5d components
\hat{A}_{\mu}^{a}
and
\hat{A}_{5}^{a}

by imposing the following
R_{\xi}
gauge-fixing term:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{GF}}=-\frac{~e^{A(z)}\hskip 0.85358pt}{2\hskip 0.85358pt\xi}\big(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{a}\big)^{\!2},~~~~~\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{a}=\partial^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{A}_{\mu}^{a}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\xi(A^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\partial_{z})\hat{A}_{5}^{a}\,,

(8)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\xi\hskip 0.85358pt
is the gauge-fixing parameter and

A^{\prime}\!\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\partial_{z}A(z)\hskip 0.85358pt
. We note that the gauge-fixing term (
8
)
contains an
\hat{A}_{\mu}^{a}
-
\hat{A}_{5}^{a}
mixing term which precisely cancels
the corresponding mixing term in the original Lagrangian
(
265
) upon integration by part. The fifth component
\hat{A}_{5}^{a}
will be identified as the would-be Goldstone boson
via a geometric “Higgs” mechanism of the KK
compactification
[
12
]
[
14
]
.
Using Eqs.(
265
) and (
8
), we derive the 5d
equations of motion (EOM) for the free fields
(\hat{A}_{\mu}^{a},\hat{A}_{5}^{a}
)
as in Eqs.(
267a
)-(
267b
).

Next, we compactify the warped 5d space under
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

orbifold, which corresponds to compactifying the fifth-dimension to
a line segment
\hskip 0.85358pty\!\in\![0,\bar{L}]\hskip 0.85358pt
or

\hskip 0.85358ptz\!\in\![0,L]\hskip 0.85358pt
,
with
\bar{L}\!=\!\pi\hskip 0.85358ptr_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{c}}
and
{L}\equiv(e^{\hskip 0.85358ptk\bar{L}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!1)\hskip-0.85358pt/k\,
,
where
r_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{c}}
denotes the compactification radius. We impose the gauge-covariant boundary conditions
on the 5d gauge field strength,

\hat{F}_{M5}^{a}\!=\!\hat{F}_{5M}^{a}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt0\,
at
z=0,L\hskip 0.85358pt
. This leads to the Neumann and Dirichlet boundary conditions on
the 5d gauge field components

\hat{A}_{\mu}^{a}
and
\hat{A}_{5}^{a}
as follows:

\partial_{z}\hat{A}^{a}_{\mu}({x},z)\Big|_{z=0,L}\!=\hskip 0.85358pt0\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\hat{A}^{a}_{5}({x},z)\Big|_{z=0,L}\!=\hskip 0.85358pt0\,.

(9)

Then, we can make KK expansions for the 5d gauge fields

\hat{A}_{\mu}^{a}
and
\hat{A}_{5}^{a}

in terms of the eigenfunctions of warped 5d space:

\displaystyle\hat{A}^{a\mu}(x,z)

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}A^{a\mu}_{n}({x})\,\mathsf{f}_{n}(z)\,,

(10a)

\displaystyle\hat{A}^{a5}(x,z)

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}A^{a5}_{n}({x})\,\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n}(z)\,.

(10b)

Substituting the KK expansions (
10
) into Eq.(
9
),
we obtain the boundary conditions imposed on
(\mathsf{f}_{n},\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n})
:

\partial_{z}\mathsf{f}_{n}(z)\big|_{z=0,L}=0\,,\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n}(z)\big|_{z=0,L}=0\,.

(11)

The 5d wavefunctions
(\mathsf{f}_{n},\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n})

also obey the orthonormal conditions shown in Eq.(
272
).

Under the KK expansions for the 5d gauge fields (
10
),
the 4d effective KK Lagrangian is derived by integrating over
the fifth coordinate
z
on the interval
[\hskip 0.85358pt0,L\hskip 0.85358pt]
. The quadratic KK Yang-Mills Lagrangian is given by

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{(2)}=\!\int_{0}^{L}\!\!\hskip-0.85358pt\text{d}z\,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{(2)}=-\frac{1}{\,4\,}\!\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\!\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{an}\!-\!\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{an}\!\right)^{\!2}\!-\!\frac{1}{\,2\,}\!\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\!\left(\!\partial_{\mu}A_{5}^{an}\!+\!M_{n}A_{\mu}^{an}\!\right)^{\!2}.

(12)

Then, using Eq.(
8
) and making the KK expansions (
10
),
we can derive the following
R_{\xi}
gauge-fixing term for the KK Lagrangian:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{GF}}

\displaystyle\,=\,\int_{0}^{L}\!\!\text{d}z\,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{GF}}\,=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{-1}{\,2\hskip 0.85358pt\xi_{n}}(\mathcal{F}^{a}_{n})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(13a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{a}_{n}

\displaystyle\,=\,\partial_{\mu}A^{a\mu}_{n}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\xi_{n}M_{n}A_{n}^{a5}=\mathbf{K}_{n}^{T}\mathbf{A}_{n}^{a}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(13b)

in agreement with Refs.
[
12
]
[
14
]
, where

\,\mathbf{K}_{n}\!=\!(\partial_{\mu},\hskip 0.85358pt\xi_{n}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n})^{T}
and

\mathbf{A}_{n}^{a}\!=\!(A_{n}^{a\mu},\hskip 0.85358ptA_{n}^{a5})^{T}
. In the above gauge-fixing term,
we introduce a gauge-fixing parameter
\xi_{n}
for each KK level-
n\hskip 0.85358pt

and use
M_{n}
to denote the KK gauge boson mass of level-
n
. By substituting the KK expansions (
10
) into the 5d EOM
(
267
) and using the 4d EOM (
268
) for free KK gauge fields,
we can derive the EOMs for the 5d eigenfunctions as in Eqs.(
269a
)-(
269b
). The KK gauge boson mass
M_{n}
is determined as in Eq.(
275
) by solving the roots
of the eigenvalue equations (
269a
)-(
269b
).

Then, corresponding to the 5d gauge-fixing term (
8
), we have the
following 5d Faddeev-Popov ghost term:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{FP}}=e^{A(z)}\hat{\bar{c}}^{a}\widehat{\tt s}\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{a}\,,

(14)

where
\widehat{\tt s}
is the Becchi-Rouet-Stora-Tyutin (BRST) operator
[
41
]

and the 5d BRST transformations take the form:

\widehat{\tt s}\hat{A}^{aN}\!=D_{b}^{aN}\!(\hat{A})\hat{c}^{b},~~~~~\widehat{\tt s}\hat{c}^{a}\!=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\hat{g}_{5}f^{abc}\hat{c}^{b}\hat{c}^{c},~~~~~\widehat{\tt s}\hat{\bar{c}}^{a}\!=-\xi^{-1}\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{a}.

(15)

Accordingly, we make KK expansions for 5d ghost and anti-ghost fields as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{a}(x,z)

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c^{a}_{n}(x)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{f}_{n}(z)\,,

(16a)

\displaystyle\hat{\bar{c}}^{a}(x,z)

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\bar{c}^{a}_{n}(x)\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n}(z)\,.

(16b)

With these, we derive the BRST transformations for the KK ghost fields as in Eqs.(
280
)-(
281
) of Appendix
B.1
.

Using these, we can further derive a BRST identity for the propagator of the
gauge fields
\mathbf{A}_{n}^{a}\!=\!(A_{n}^{a\mu},\hskip 0.85358ptA_{n}^{a5})^{T}
as follows:

\mathbf{K}_{n}^{T}\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{nm}^{ab}(k)=-{\mathbf{X}}_{nm}^{ab}(k)^{T}\,,

(17)

where we introduce the notations,

\displaystyle\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{nm}^{ab}(k)

\displaystyle=\big\langle 0|T\mathbf{A}_{n}^{a}{\mathbf{A}_{m}^{b}}^{T}|0\big\rangle\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~\mathcal{S}_{nm}(k)\delta^{ab}=\big\langle 0|Tc_{n}^{a}{\bar{c}_{m}^{b}}|0\big\rangle\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{X}}_{nm}^{ab}(k)

\displaystyle=\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{mj}^{ab}(k)\mathcal{S}_{jn}(k)\,,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{mj}^{ab}(k)

\displaystyle=\big\langle 0|T\widehat{\tt s}\mathbf{A}_{m}^{b}|\bar{c}_{j}^{a}\big\rangle=\!\left(\!\begin{matrix}\big\langle 0|T\widehat{\tt s}A_{m}^{b\mu}|\bar{c}_{j}^{a}\big\rangle\\[2.84526pt]
\big\langle 0|T\widehat{\tt s}A_{m}^{b5}|\bar{c}_{j}^{a}\big\rangle\end{matrix}\!\right)\equiv\!\left(\!\begin{matrix}-\text{i}k^{\mu}\delta^{ab}[\delta_{jm}\!+\!\Delta^{a}_{jm}(k^{2})]\\[2.84526pt]
-M_{m}\delta^{ab}[\delta_{jm}\!+\!\tilde{\Delta}^{a}_{jm}(k^{2})]\end{matrix}\right)\!,

(18)

with
\Delta^{a}_{jm}(k^{2})
and
\tilde{\Delta}^{a}_{jm}(k^{2})

being loop-level quantities.

In the following, we derive the
KK gauge boson equivalence theorem (KK GAET)
for the compactified warped 5d Yang-Mills gauge theory. The KK GAET was discussed
[
14
]

for the compactified 5d Yang-Mills gauge theories with a general metric
in connection to its deconstruction formulation,
as an extension of the formulations in the flat
5d KK gauge theories
[
12
]
[
13
]
. Our current derivation differs from
[
14
]

since we focus on the warped RS1 and choose the conformal coordinate frame of
(x^{\mu},z)

instead of the physical coordinate frame of
(x^{\mu},y)
. Besides, we will further study explicitly the three-point and four-point KK gauge (Goldstone) boson
scattering amplitudes in Section
3
and their double-copy in Section
4
.

Due to the 5d gauge invariance, we can derive a Slavnov-Taylor (ST) type of identity:

\big\langle 0|T\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{1}^{~}}(\hat{x}_{1})\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{2}}(\hat{x}_{2})\cdots\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{\!N}}(\hat{x}_{\!N}){\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}}|0\big\rangle\hskip 0.85358pt=\,0\,,

(19)

where
\hat{\tilde{\Phi}}
denotes any possible external physical states in 5d spacetime. This is analogous to the ST identity (with external lines being gauge-fixing functions)
in the 4d SM having spontaneous electroweak gauge symmetry breaking
[
42
]
. Then, after making the KK expansion, we obtain the following ST identities for
the external KK gauge-fixing functions:

\big\langle 0|T\mathcal{F}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{1}}_{n_{1}}(x_{1})\mathcal{F}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{2}}_{n_{2}}(x_{2})\cdots\mathcal{F}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{\!N}}_{n_{\!N}}(x_{\!N}){\tilde{\Phi}}|0\big\rangle\,=\,0\,.

(20)

Using the BRST identity (
17
),
we can amputate each external
\mathcal{F}^{an}
function
in the momentum space and obtain
a corresponding identity for the
S
-matrix amplitude:

\mathcal{T}[\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{1}}_{n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{1}),\hskip 0.85358pt\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{2}}_{n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{2}),\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\!,\hskip 0.85358pt\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{\!N}}_{n_{\!N}}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{N}),{\Phi}]\,=\,0\,,

(21)

where each external momentum
k_{j}
(
j\!=\!1,2,\cdots
)
is set on-shell with
k_{j}^{2}\!=\!\!-M_{n_{j}}^{2}
,
each
\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{j}}_{n_{j}}\!(k_{j})
represents
the amputated external gauge-fixing function, and
{\Phi}

denotes any possible physical states
after the LSZ (Lehmann-Symanzik-Zimmermann) amputation. We can express the amputated gauge-fixing function as follows:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt

\displaystyle=k_{\mu}A^{a\mu}_{n}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}\hat{C}^{a}_{nm}A_{m}^{a5}=M_{n}(A^{an}_{S}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{C}^{a}_{nm}A_{5}^{am})=M_{n}(A_{L}^{an}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt{\mathbb{Q}}^{a}_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(22a)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta}_{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{C}^{a}_{nm}A_{5}^{am}\!+v_{n}^{a}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~A_{S}^{an}\hskip-0.85358pt=\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}A_{\mu}^{an},~~~A_{L}^{an}\hskip-0.85358pt=\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}A_{\mu}^{an},~~~v^{a}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt=v_{n}^{\mu}A_{\mu}^{an},

(22b)

with notations defined as

\displaystyle\hat{C}^{a}_{nm}

\displaystyle=\text{i}(M_{m}/M_{n})\big[(\mathbf{1}\!+\!\bm{\tilde{\Delta}}^{\!a}(k^{2}))(\mathbf{1}\!+\!\bm{\Delta}^{\!a}(k^{2}))^{-1}\big]_{mn}=\text{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{nm}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptO(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(23a)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=k^{\mu}\!/M_{n}\!=\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\!-v_{n}^{\mu}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~v_{n}^{\mu}\!=O(M_{n}/E_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(23b)

where the matrix form is used such that

\big(\bm{\tilde{\Delta}}^{\!a}\big)_{\!jj^{\prime}}\!\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{\Delta}^{\!a}_{jj^{\prime}}

and

\big(\bm{\Delta}^{\!a}\big)_{\!jj^{\prime}}\!\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt\Delta^{\!a}_{jj^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85358pt
. In the above,
\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}
and
\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}
denote the KK gauge boson’s
longitudinal polarization vector and scalar polarization vector
respectively. Thus, from the identity (
21
) we deduce
the KK gauge equivalence theorem (GAET) identity:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\hskip 0.85358ptA_{L\hskip 0.85358ptn_{1}}^{a_{1}},\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\!,\hskip 0.85358ptA_{L\hskip 0.85358ptn_{\!N}}^{a_{N}}\!,{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt]=\mathcal{T}[\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathbb{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{1}}_{n_{1}},\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\!,\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathbb{Q}}_{n_{\!N}}^{\hskip 0.85358pta_{N}}\!,{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt]

(24a)

\displaystyle\hskip 102.43008pt=\hat{C}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\mathcal{T}[\,A_{5}^{a_{1}m_{1}}\!,\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\!,\hskip 0.85358ptA_{5}^{a_{N}m_{N}}\!,{\Phi}\,]+\mathcal{T}_{v}\,,

(24b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{v}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\tilde{C}_{\!\mathrm{mod},\ell}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}[\,v^{a_{1}n_{1}},\cdots\!,v^{a_{\ell}n_{\ell}},A_{5}^{a_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}},\cdots\!,A_{5}^{a_{N}m_{N}},{\Phi}\,]\,,

(24c)

where the multiplicative modification factors are given by

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}

\displaystyle=\hat{C}^{a_{1}}_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\cdots\hat{C}^{a_{N}}_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}=\,\text{i}^{N}\delta_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}\!+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(25a)

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{\!\mathrm{mod},\ell}^{n_{j}m_{j}}

\displaystyle=\hat{C}^{a_{\ell+1}}_{n_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}}\!\cdots\hat{C}^{a_{N}}_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}=\,\text{i}^{N-\ell}\delta_{n_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}\!+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(25b)

Such radiative modification factors also exist in the ET formulation
for the 4d electroweak gauge theory of the SM
[
42
]
-
[
44
]
. Note that the residual term
\mathcal{T}_{v}
in the KK-ET identity is suppressed
under high energy expansion due to

v_{n}^{\mu}\!=O(M_{n}/E_{n})
. With these, we derive the KK Equivalence Theorem for warped gauge theory
(KK GAET) for the high energy scattering:

\mathcal{T}[\,A_{L}^{a_{1}n_{1}},\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\!,\hskip 0.85358ptA_{L}^{a_{N}n_{N}}\!,{\Phi}\,]\hskip 0.85358pt=\,\hat{C}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\mathcal{T}[\,A_{5}^{a_{1}m_{1}}\!,\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\!,\hskip 0.85358ptA_{5}^{a_{N}m_{N}}\!,{\Phi}\,]+O(M_{n}/E_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(26)

where
\Phi
denotes any other external physical state(s)
after LSZ amputation, and at tree-level
the multiplicative factor
\hat{C}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}

just reduces to a simple factor

(\text{i}^{N}\delta_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}})\hskip 0.85358pt
.

2.3
Formulating GRET for Warped KK Gravity Theory

In this subsection, we first present the BRST quantization
for the warped KK gravity theory in Section
2.3.1
. Then, we formulate the warped Gravitational Equivalence Theorem (GRET)
for the KK gravitons with helicity
\pm 1
(called GRET of type-II) in Section
2.3.2

and for the longitudinal KK gravitons with helicity-0 (called GRET of type-I)
in Section
2.3.3
, respectively. Using the GRET and our generalized energy-power counting method
[
16
]
,
we further prove the different large energy cancellations for the
scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons with helicity-0 and with
helicity
\pm 1
around the end of this subsection.

2.3.1
Warped KK Gravity Theory with BRST Quantization

In this subsection, we consider the 5d RS1 with the fifth spatial dimension
compactified on an interval
[0,\hskip 0.85358pt{L}]
. The setup of RS1 contains warped 5d Einstein-Hilbert action and is
compactified on a 5d interval
[0,\hskip 0.85358pt{L}]
with the two 3-branes
located at the boundaries. The relevant part of the warped 5d Lagrangian can be written as

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{RS}}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{-\hat{g}\,}\hat{M}_{\mathrm{P}}^{3}\big(\hat{R}-2\Lambda\big)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(27)

where
\hat{R}
and
\Lambda\,(<0)
are the 5d Ricci scalar curvature and cosmological constant respectively. The 5d Planck mass
\hat{M}_{\mathrm{P}}
is given by

\hat{M}_{\mathrm{P}}\!=\!M_{\mathrm{P}}^{\frac{2}{3}}\bar{L}^{-\frac{1}{3}}
,
where
M_{\mathrm{P}}
denotes the 4d reduced Planck mass
and is connected to the 4d Newton constant
G
via

M_{\mathrm{P}}\!=\!(8\hskip 0.85358pt\pi\hskip 0.85358ptG)^{-1/2}
. The possible brane terms
[
19
]
are irrelevant for the current analysis.

To study the linearized theory, we apply the following weak field expansion
for the 5d Lagrangian (
27
):

\hat{g}_{MN}\,=\,e^{2A(z)}\big(\hat{\eta}_{MN}+\hat{\kappa}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{h}_{MN}\big)\,,

(28)

where the 5d gravitational coupling
\hat{\kappa}
has mass-dimension
-3/2

and is connected to the 4d gravitational coupling
{\kappa}
via

\,\hat{\kappa}=\kappa\sqrt{L\,}\!=2M_{\mathrm{P}}^{-1}\hskip-1.42262pt\sqrt{L\,}\,
. (Here
M_{\mathrm{P}}
is the 4d reduced Planck mass.)
The 5d graviton field
\hat{h}_{MN}
can be further parametrized
as follows
[
16
]
[
17
]
:

\hat{h}_{MN}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\!-\!\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}\hat{\phi}&~~\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}\\[5.69054pt]
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\nu}&~~\hat{\phi}\end{pmatrix}\!,

(29)

where we denote the (off-diagonal) vector component as

\,\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{h}_{\mu 5}\hskip 0.85358pt
and
the (diagonal) scalar component as
\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\phi}\equiv\hat{h}_{55}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Then, we derive the 5d Lagrangian at quadratic order, which is presented
in Eq.(
282
) of Appendix
B.2
. To eliminate the mixing terms appearing in Eq.(
282
),
we choose the 5d gauge-fixing terms as follows:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{GF}}=-\frac{\,e^{3A(z)}\,}{\xi}\big(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu}^{2}+\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{5}^{2}\,\big)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(30)

where
\,\xi\,
is a gauge-fixing parameter and the gauge-fixing functions

\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu}
and
\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{5}
take the following form:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\nu}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\!-\!\left(\!1\!-\!\frac{1}{\,2\xi\,}\right)\!\partial_{\mu}\hat{h}+\xi(3A^{\prime}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\partial_{z})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}\,,

(31a)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{5}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,2\,}\!\left[\partial_{z}\hat{h}-3\hskip 0.85358pt\xi(2A^{\prime}\!+\partial_{z})\hat{\phi}-\hskip-0.85358pt2\partial_{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu}\right]\!.

(31b)

In the above, we denote the gravitational gauge-fixing parameter
by
\,\xi\,
for notational convenience,
but it is independent of the gauge-fixing parameter
\,\xi\,

for gauge theories as defined in Eq.(
8
).

For the BRST quantization, the 5d gravitational ghost fields

(\hat{c}^{M}\!,\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\bar{c}}^{M})
are introduced to compensate
the gravitational gauge-fixing term (
30
)
in the path integral formulation. The 5d Faddeev-Popov Lagrangian takes the following form:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{FP}}\,=\,e^{3A(z)}\,\hat{\bar{c}}^{M}\widehat{\tt s}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{M}\,,

(32)

where the gauge-fixing functions,

\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu},\,\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{5})
, are defined as in Eq.(
31
),
with the 5d Lorentz index
M\!=\!\mu,5\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Under the
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold compactification,
the tensor component of graviton field
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

and the scalar component
\hat{\phi}
are assigned to be even under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parity,
whereas the vector component
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
odd:

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}(x,z)=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}(x,-z)\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}(x,z)=-\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}(x,-z)\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\hat{\phi}(x,z)=\hat{\phi}(x,-z)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(33)

We find that the ghost fields
(\hat{c}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu},\hat{c}^{\hskip 0.85358pt5})

obey the same equations of motion as that of the gravitational fields

(\hat{h}^{\mu\nu},\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu})
respectively. Thus, we assign the following
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parities for the ghost fields:

\hat{c}^{\mu}(x,z)=\hat{c}^{\mu}(x,-z)\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\hat{c}^{5}(x,z)=-\hat{c}^{5}(x,-z)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(34)

and their corresponding anti-ghost fields share the same
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parities. With these, we make the following KK expansions for
the 5d graviton fields in terms of Fourier series containing the zero-modes and KK-modes:

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}h^{\mu\nu}_{n}(x)\,\mathsf{u}_{n}(z)\,,

(35a)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{n}(x)\,\mathsf{v}_{n}(z)\,,

(35b)

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\phi_{n}(x)\,\mathsf{w}_{n}(z)\,,

(35c)

where the 5d wavefunctions

\big\{\mathsf{u}_{n}(z),\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{v}_{n}(z),\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{w}_{n}(z)\big\}

obey the orthonormal conditions (
288
). These 5d wavefunctions also satisfy the following boundary conditions,

\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{u}_{n}(z)\right|_{z=0,L}=0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\left.\mathsf{v}_{n}(z)\right|_{z=0,L}=0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\left.(2A^{\prime}\!+\partial_{z})\mathsf{w}_{n}(z)\right|_{z=0,L}=0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(36)

which can be derived by imposing the orbifold symmetry and integrating around the fixed points
to obtain the junction conditions
[
45
]
,
or by introducing the Gibbons-Hawking extrinsic curvature term
[
46
]
and
applying the variational principle
[
47
]
[
48
]
.

Making KK expansions for the 5d Lagrangian (
282
)
and integrating
z
over the interval
[\hskip 0.85358pt0,\hskip 0.85358ptL\hskip 0.85358pt]
,
we derive the 4d KK Lagrangian to the quadratic order
as given in Eq.(
296
). Thus, we can derive the following 4d
R_{\xi}
-type gauge-fixing terms under the KK expansions:

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{GF}}=\int_{0}^{L}\!\!\text{d}z\,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{GF}}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\!\frac{1}{\,\xi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt}\!\left[(\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu})^{2}\!+\!(\mathcal{F}_{n}^{5})^{2}\right]\!,

(37)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\xi_{n}
is the gauge-fixing parameter for KK level-
n\hskip 0.85358pt
, and
the gauge-fixing functions
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}
and
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{5}
take the following forms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\,=\,\partial_{\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\!-\!\left(\!1\!-\!{\frac{1}{\,2\hskip 0.85358pt\xi_{n}}\,}\!\right)\!\partial^{\mu}h_{n}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\xi_{n}\mathbb{M}_{n}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{n}\,,

(38a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n}^{5}

\displaystyle\,=\hskip 0.85358pt{\frac{1}{\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt}}\,\!\mathbb{M}_{n}h_{n}\!-\!{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}}\,\xi_{n}\mathbb{M}_{n}\phi_{n}\!+\!{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}}\,\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{n}\,,

(38b)

where
\mathbb{M}_{n}
denotes the mass eigenvalue for the KK gravitons of level-
n
. In the above, to ensure proper normalization of the kinetic terms of
\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu}
and
\phi_{n}
,
we have made the field rescaling,

\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{n}
and

\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\,}\phi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt

as defined in Eq.(
299
). With these, we can derive the propagators of KK fields
(h_{n}^{\mu\nu},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu},\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n})

at tree level, which take simple diagonal forms
as shown in Eqs.(
301a
)-(
301c
). Using the KK decompositions (
35
),
we derive the equations of motion (EOM)
for the 5d gravitational KK wavefunctions as in Eq.(
287
). The KK mass eigenvalue
\mathbb{M}_{n}
can be determined by solving Eq.(
294
),
and differs from the KK gauge boson mass
M_{n}
solved from Eq.(
275
).

As shown in Eqs.(
33
) and (
34
),
the 5d ghost fields
(\hat{c}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu},\hat{c}^{\hskip 0.85358pt5})

share the same
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parities as that of the gravitational fields

(\hat{h}^{\mu\nu},\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu})
respectively. Thus, we make the following KK expansions for the gravitational
ghost and anti-ghost fields:

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{\mu}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\!\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\!c^{\mu}_{n}(x)\mathsf{u}_{n}(z)\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\hat{\bar{c}}^{\mu}(x,z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\!\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\!\bar{c}_{n}^{\mu}(x)\mathsf{u}_{n}(z)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(39a)

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{5}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\!\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\!c_{n}^{5}(x)\mathsf{v}_{n}(z)\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\hat{\bar{c}}^{5}(x,z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\,}\,}\!\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\!\bar{c}_{n}^{5}(x)\mathsf{v}_{n}(z)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(39b)

We have further derived the BRST transformations for the KK fields,
which are presented in Eq.(
303
) of Appendix
B
.

Then, we derive a general (Slavnov-Taylor-type) identity in the momentum space
for the gravitational gauge-fixing functions (
38a
)-(
38b
)
as external lines of the Green function
[
16
]
[
17
]
:

\big\langle 0\hskip 0.85358pt|\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{F}_{n_{1}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{F}_{n_{2}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{2})\cdots\mathcal{F}_{m_{1}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt5}\!(p_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{F}_{m_{2}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt5}\!(p_{2})\cdots\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt|\hskip 0.85358pt0\big\rangle=\,0\,,

(40)

where each external momentum (
k_{j}
or
p_{j}
) is chosen as incoming and

\Phi
denotes any possible external physical states
after the LSZ reduction.

2.3.2
Formulating GRET for Warped KK Gravity Theory (I)

In this subection, we first formulate a KK Gravitational Equivalence Theorem (GRET of type-II)
3
3

3

For convenience, we call this GRET (for KK gravitons with helicity
\pm 1
) the GRET of type-II, and
call the other GRET (for KK gravitons with helicity-0) the GRET of type-I
which will be given in Section
2.3.3
.

to quantitatively connect the scattering amplitude of KK gravitons
h^{\mu\nu}_{n}
with helicity
\pm 1

to that of the corresponding gravitational KK vector Goldstone bosons
\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu}
. For this purpose, we have derived the general identity (
40
)
for the gravitational gauge-fixing functions

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}
and
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt5}
of Eq.(
38
).

We first reexpress the gauge-fixing function (
38a
) into the following matrix form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,\text{i}\mathbb{M}_{n}\,}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\mathbb{F}_{n}^{\mu}\,,\quad\mathbb{F}_{n}^{\mu}=\mathbf{K}_{n}^{T}\mathbf{H}_{n}\,,

(41a)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt

\displaystyle=\hskip-0.85358pt\Big(\!\frac{1}{\,\sqrt{2\,}\mathbb{M}_{n}}\!\big[2k_{\beta}\mathchoice{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\!-k^{\mu}(2\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\xi_{n}^{-1})\eta_{\alpha\beta}\big],\,\text{i}\xi_{n}\Big)^{\!T},\quad\mathbf{H}_{n}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\big(h_{n}^{\alpha\beta},\,\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu}\big)^{\!T}.

(41b)

Then, we derive the following BRST identity including the gauge-fixing function

\mathbb{F}_{n}^{\mu}\hskip 0.85358pt
,

\big\langle 0\big|T\,\mathbb{F}_{n}^{\mu}\mathbf{H}_{m}^{T}\big|0\big\rangle=-\frac{\text{i}\xi_{n}}{\sqrt{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n}}\big\langle 0\big|T\,\widehat{\tt s}\mathbf{H}_{m}^{T}\bar{c}^{\mu}_{n}\big|0\big\rangle\,.

(42)

Using Eq.(
41
), we can rewrite Eq.(
42
) as follows:

\mathbf{K}_{n}^{T}\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{nm}(k)=-\mathbf{X}^{T}_{nm}(k)\,,

(43)

where we have defined full propagator in the matrix form

\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{nm}(k)\!=\!\big\langle 0\big|T\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{m}^{T}\big|0\big\rangle\hskip-0.85358pt(k)
,
and the matrix quantity
\mathbf{X}_{nm}
is given in
(
323
) of Appendix
B.4
.

Then, we analyze the identity (
40
) and construct an external state
as a combination of gauge-fixing functions
(
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}
or
\mathbb{F}_{n}^{\mu}
) as given in Eq.(
41
). Thus, we amputate this external state by using the BRST identity (
43
)
and obtain:

\displaystyle 0\,=\,\big\langle 0|\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{F}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}(k)\cdots{{\Phi}}\hskip 0.85358pt|0\big\rangle=-\mathbf{X}_{nm}^{T}(k)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{m}(k),\cdots\!,{{\Phi}}\hskip 0.85358pt\big].

(44)

From the above, we can derive the following identity:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt(k),\cdots\!,{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]=0\,,

(45)

where
\mathcal{M}[\cdots]
denotes the amputated scattering amplitude. In Eq.(
45
), the amputated external state
\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}

takes the following form (after removing an irrelevant overall coefficient):

\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}(k)=\frac{\sqrt{2\,}\,}{\,\mathbb{M}_{n}}\hskip 0.85358ptk_{\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\!-\hat{C}^{\hskip 0.85358ptnm}\hskip 0.85358pt{\eta}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu\nu}\mathcal{V}_{m,\nu}\,,

(46)

where the loop-induced modification factor is derived as follows:

\hat{C}_{\hskip 0.85358ptnm}(k^{2})=-\frac{\,\text{i}\mathbb{M}_{m}\,}{\mathbb{M}_{n}}\!\!\left[\!\frac{\,{\mathbf{1}}\!+\!\tilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\!(2)}(k^{2})\,}{\,{\mathbf{1}}\!+\!\bm{\Delta}^{\!(1)}(k^{2})\,}\!\right]_{mn}\!=-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{nm}+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(47)

In the above, the matrix form is used such that the loop-level quantities

(\bm{\Delta}^{\!(1)})_{jj^{\prime}}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt{\Delta}^{\!(1)}_{jj^{\prime}}
and

(\bm{\tilde{\Delta}}^{\!(2)})_{jj^{\prime}}\!=\!\tilde{\Delta}^{\!(2)}_{jj^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
where the loop quantities
({\Delta}^{\!(1)}_{jj^{\prime}},\,\tilde{\Delta}^{\!(2)}_{jj^{\prime}})

are defined in Eq.(
324
). The derivations of Eqs.(
44
)-(
47
) are given
in detail around
Eqs.(
325
)-(
326
) of Appendix
B
.

Then, we utilize a transverse polarization vector
\epsilon^{\mu}_{\pm}
to contract with

\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}(k)
in Eq.(
46
) and obtain the formula:

\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}(k)\equiv\epsilon_{\mu}^{\pm}\hskip 0.85358pt\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}(k)=h_{n}^{S,\pm 1}-\hat{C}_{\hskip 0.85358ptnm}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{m}\,,

(48)

where we have used the fact
\hskip 0.85358pt\epsilon^{\mu}_{\pm}\hskip 0.85358ptk_{\mu}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and defined the external states
h^{S,\pm 1}_{n}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{m}
as follows:

h_{n}^{S,\pm 1}\!=\varepsilon^{S,\pm 1}_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{m}\!=\epsilon_{\mu}^{\pm}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}_{m}^{\mu}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\varepsilon^{S,\pm 1}_{\mu\nu}\!=\!{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}}\hskip-0.85358pt\big(\epsilon^{S}_{\mu}\epsilon_{\nu}^{\pm}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon^{S}_{\nu}\epsilon_{\mu}^{\pm}\big),

(49)

with
\epsilon^{S}_{\mu}\!\!=\!\!k_{\mu}/\mathbb{M}_{n}
being the scalar-polarization vector. A massive spin-2 KK graviton has 5 physical helicity states
(\lambda\!=\!\pm 2,\pm 1,0)
. The polarization tensors of KK graviton with helicities
\pm 1
are given by

\varepsilon_{\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}={\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}}\!\left(\epsilon_{\pm}^{\mu}\epsilon_{L}^{\nu}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\pm}^{\nu}\right)\!,

(50)

where
(\epsilon_{\pm}^{\mu},\,\epsilon_{L}^{\mu})
denote the 3 polarization vectors of a massive spin-1 KK gauge boson. Under high energy expansion, we have
\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptv^{\mu}

with
\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\!=\!k^{\mu}\!/\mathbb{M}_{n}
and
v^{\mu}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptO(\mathbb{M}_{n}/E_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, we can decompose the polarization tensor
\varepsilon_{\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}
as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{S,\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}+v_{\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}\,,~~~~\varepsilon_{S,\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}=\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}$}\hskip-0.85358pt\big(\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\epsilon^{\nu}_{\pm}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{S}^{\nu}\epsilon^{\mu}_{\pm}\big),

(51a)

\displaystyle v_{\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}$}\hskip-0.85358pt\big(v^{\mu}\epsilon^{\nu}_{\pm}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptv^{\nu}\epsilon^{\mu}_{\pm}\big)\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358ptO(\mathbb{M}_{n}/\hskip-0.85358ptE)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(51b)

With these, we can reexpress the function
\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt(k)

of Eq.(
48
) in the form:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt(k)=h_{n}^{\pm 1}\!-\Theta_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\Theta_{n}\!=\hat{C}^{\hskip 0.85358ptnm}\mathcal{V}_{m}^{\pm 1}\!+v_{n}^{\pm 1}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(52)

where we have used the notations,

h_{n}^{\pm 1}\!=\varepsilon^{\pm 1}_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}_{n},\quad~\mathcal{V}_{m}^{\pm 1}\!=\epsilon^{\pm}_{\mu}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{m}\hskip 0.85358pt,\quad~v_{n}^{\pm 1}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptv_{\mu\nu}^{\pm 1}h^{\mu\nu}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(53)

with the tensor
v_{\pm 1}^{\mu\nu}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptO(\mathbb{M}_{n}/\hskip-0.85358ptE_{n})
under high energy expansion and
thus
v_{n}^{\pm 1}
is suppressed by an energy factor of
O(\mathbb{M}_{n}/\hskip-0.85358ptE_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt
. For later analysis, we can also multiply
\,v^{\mu}\!=\!\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\,

on both sides of Eq.(
45
) and use the expression (
46
) to
obtain the following identity:

\epsilon_{\mu}^{S}v_{\nu}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\hskip-0.85358pt(k),\cdots\!,{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]=\hat{C}_{nm}\hskip 0.85358ptv_{\mu}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\big[\mathcal{V}_{m}^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt(k),\cdots\!,{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big],

(54)

where we see that the right-hand side is suppressed by the external factor

v_{\mu}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptO(\mathbb{M}_{n}/\hskip-0.85358ptE_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt
. Hence the above identity (
54
) ensures that its left-hand side must receive a suppression
such that its overall energy-power dependence is the same as the right-hand side
[despite that the external factor of the left-hand side is

\epsilon_{\mu}^{S}v_{\nu}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptO(E^{0}_{n})
].

Then, we reformulate the identity (
45
) and extend it to the general case of
N
external amputated
gauge-fixing functions:

\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{1}),\cdots\!,\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n_{\!N}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{\!N}),{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]=0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(55)

Using this identity, we can further derive the gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET) identity
which connects the
h_{n}^{\pm 1}
amplitude to the amplitude of
\Theta_{n}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[h_{n_{1}}^{\pm 1}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{1}),\cdots\!,h_{n_{\!N}}^{\pm 1}\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{N}),{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\Theta_{n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{1}),\cdots\!,\Theta_{n_{\!N}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{N}),{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(56)

The above identity can be proven by computing the amplitude on its right-hand side and
using the relation
\Theta_{n}\!\!=\!h_{n}^{\pm 1}\!-\!\underline{\mathbb{F}}_{n}
in Eq.(
52
). Expanding
\Theta_{n}(=\!\hat{C}_{\hskip 0.85358ptnm}\mathcal{V}_{m}^{\pm 1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\!v_{n}^{\pm 1})

for each external state on the right-hand side of the identity (
56
),
we derive the following gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET) identity:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}[\,h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}},\cdots\!,h^{\pm 1}_{n_{N}},\Phi\,]\,=\,\hat{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\mathcal{M}[\,{\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{m_{1}},\cdots\!,\mathcal{V}_{m_{N}}^{\pm 1}},\Phi\,]+\mathcal{M}_{v}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(57a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{v}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\tilde{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod},\ell}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\,\mathcal{M}[\,v_{n_{1}}^{\pm 1},\cdots\!,v_{n_{\ell}}^{\pm 1},\mathcal{V}_{m_{\ell+1}}^{\pm 1},\cdots\!,\mathcal{V}_{m_{N}}^{\pm 1},\Phi\,]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(57b)

where the multiplicative modification factors are given by

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}

\displaystyle=\,\hat{C}_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\cdots\hat{C}_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}=\,(-\text{i})^{N}\delta_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}\!+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(58a)

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod},\ell}^{n_{j}m_{j}}

\displaystyle=\,\hat{C}_{n_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}}\!\cdots\hat{C}_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}=\,(-\text{i})^{N-\ell}\delta_{n_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}\!+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(58b)

Under the high energy expansion, the residual term is suppressed by the energy factor of

O(\mathbb{M}_{n}/\hskip-0.85358ptE)\hskip 0.85358pt
. Hence, from the identity (
57a
)
we derive the following warped GRET of type-II:

\mathcal{M}[\,h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}},\cdots\!,h^{\pm 1}_{n_{N}},\Phi\,]\,=\,\hat{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\mathcal{M}[\,{\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{m_{1}},\cdots\!,\mathcal{V}_{m_{N}}^{\pm 1}},\Phi\,]\hskip 0.85358pt+\hskip 0.85358ptO({\mathbb{M}_{n}}/\hskip-0.85358pt{E_{n}}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{-suppressed})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(59)

This connects the scattering amplitude of the KK gravitons (
h_{n}^{\pm 1}
) with helicity
\pm 1
to the
corresponding gravitational KK vector-Goldstone bosons (
\mathcal{V}_{m}^{\pm 1}
).

2.3.3
Formulating GRET for Warped KK Gravity Theory (II)

In this subsection, we further derive the KK GRET to connect the scattering amplitude of
helicity-0 longitudinal KK gravitons (
h_{L}^{n}
) to that of the corresponding gravitational KK
scalar Goldstone bosons (
\phi_{n}
) under high energy expansion. This GRET will be called GRET of type-I.

We use the combination of gauge-fixing functions

\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}\!-\xi_{n}\mathbb{M}_{n}\mathcal{F}_{n}^{5}\,

to eliminate the KK vector-Goldstone field
\,\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu}\,
and
derive an expression in momentum space:

-\text{i}k_{\mu}\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}\!-\xi_{n}\mathbb{M}_{n}\mathcal{F}_{n}^{5}\,=\,-k_{\mu}k_{\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\!+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\!\left[\!(2\!-{\xi_{n}^{-1}})k^{2}\!-\xi_{n}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}\right]\!h_{n}\!+\!\sqrt{\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}\,}{\xi_{n}^{2}}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}\phi_{n}\,,

(60)

where we have made the rescaling of
\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}

as in Eq.(
299
). Imposing the on-shell condition
k^{2}\!=\!-\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
we can rewrite the above formula as follows:

\displaystyle\text{i}k_{\mu}\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\mu}\!+\xi_{n}\mathbb{M}_{n}\mathcal{F}_{n}^{5}\,=\sqrt{\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{3}\,}{2}$}\,}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{n}\,,

(61a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{n}=\sqrt{\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{2}\,}{3}$}\,}h_{n}^{S}+\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}$}\big(2\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\xi_{n}\!-\xi_{n}^{-1}\big)h_{n}-\xi_{n}^{2}\phi_{n}=\mathbf{K}^{T}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}

(61b)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.66563pt=\sqrt{\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{2}\,}{3}$}\,}\big(h_{n}^{S}+h_{n}\big)\!-\phi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 19.91692pt(\text{for}~\xi_{n}\!=\!1)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(61c)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{n}\!=\Big(\!\sqrt{\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{2}\,}{3}$}\,}\varepsilon^{S}_{\mu\nu}\!+\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}$}\big(2\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\xi_{n}\!-\xi_{n}^{-1}\big)\eta_{\mu\nu},\,-\xi_{n}^{2}\Big)^{\!\!T}\hskip-0.85358pt\!,~~~~\mathbf{H}_{n}=(h_{n}^{\mu\nu},\,\phi_{n})^{T}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(61d)

where we have defined the scalar KK graviton

\,h_{n}^{S}\!=\!\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}^{S}{h_{n}^{\mu\nu}}\,
with its scalar-polarization tensor

\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}^{S}\!=\!k_{\mu}k_{\nu}/\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt
. Then, we inspect the KK graviton’s longitudinal polarization tensor
\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}

and make high energy expansion of it as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}\!\left(\epsilon_{+}^{\mu}\epsilon_{-}^{\nu}\!+\epsilon_{+}^{\nu}\epsilon_{-}^{\mu}\!+2\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\epsilon_{L}^{\nu}\right)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\,}\varepsilon_{S}^{\mu\nu}\!+\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu},

(62)

where the longitudinal polarization vector

\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\!=\!(k^{0}\!/\mathbb{M}_{n})(|\vec{k}|/k^{0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,\vec{k}/|\vec{k}|)\!=\!\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\!+\!v^{\mu}
with
\,\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}=k^{\mu}\!/\mathbb{M}_{n}\,

and
\,v^{\mu}\!=\!\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{M}_{n}/E_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt
. In the above, the residual term
\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}
is given by

\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\,\varepsilon_{L}^{\mu\nu}\!\!-\!\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\,}\varepsilon_{S}^{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}\!\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\big(\epsilon_{+}^{\mu}\epsilon_{-}^{\nu}\!+\epsilon_{+}^{\nu}\epsilon_{-}^{\mu}\big)\!+2\big(\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}v^{\nu}\!+\!\epsilon_{S}^{\nu}v^{\mu}\!+\!v^{\mu}v^{\nu}\big)\hskip-0.85358pt\right]\!,

(63)

where
\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\!=\!k^{\mu}/\mathbb{M}_{n}
and
\,\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}\!\!=\!\mathcal{O}(E_{n}^{0})

under high energy expansion.

According to Appendix
B.3
, we derive a BRST identity for the full propagator
of the matrix field
\mathbf{H}_{n}\!=\!(h_{n}^{\mu\nu},\,\phi_{n})^{T}
,

\mathbf{K}_{n}^{T}\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{nm}(k)=-\mathbf{X}_{nm}^{T}(k)\,,

(64)

where we have defined full propagator in the matrix form

\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{nm}(k)\!=\!\big\langle 0\big|T\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{m}^{T}\big|0\big\rangle\hskip-0.85358pt(k)
,
and the other matrix quantity
\mathbf{X}_{nm}
is given in
(
308
) of Appendix
B.3
. We also note that the content of Eq.(
64
) actually differs from that of
Eq.(
43
) although they are expressed in the same notations.

Using the identity (
40
) and Eq.(
61
) together with
Eq.(
64
), we can derive a new identity with
external state
\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{n}(k)\hskip 0.85358pt
,

\displaystyle 0\,=\,\big\langle 0\hskip 0.85358pt|\,\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{n}(k)\cdots\Phi\,|\hskip 0.85358pt0\big\rangle=-\mathbf{X}_{nm}^{T}(k)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{m}(k),\cdots\!,\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt\big],

(65)

from which we derive the following identity,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt(k),\cdots\!,\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt\big]=0\,,

(66)

where the amputated external state

\underline{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt(k)
is given as follows:

\displaystyle\underline{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{2}\,}{3}$}\,}(h_{n}^{S}\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}h_{n})\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptC_{nm}\phi_{m}=h_{n}^{L}-\Omega_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(67a)

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}

\displaystyle=C_{nm}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{m}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{\Delta}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\tilde{\Delta}_{n}=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}$}h_{n}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{v}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\tilde{v}_{n}\!=v_{\mu\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(67b)

In the above,
C_{nm}
is a multiplicative modification factor
induced at loop level:

C_{nm}(k^{2})=\left[\!\frac{~\mathbf{1}\!+\!\bm{\tilde{\Delta}}^{\!(4)}(k^{2})~}{\,\mathbf{1}\!+\!\bm{\Delta}^{\!(3)}(k^{2})}\!\right]_{mn}\!\equiv\,\delta_{nm}+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(68)

where the matrix form is used such that

(\bm{\Delta}^{\!(3)})_{jj^{\prime}}\!\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\!{\Delta}^{\!(3)}_{jj^{\prime}}
and

(\bm{\tilde{\Delta}}^{\!(4)})_{jj^{\prime}}\!\!=\!\!\tilde{\Delta}^{\!(4)}_{jj^{\prime}}

with the matrix elements
({\Delta}^{\!(3)}_{jj^{\prime}},\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\Delta}^{\!(4)}_{jj^{\prime}})

given by Eq.(
308
). More details of the derivations are given in Appendix
B.3
.

Then, using Eq.(
66
)
we can derive the following general identity for
the gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET):

\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}}_{n_{1}}\!(k_{1}),\cdots\!,\underline{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}}_{n_{\!N}}\!(k_{N}),{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]=0\,.

(69)

From this, we further derive the following GRET identity:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[h_{n_{1}}^{L}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{1}),\cdots\!,h_{n_{\!N}}^{L}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{N}),{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\Omega_{n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{1}),\cdots\!,\Omega_{n_{\!N}}\hskip-0.85358pt\!(k_{N}),{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(70)

which can be proven by computing the amplitude on its right-hand side and using
the relation
\Omega_{n}\!=\!h_{n}^{L}\!-\underline{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}}_{n}

in Eq.(
67a
). Expanding the right-hand side of the GRET identity (
70
),
we connect the scattering amplitude of helicity-zero longitudinal KK gravitons (
h_{n}^{L}
)
to that of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons
(\phi_{m})\hskip 0.85358pt
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}[\hskip 0.85358pth^{L}_{n_{1}},\cdots\!,h^{L}_{n_{\!N}},\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt]\,=\,{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt[\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{m_{1}},\cdots\!,\phi_{m_{\!N}}\hskip-0.85358pt,\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt]+\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}\,,

(71a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}=\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\!\widetilde{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod},\ell}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\,\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\tilde{\Delta}_{n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt,\cdots\!,\tilde{\Delta}_{n_{\ell}},\phi_{m_{\ell+1}},\cdots\!,\phi_{m_{N}}\hskip-0.85358pt,\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt\big],

(71b)

where
\tilde{\Delta}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}h_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt\hskip-0.85358pt+\tilde{v}_{n}

with
\tilde{v}_{n}\!=\!v_{\mu\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}
, and

\Phi
denotes any other external on-shell physical state(s) after the LSZ reduction. In the above, the multiplicative modification factors are defined as follows:

\displaystyle C_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{\!j}m_{\!j}}

\displaystyle=\,C_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\!\cdots C_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}=\,\delta_{n_{1}m_{1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}\!+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(72a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod},\ell}^{n_{\!j}m_{\!j}}

\displaystyle=\,C_{n_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}}\!\!\cdots C_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}=\,\delta_{n_{\ell+1}m_{\ell+1}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\delta_{n_{\!N}m_{\!N}}\!+O(\mathrm{loop})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(72b)

which are renormalization-scheme-dependent and can be normalized to one
under proper setup of the renormalization scheme. They reduce to one at tree level. We can further justify that in the residual term (
71b
) each external state

\tilde{\Delta}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt(=\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}h_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{v}_{n})

is suppressed by an energy factor
\mathbb{M}_{n}/E_{n}
under high energy expansion. For this, using the expression (
63
) and the normalization condition

\sum_{\sigma}\epsilon_{\sigma}^{\mu}(\epsilon_{\sigma}^{\nu})^{*}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\eta^{\mu\nu}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk^{\mu}k^{\nu}/\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt

(with helicity
\sigma\!=\!\pm,L
), we derive the following:

\tilde{\Delta}_{n}=\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}$}h_{n}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{v}_{n}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\,}\big(2\epsilon^{S}_{\mu}v_{\nu}\!+\!v_{\mu}v_{\nu}\big)h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\,,

(73)

where
v_{\mu}v_{\nu}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\!O(\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}/\hskip-0.85358ptE_{n}^{2})
and
\epsilon^{S}_{\mu}v_{\nu}\!\!=\!\!O(E_{n}^{0})
. The external state of
(v_{\mu}v_{\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu})
is clearly suppressed
by the extra energy factor

v_{\mu}v_{\nu}\!\sim\!\mathbb{M}_{n}^{2}/E_{n}^{2}
in the high energy limit. As proven by Eq.(
54
), despite the external factor
\epsilon_{\mu}^{S}v_{\nu}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptO(E^{0}_{n})
,
the amplitude with external state

\epsilon^{S}_{\mu}v_{\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}
is actually suppressed by an overall energy factor

v^{\mu}\!=\!O(\mathbb{M}_{n}/E_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, as the external state, we can reexpress
\tilde{\Delta}_{n}
as follows:

\tilde{\Delta}_{n}=\,\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\,}\big(\hat{C}_{nm}2\hskip 0.85358ptv_{\!\mu}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{m}\!+\!v_{\!\mu}v_{\nu}h_{n}^{\mu\nu}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(74)

where the two terms on right-hand side is suppressed by external factors of

v_{\!\mu}\!\!=\!O(E^{-1})
and
v_{\!\mu}v_{\nu}\!\!=\!O(E^{-2})
, respectively. Hence, the residual term
\mathcal{M}_{\!\Delta}
in Eq.(
71b
) must be suppressed
(relative to the leading KK Goldstone amplitude) at least by an overall energy factor

v^{\mu}\!=\!O(\mathbb{M}_{n}/E_{n})\hskip 0.85358pt
under the high energy expansion. Thus, we can further formulate the following warped GRET of type-I:

\mathcal{M}[\hskip 0.85358pth^{L}_{n_{1}},\cdots\!,h^{L}_{n_{\!N}},\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt]\,=\,{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.85358pt[\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{m_{1}},\cdots\!,\phi_{m_{\!N}}\hskip-0.85358pt,\Phi\hskip 0.85358pt]+O({\mathbb{M}_{n}}/\hskip-0.85358pt{E_{n}}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{-suppressed})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(75)

This ensures that the GRET identity (
71a
) can establish
the equivalence between the helicity-zero longitudinal KK graviton amplitude

\mathcal{M}[h_{n_{1}}^{L},\cdots]
and the corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone amplitude

{C}_{\hskip-0.85358pt\mathrm{mod}}^{n_{j}m_{j}}\mathcal{M}[\phi_{m_{1}},\cdots]
in the high energy limit. Thus, the identity (
71
) gives in Eq.(
75
)
the KK gravitional equivalence theorem (GRET)
for the warped 5d GR theory under high energy expansion,
which extends our previous formulation of the KK GRET for the
compactified flat 5d GR
[
16
]
[
17
]
.

We note that the generalized power-counting method
for the compactified KK gauge theories and KK GR theories
as established in Ref.
[
16
]
does hold for both flat and warped extra dimensions. Thus, we can deduce the leading energy-power dependence of

N
-point helicity-zero longitudinal KK graviton amplitudes
and of the corresponding
N
-point KK Goldstone amplitudes:

\displaystyle D_{E}(Nh^{L}_{n})=2(N\!+\!L\!+\!1),\hskip 19.91692ptD_{E}(N\phi_{n})=2(L\!+\!1),

(76a)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow~~D_{E}(Nh^{L}_{n})\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptD_{E}(N\phi_{n})=2N,

(76b)

where
L\!\geqslant\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
denotes the number of loops involved. For the scattering amplitudes of
N
longitudinal KK gravitons
(with helicity-zero) at tree level (
L\!=\!0
),
we use the right-hand side of the GRET identity (
71a
)
and prove the large energy cancellations of
E^{2N+2}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{2}

by an energy factor
E^{2N}

in the
N
longitudinal KK graviton amplitude.

The case of 3-point KK graviton amplitudes needs a further discussion. According to the above power counting rule (
76a
), the tree-level
3-point longitudinal KK graviton amplitude (
3h_{n}^{L}
) have a possible leading energy dependence of

E^{2(N+1)}\!\!=\!E^{8}
for
N\!\!=\!3\,
. The tree-level amplitude of 3 gravitational
KK Goldstone bosons (
3\phi_{n}
) arises from trilinear KK Goldstone vertex
whose coupling factor contains products of two external momenta

\,p_{i}\cdot p_{j}
. So the naive power counting shows that this KK Goldstone amplitude
would have leading energy dependence of
E^{2}
in agreement with Eq.(
76a
). Thus, according to the GRET (
71
), the 3-point tree-level longitudinal KK graviton amplitude
should have a total energy-power deduction of
E^{8-2}\!=\!E^{6}
, which agrees to Eq.(
76b
). But, as we will further explain at the end of the following Section
3.1.2
,
the
N\!=\!3
is a special case where the momentum conservation and on-shell condition of each external state
plays additional roles to reduce the leading energy dependence. Namely, after imposing the momentum conservation at the trilinear vertex and the on-shell condition
for each momentum, the individual leading-energy dependence of 3-point longitudinal KK graviton amplitude
reduces to
E^{4}
and that of the corresponding KK Goldstone amplitude becomes
E^{0}
. Hence the GRET (
71
) further ensures a nontrivial energy cancellation of

E^{4}\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}
.

Then, we can further consider the scattering amplitudes of

N
KK gravitons of
h_{n}^{\pm 1}
(with helicity
\pm 1
) and of the
corresponding gravitational KK vector-Goldstone bosons (
\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\pm 1}
). Using the generalized power-counting method
[
16
]
,
we derive their leading energy-power dependence as follows:

\displaystyle D_{E}(Nh_{n}^{\pm 1})=N\!+\!2(L\!+\!1),\hskip 17.07164ptD_{E}(N\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\pm 1})=2(L\!+\!1),

(77a)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow~~D_{E}(Nh_{n}^{\pm 1})\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptD_{E}(N\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\pm 1})=N.

(77b)

For the scattering amplitudes of
N
KK gravitons (
h_{n}^{\pm 1}
) at tree level,
we use the right-hand side of the GRET identity (
57
)
[or (
59
)]
and prove the large energy cancellations of
E^{N+2}\!\!\rightarrow\!E^{2}

by an energy factor
E^{N}

in the scattering amplitude of
N
KK gravitons (with helicity
\pm 1
).

For the warped KK gauge theories as studied in Section
2.2
,
using the generalized power-counting method
[
16
]
and
the GAET identity (
24
),
we derive their leading energy-power dependence for
N
longitudinal
as follows:

\displaystyle D_{E}(N\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn})=4\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 16.5026ptD_{E}(N\hskip-0.85358ptA_{5}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn})=4\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-1.42262ptN\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-1.42262ptN_{v}\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-1.42262pt\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(78a)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow~~D_{E}(N\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn})\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptD_{E}(N\hskip-0.85358ptA_{5}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn})=N\!+\hskip-0.85358ptN_{v}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(78b)

where
\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}
denotes
the involved minimal number of non-derivative cubic vertices
in the KK Goldstone boson amplitude,
with
\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}=0\,(1)
for
N\!=\hskip 0.85358pt
even (odd). Hence, this proves that the GAET identity (
24
)
guarantees nontrivial energy cancellations by an energy power of

E^{N}
(or,
E^{N+1}
) for
N\!\!=\hskip 0.85358pt
even (or,
N\!\!=\hskip 0.85358pt
odd)
in the scattering amplitude of
N
longitudinal KK gauge bosons. In the above,
N_{v}
denotes the number of possible
external
v_{\mu}A_{n}^{a\mu}
lines in the KK Goldstone boson amplitude,
where
v_{\mu}\!\!=\!O(M_{n}/E)
in the high energy limit. We find that the above large energy cancellations [as shown in
Eqs.(
76b
), (
77b
) and
(
78b
)] hold for both the flat and warped
5d KK gravity (gauge) theories. Before ending this discussion, we further comment on
the
N\!=\!3
case for KK gauge boson (KK Goldstone boson) scattering amplitudes. The 3-point longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude (
3A_{L}^{an}
) has a leading energy dependence
of
E^{4}
by the power counting rule (
78a
),
which arises from the trilinear KK gauge boson vertex
(containing one external momentum) and the three external longitudinal polarization vectors
(each of which scales like
\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}\!\sim\!p_{j}^{\mu}/M_{n_{j}}
in the high energy limit). On the other hand, the corresponding 3-point KK Goldstone amplitude vanishes at tree level
due to the absence of cubic vertex
3A_{5}^{an}
. According to the GAET identity (
24
), the leading nonzero contribution
on its right-hand side arises from the residual terms with the trilinear vertex having
two KK Goldstone bosons and one KK gauge boson (contracting with the external vector
v^{\mu}
). So we have
(N,N_{v})\!=\!(3,1)
and
\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}\!=\!0
, and thus this residual term
has energy power

D_{E}\!=\!4\!-\!3\!-\!1\!-\!0\!=\!0
,
showing that the right-hand side of the GAET identity (
24
)
has leading energy dependence of
E^{0}
. Hence, the total energy reduction of the 3-point longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude is

E^{4}\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}
.

We also note that for the Goldberger-Wise (GW) model
[
56
]
, it further introduces a bulk scalar field

\hat{\mathcal{S}}
to stabilize the size of the 5d extra dimension of the RS1. Its zero-mode
\mathcal{S}_{0}

can mix with the radion field
{h}_{0}^{55}
, so that the physical radion after stabilization becomes a mixture
of
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
{h}_{0}^{55}
, whereas the scalar KK mode
\mathcal{S}_{n}
mixes with the Goldstone KK mode
h_{n}^{55}

(denoted as
\phi_{n}
in our notation)
at each KK level, leading to a mass-eigenstate
\mathcal{S}_{n}^{\prime}
and the true Goldstone KK state
h_{n}^{55\prime}
. Thus, the gauge-fixing term (
30
)-(
31
) should be extended to
contain the true KK Goldstone boson field (
h_{n}^{55\prime}
). Consequently, the GRET (
75
) can be extended to hold the equivalence between
the scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK graviton states (
h_{n}^{L}
)
and of the true KK Goldstone boson states (
h_{n}^{55\prime}
)
in the high energy limit.

]

which studied the tree-level formulation of the

and the

[

]
.
Our general formulations in this section significantly differ from
[

]

in several aspects. We first derived the new GAET for the warped KK gauge theory in Sec.
