,
in addition to deriving the general GRET for warped KK gravity theory in Sec.
2.3
. The GAET of the warped KK gauge theory is the focus of our present work and serves as
the most fundamental KK ET formulation, because we will prove in Sec.
4

that the GRET of the warped KK gravity can be derived (reconstructed) from the warped GAET
by our leading-order double-copy method. We derived in Sec.
2.3
two new BRST identities
(
43
) and (
64
) to generally amputate the
mixed

external KK graviton states (
h_{n}^{\mu\nu}
) and KK Goldstone states
(
\phi_{n}
or
\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu}
), which are needed because such mixings generally reappear
at loop levels. We presented GRET type-II in general
R_{\xi}
gauge up to loop level (Sec.
2.3.3
)
and GRET type-I up to loop level in ’t Hooft-Feynman gauge for simplicity (Sec.
2.3.2
),
whereas in Appendix
B.2
we also formulated the GRET type-I
(including the LSZ reduction for external states) in the general
R_{\xi}
gauge
at tree level. Using the generalized power-counting method
[

]

for the compactified KK GR theories [with the GRET (
71
) and (
57
)]
and the KK gauge theories [with the GAET (

)],
we proved the different energy cancellations
for the scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gravitions (with helicity-0)
in Eq.(
76
), of the KK gravitons (with helicity
\pm 1
)
in Eq.(
77
), and of the KK gauge bosons in Eq.(
78
).

3
Structure of Massive Scattering Amplitudes from Warped

Gauge and Gravitational Equivalence Theorems

In this section, we systematically compute the 3-point and 4-point scattering amplitudes
of KK gauge bosons (KK gravitons) and of their corresponding KK Goldstone bosons. Then, we explicitly demonstrate the validity of KK GAET (
26
)
and GRET (
59
)(
75
)
by comparing the scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons (KK gravitons)
with their corresponding KK Goldstone boson amplitudes
at the leading order of high energy expansion. The major motivation of this section is not only to explicitly verify the GAET and GRET
for the 3-point and 4-point massive KK scattering amplitudes,
but also to explicitly analyze and uncover the
structures

of the basic massive KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes under the warped compactification,
which are highly intricate due to various nontrivial large energy-cancellations
in these massive amplitudes
and due to the warped nonlinear KK mass-spectra plus KK couplings. Moreover, the analyses of this section provide all the 3-point and 4-point KK
gauge/gravity amplitudes that are needed for the extended double-copy study
in Section
4
.

3.1
Warped Gauge and Gravitational ETs for
3-Point KK Amplitudes

In this subsection, we systematically compute the basic
3-point scattering amplitudes of massive KK gauge bosons at tree level
in Section
3.1.1
, and the basic 3-point scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons in Section
3.1.2
.

3.1.1
Warped GAET for 3-Point KK Gauge Boson Amplitudes

To compute the on-shell 3-point KK gauge and Goldstone scattering amplitudes,
we derive the Feynman rules for cubic KK gauge/Goldstone vertices
in Appendix
C.1
. Then, we derive the following general 3-point on-shell
scattering amplitude of KK gauge bosons:

\hskip-11.38109pt\mathcal{T}[A^{an_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}\!\hskip-0.85358ptA^{bn_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}\!\hskip-0.85358ptA^{cn_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}]=-\text{i}2\hskip 0.85358ptg\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}f^{abc}\hskip-0.85358pt\left[(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})\right]\!,

(79)

where the subscript
\lambda_{j}\!\!=\!\hskip-0.85358pt\pm,0
(
j=1,2,3
) denotes the helicity
of each external state and
\epsilon_{j}^{\mu}(=\!\epsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu})
is
the shorthand notation for the polarization vector of each external
KK auge boson. All the three external momenta
\{p_{j}\}

are outgoing and obey the momentum conservation

p_{1}\!+p_{2}\!+p_{3}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt
. In the above tree-level scattering amplitude,
g
is the Yang-Mills gauge coupling and

a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}
denotes coupling coefficient induced by
integrating over the compactified 5th-dimension coordinate
as defined in Eq.(
334
).

With these, we compute the 3-point scattering amplitude of
KK gauge bosons of
LLT
type at tree level, which contains
two longitudinal KK gauge bosons [chosen as particles 1 and 2 and
denoted by
A_{L}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{1})
and
A_{L}^{b\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{2})
],
and one transverse KK gauge boson [chosen as the particle 3 and
denoted by
A_{\pm}^{c\hskip 0.85358ptn_{3}}\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{3})
]. Thus, we derive the following 3-point KK gauge boson amplitude:

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an_{1}}\!A_{L}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{\pm}^{cn_{3}}]\,

\displaystyle=\,\mp\text{i}\hskip 0.85358ptgf^{abc}\frac{\,[E_{1}^{2}\!+\!E_{2}^{2}\!+\!E_{3}^{2}\!-\!2E_{1}E_{2}(1\!+\!c_{2\theta})\!-\!2k^{2}]k\hskip 0.85358pts_{\theta}\,}{\sqrt{2\,}M_{n_{1}}\!M_{n_{2}}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}

\displaystyle=\,\mp\text{i}\hskip 0.85358ptg\hskip 0.85358ptf^{abc}\frac{~(M_{n_{3}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{2}}^{2})k\hskip 0.85358pts_{\theta}~}{\sqrt{2\,}M_{n_{1}}\!M_{n_{2}}}\hskip 0.85358pt{a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}}+O(E^{0})

(80)

\displaystyle=\,-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358ptg\hskip 0.85358ptf^{abc}(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt{p_{1}})\frac{~(M_{n_{3}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{2}}^{2})~}{M_{n_{1}}M_{n_{2}}}\hskip 0.85358pt{a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}}+O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt,

where each external momentum is on-shell (
\hskip 0.85358ptp_{j\mu}\hskip 0.85358ptp_{j}^{\mu}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt-M_{n_{j}}^{2}
) and

s_{\theta}\!=\!\sin\hskip-0.85358pt\theta\,
with
\theta
denoting the angle
between the moving directions of particles 1 and 3. The cubic coupling coefficient of KK gauge bosons
a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!

is given by Eq.(
334
). We further compute the corresponding 3-point scattering amplitude with
particles 1 and 2 replaced by the KK Goldstone bosons,

A_{5}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{1})A_{5}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{2})
. Thus, we derive the following 3-point scattering amplitudes of KK Goldstone
and gauge bosons:
6
6

6

From this section and hereafter,
we denote scattering amplitudes containing external Goldstone states with a tilde symbol,
such as
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
, to distinguish them from the amplitudes with all external states
being physical particles.

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A_{5}^{an_{1}}\!A_{5}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{\pm}^{cn_{3}}]\,=\,-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358ptgf^{abc}(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}=\mp\text{i}\hskip 0.85358ptg\hskip 0.85358ptf^{abc}\hskip-0.85358pt\sqrt{2\,}k\hskip 0.85358pts_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(81)

where the cubic KK Goldstone-gauge coupling coefficient
\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!

is given by Eq.(
334
).

According to the KK GAET (
26
), the equivalence between the two KK scattering amplitudes (
80
) and (
81
) in the high energy limit,

\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an_{1}}\!A_{L}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{\pm}^{cn_{3}}]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt-\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an_{1}}\!A_{5}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{\pm}^{cn_{3}}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(82)

where
\mathcal{T}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
denote the leading-order KK amplitudes
under high energy expansion.
The 3-point GAET (
82
) is equivalent to the following
condition:

\left(M_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!+\!M_{n_{2}}^{2}\!-\!M_{n_{3}}^{2}\right)a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}=\,2{M_{n_{1}}\!M_{n_{2}}}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(83)

which connects the trilinear KK gauge boson coupling
\,a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip-0.85358pt

to the corresponding trilinear KK Goldstone-gauge boson coupling

\,\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}
. The condition (
83
)
does hold for the warped 5d KK Yang-Mills gauge theory
as we directly prove it in Eq.(
352
) of Appendix
D
.
For the special case of
(n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{3})=(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)
,
the condition (
83
) takes the following simple form:

\big(1\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\hskip 0.85358ptr_{j}^{2}\big)a_{nnj}=\,\tilde{a}_{nnj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(84)

where we denote
\,r_{j}\!=\!M_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
. We can also examine the condition (
83
) by taking the flat 5d limit. Under this limit, we have the KK indices

\{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\}\!=\!\{n,n,2n\},\{n,n,0\}
,
the KK mass
M_{n}\!=\!n/R
, and the KK couplings

a_{nn2n}\!=\!-\tilde{a}_{nn2n}\!=\!1/\sqrt{2\,}
and

a_{nn0}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nn0}\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
,
as shown in Table
1
of Appendix
C
. For the case
\{n,n,2n\}
, we can use the coupling relation

a_{nn2n}\!=\!-\tilde{a}_{nn2n}
to simplify Eq.(
83
) as

M_{n_{1}}\!\!\!+\!M_{n_{2}}\!\!=\!M_{n_{3}}
,
which corresponds to the KK number conservation

n_{1}\!+\!n_{2}\!=\!n_{3}
. But, under the warped 5d compactification, the 5d momentum is no longer conserved
due to the absence of translation invariance,
so that both the mass formula
and coupling relation of the flat 5d limit do not hold,
and the condition (
83
) works in a highly nontrivial way
[as proved in Eq.(
352
)].

Next, we compute the 3-point KK gauge boson scattering amplitude including
one longitudinal and two transverse external states and the corresponding KK Goldstone-gauge boson
scattering amplitude:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[A^{an_{1}}_{\pm}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{\pm}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{L}]

\displaystyle=-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358ptgf^{abc}{a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}}\Big\{\!(\epsilon_{1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})\!\!\left[\!(M_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{2}}^{2})M_{n_{3}}^{-1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\!(p_{1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})\!\cdot\!v_{3}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt+\!2(\epsilon_{1}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-\!2(\epsilon_{1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\!\Big\}

\displaystyle=0+O(E^{0})\,,

(85a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A^{an_{1}}_{\pm}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{\pm}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{5}]

\displaystyle=gf^{abc}\big(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}\big)(\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{3}n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n_{1}}\!-\tilde{a}_{n_{2}n_{3}n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n_{2}})

\displaystyle=gf^{abc}{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})\big(M_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{2}}^{2}\big)\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n_{3}}^{-1}=0+O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(85b)

where
v_{3}^{\mu}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3,L}^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3}^{\mu}/{M_{n_{3}}}\!\!=\!O(M_{n_{3}}\hskip-0.85358pt/\hskip-0.85358ptE_{3})
,
and we have used the sum rules (
351
)
to simplify the second KK amplitude (
85b
). From the above, we see that the leading contributions of
O(E^{1})
vanishes,
and the remaining contributions are of the same
O(E^{0})
as the residual term

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{v}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}[A^{an_{1}}_{\pm}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{\pm}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{\mu}]\hskip 0.85358ptv^{\mu}
.

We have also analyzed the 3-point scattering amplitudes
of the pure longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of their corresponding KK Goldstone bosons. The 3-point longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude
\mathcal{T}[3A_{L}^{an}]

has the leading energy dependence
of
E^{4}
by the power counting rule (
78a
),
which arises from the trilinear KK gauge boson vertex
(depending on the first power of external momenta)
and the three external longitudinal polarization vectors,
each of which has the high energy behavior

\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}=\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptv^{\mu}=O(E^{1})\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptO(E^{-1})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(86)

according to Eq.(
23b
),
where
\hskip 0.85358pt\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}\!=\!p_{j}^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt/M_{n_{j}}
for the
j
-th external state (
j\!=\!1,2,3
). The four external momenta (from either the trilinear vertex or the
\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}
part
of the external longitudinal polarization vector
\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}
) will form two bilinear contractions
like
\,p_{i}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j}
or
p_{j}^{2}
, which can be converted into squared KK-masses
due to the momentum conservation
\hskip 0.85358ptp_{1}\!+\!p_{2}\!+\!p_{3}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

and the on-shell condition of each external momentum
p_{j}^{2}\!=\!\!-M_{n_{j}}^{2}
. So all the leading energy dependence of
E^{4}
will be reduced to
E^{0}
. The remaining contributions
arise from replacing the external longitudinal polarization vectors by one, two, and three
vectors of
v^{\mu}\!\!=\!O(E^{-1})
,
which can contribute to the 3-point KK gauge boson amplitude with leading energy dependence of

E^{0}
,
E^{0}
, and
E^{-2}
respectively. Hence, the 3-point longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude should actually have a deduction on
its leading energy dependence,
E^{4}\!\rightarrow\!E^{0}
. We can show that this is consistent with the requirement of the GAET identity (
24
). We note that the corresponding 3-point KK Goldstone amplitude vanishes at tree level
due to the absence of cubic vertex
A_{5}^{an}
-
A_{5}^{am}
-
A_{5}^{a\ell}
. Thus, according to the GAET identity (
24
), the leading nonzero contribution
on its right-hand side arises from the residual terms with the trilinear vertex having
two KK Goldstone bosons and one KK gauge boson (contracting with the external vector
v^{\mu}
)
which is apparently of
O(E^{0})
. Note that the second power counting rule in
Eq.(
78a
) gives:

D_{E}\!=\!4\!-\!3\!-\!1\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
, where we have inputs

(N,\hskip 0.85358ptN_{v})\!=\!(3,\hskip 0.85358pt1)
and
\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. Hence, the GAET identity (
24
) also enforces the energy deduction of

E^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptE^{0}
for the 3-point longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude on its
left-hand side.

Then, we explicily compute the 3-point scattering amplitude of pure longitudinal KK gauge bosons,
in which each external longituding polarization vector can be split
into two parts,
\epsilon_{L}^{\mu}=\epsilon_{S}^{\mu}+v^{\mu}
, as shown in Eq.(
86
). Thus, we derive the following expanded scattering amplitude of 3 longitudinal KK gauge bosons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[A^{an_{1}}_{L}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{L}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{L}]=-\text{i}gf^{abc}({M_{n_{1}}\!M_{n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n_{3}}})^{-1}a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164pt\Big[(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})-(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt+(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(v_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(v_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})+(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})+(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt-(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})-(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})+(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})(v_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})+(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})(v_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})(v_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})\Big].

(87)

In the above amplitude, we see that all the terms having leading energy dependence of
E^{4}
disappear as expected,
and all the remaining terms can be classfied into the following 4 types according to the numbers of
v_{j}
’s
contained therein:

(p_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j})(p_{k}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{\ell}),\quad(p_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{j})(p_{k}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{\ell}),\quad(p_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j})(v_{k}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{\ell}),\quad(p_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{j})(v_{k}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{\ell}).

(88)

Due to the momentum conservation

\hskip 0.85358ptp_{1}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

and the on-shell condition of each external momentum,
the bilinear momentum products can all be converted into squared KK-masses,

\hskip 0.85358ptp_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j}\!=\!\frac{1}{2}(M_{n_{i}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\!M_{n_{j}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!M_{n_{k}}^{2})

(with
i\!\neq\!j\!\neq\!k
)
and
p_{j}^{2}\!=\!-M_{n_{j}}^{2}
. Since
v_{j}\!=\!O(M_{n_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt/E_{j})
,
the 3 longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude (
87
)
could only have leading energy dependence of
E^{0}
,
which is given by the terms of the first and second types in Eq.(
88
). Thus, the GAET identity (
24
) for the 3-point tree-level scattering amplitude of longitudinal KK gauge bosons
take the following form:

\mathcal{T}[A^{an_{1}}_{L}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{L}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{L}]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\text{i}^{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A^{an_{1}}_{5}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{5}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{5}]\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{v}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt0+O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(89)

where on the right-hand side the 3-point KK Goldstone amplitude vanishes and the residual term
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{v}\hskip 0.85358pt

gives the leading nonzero contributions
to the corresponding longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude on the left-hand side. Finally, we can further deduce the
O(E^{0})
contributions to the 3-point scattering amplitude
of pure longitudinal KK gauge bosons as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\mathcal{T}_{0}[A^{an_{1}}_{L}\!A^{bn_{2}}_{L}\!A^{cn_{3}}_{L}]=-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358ptgf^{abc}(M_{n_{1}}M_{n_{2}}M_{n_{3}})^{-1}a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\times

\displaystyle\Big[M_{13}(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})\!-\!M_{12}(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})\!-\!M_{23}(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})\!+\!M_{23}(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})\!+\!M_{12}(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})\!-\!M_{13}(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})

\displaystyle+(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})\!-\!(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})-(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})\!+\!(p_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})

\displaystyle+(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{2})-(p_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{3})(p_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptv_{1})\Big],

(90)

where we have defined

{M_{ij}}\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358ptp_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j}\!=\!\frac{1}{2}(M_{n_{i}}^{2}\!+\!M_{n_{j}}^{2}\!-\!M_{n_{k}}^{2})

with
i\!\neq\!j\!\neq\!k\hskip 0.85358pt
denoting the three external states.

3.1.2
Warped GRET for 3-Point KK Graviton Amplitudes

In this subsection, we compute the 3-point
massive scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons
and of the corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone bosons. With these we explicitly demonstrate that the KK GRETs
(
75
) and (
59
) hold at tree level.

Using the Feynman rules of Appendix
C.2
, we explicitly compute the most general
3-point on-shell scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons at tree level as given in Eq.(
346
). Using this, we derive the 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitude with the external states (1, 2, 3)
having helicities
(\pm 1,\,\pm 1,\,\pm 2)
. We also compute the corresponding on-shell
3-point scattering amplitudes with external states (1,2) replaced by the gravitational
KK vector Goldstone bosons having helicities
\pm 1\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, we derive the following 3-point
gravitational KK scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]={-\kappa}\Big[(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}^{L})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})+(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}^{L})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}^{L})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2}^{L})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}

(91a)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})+(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})+(\epsilon_{1}\cdot p_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{1}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})+2(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1}^{L})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{2}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})\Big]\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]={\kappa}\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-(\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!+\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}\!\!+\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}^{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})\Big]\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(91b)

where
\epsilon_{j}\!=\!\epsilon_{j}^{\pm}
denotes transverse polarization vector associated
with the external KK graviton
j\hskip 0.85358pt(=\!1,2,3)
,
and
\epsilon_{j^{\prime}}^{L}
(
j^{\prime}\!=\!1,2
) denotes longitudinal polarization vector associated
with the external KK graviton
j^{\prime}
. [The polarization tenors of each massive KK graviton with helicities
(\pm 2,\,\pm 1,\,\pm 0)

are defined in Eq.(
262
) of Appendix
A
.]
In the above, the three-point gravitational KK coupling coefficients

(\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},\,\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}})

are defined in Eq.(
334
) of Appendix
C.1
. Then, we make high energy expansions of the above 3-point gravitational KK scattering amplitudes
(
91a
) and (
91b
),
and derive their leading-order scattering amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle=\frac{\,\kappa\big(\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}^{2}\!\!-\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!-\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\big)\,}{2\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}}\Big[\!(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\!+(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\Big]\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},

(92a)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle={\kappa}\Big[(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\Big]\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}.

(92b)

The equivalence between the two leading-order KK scattering amplitudes

\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}
in Eqs.(
92a
)-(
92b
)
is realized by the identity,

\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(93)

where the minus sign on the right-hand side comes from the multiplicative modification factor

\hat{C}_{\mathrm{mod}}\!=(-\text{i})^{2}
at tree level,
and Eq.(
93
) enforces the following condition:

\left(\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!+\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\!-\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}^{2}\right)\!\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!=\,2\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(94)

We have directly proved that the above condition (
94
)
does hold for the warped 5d KK gravity theory
as shown in Eq.(
354
) of Appdenix
D
. We compare the above Eq.(
94
) with Eq.(
83
) which holds the
GAET (
82
)
for 3-point KK gauge boson (KK Goldstone boson) amplitude. We observe that the condition (
94
) has
identical structure
to
the condition (
83
) and can be inferred from Eq.(
83
)
under the following mass and coupling correspondences (replacements):

M_{n_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}}\!\!\rightarrow\mathbb{M}_{n_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}},~~~~a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\!\rightarrow\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},~~~~\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\!\rightarrow\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}.

(95)

As we will show in Section
4.1
, the above correspondences (replacements) are
consistent with the double-copy construction of the GRET from the GAET
for the 3-point KK scattering amplitudes.

Besides, for the special case of
(n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)
,
the condition (
94
) can be simplified as follows:

\left(1\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\right)\!\alpha_{nnj}=\,\tilde{\alpha}_{nnj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(96)

with the defined mass ratio
\,\hat{r}_{j}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt/\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Next, we derive the 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitude with external states (1, 2)
being longitudinal KK gravitons of helicity-0 and with the external state 3 having helicities
\pm 2\hskip 0.85358pt
. We also compute the 3-point KK Goldstone scattering amplitude with the external states (1, 2)
replaced by the corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone bosons. Thus, using the general 3-point KK amplitudes (
346
) of Appendix
C.2
,
we infer the following 3-point gravitational KK scattering amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pth^{L}_{n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pth^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle=\frac{\,\kappa\hskip 0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\,}{\,6\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\,}\!\left[2\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\!\left(\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\!+\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\!-\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}^{2}\right)^{\!2}\,\right]\!\hskip-0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(97a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n_{1}}\!\phi_{n_{2}}\!h_{n_{3}}^{\pm 2}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle=\kappa(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(97b)

where the trilinear KK coupling coefficient
\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!

is defined in Eq.(
340c
) of Appendix
C.1
and the KK Goldstone boson amplitude
(
97b
) actually equals its full amplitude without expansion,
i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[\phi_{n_{1}}\!\phi_{n_{2}}\!h_{n_{3}}^{\pm 2}\hskip 0.85358pt]\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\kappa(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}
. Then, to hold the KK GRET (
71
) for the 3-point KK scattering amplitudes,

\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}h^{L}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n_{1}}\phi_{n_{2}}h_{n_{3}}^{\pm 2}\hskip 0.85358pt]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(98)

we derive the following condition to ensure
the equivalence between the two gravitational KK scattering amplitudes (
97a
) and (
97b
):

\left[\!\left(\hskip-0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}^{2}\right)^{\hskip-0.85358pt2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\right]\!\hskip-0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}=\,6\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(99)

The proof of the condition (
99
) is highly nontrivial and
we present a direct proof of it around Eq.(
360
) of Appdenix
D
. For the case of
(n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)
,
the condition (
99
) can be simplified as follows:

(\hat{r}_{j}^{4}\!-\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\!+\!6)\alpha_{nnj}=\,6\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(100)

with the mass ratio
\,\hat{r}_{j}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt/\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Then, we consider a mixed 3-point scattering amplitudes including two external KK gravitons with helicities
\pm 1
and one external KK graviton with helicity zero. Thus, we derive the following 3-point scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons at the leading order of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{L}_{n_{3}}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\,\kappa\big[2\!-\!(\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!+\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2})\!-\!(\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!-\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2})^{2}\big]\,}{\,2\sqrt{6\,}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\hat{r}_{23}\,}(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(101a)

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\big[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}\phi_{n_{3}}\big]

\displaystyle={-\kappa}\Bigg[\!\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\,}(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\!-\!\frac{\,{\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}^{2}}\hskip 0.85358pt}{\sqrt{6\,}\hskip 0.85358pt}(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\hat{r}_{13}\hat{r}_{23}\!+\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\!+\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23})\!\Bigg]\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{\rho}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}\phi_{n_{3}}\big]+O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(101b)

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{0}\big[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}\phi_{n_{3}}\big]

\displaystyle={-\kappa}\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\tilde{\rho}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(101c)

where we have defined the KK mass ratios

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}\!/\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}
and

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt/\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}
,
and the
\mathcal{V}_{n_{1}}^{\pm 1}
-
\mathcal{V}_{n_{2}}^{\pm 1}
-
\phi_{n}
trilinear coupling coefficient

\tilde{\rho}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}
is defined in Eq.(
340
) of Appendix
C
. There is another helicity combination for such mixed 3-point KK scattering amplitudes, but they vanish at the
leading order of high energy expansion:

\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\mp 1}_{n_{2}}h^{L}_{n_{3}}\big]=0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{0}\big[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\mp 1}_{n_{2}}{\phi_{n_{3}}}\big]=0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(102)

Using the 3-point gravitational KK scattering amplitudes (
101a
)-(
101c
),
we find that holding the GRET,

\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{L}_{n_{3}}\big]\,=\,-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}\phi_{n_{3}}\big],

(103)

will impose the following condition:

\Big[2\!-\!(\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!+\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2})\!-\!(\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!-\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2})^{2}\Big]{\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}}\!=\hskip 0.85358pt6\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\hat{r}_{23}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\rho}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(104)

where the KK mass ratios
(\hat{r}_{13},\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23})
and
the trilinear KK coupling coefficient
\tilde{\rho}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}
are
defined below Eq.(
101
). The above identity (
104
) can be directly proven
as in (
362
) of Appendix
D
. Hence, we have explicitly established the GRET in its mixed form (
103
)
for the 3-point gravitational KK scattering amplitudes.

Besides, we consider a specific case with

(n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)
. So we have
\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\!=\!\hat{r}_{23}\hskip 0.85358pt
and thus
the above GRET condition (
104
) is simplified as follows:

(\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\!-\!1)\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{nnj}=3\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\rho}_{nnj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(105)

where the mass ratio
\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{j}/\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
. For the case of compactified flat 5d under
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
the KK index
j
could only take values of
j=0,2n
in Eq.(
105
).

Finally, we discuss the 3-point scattering amplitudes of pure longitudinal KK gravitons (
3h_{n}^{L}
) and
of the corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone bosons (
3\phi_{n}
). We gave a brief comment on this in a separate paragraph below Eq.(
76
). The tree-level 3-point longitudinal KK graviton amplitude
\mathcal{M}[3h_{n}^{L}]
has a
possible leading energy dependence of
E^{8}
by using the power counting rule (
76a
)
for
N\!\!=\!3\,
. But, we note that the 3-point KK graviton amplitude arises from the trilinear KK graviton vertex
(whose coupling factor contains products of two external momenta)
and is expected to have individual leading energy dependence of
E^{8}

after including the 3 external longitudinal polarization tenors
of KK gravitons, each of which has the high energy behavior,

\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}_{L}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt\varepsilon_{S}^{\mu\nu}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}=O(E^{2})+O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(106)

based on Eqs.(
62
)-(
63
), where for the
j
-th external KK graviton the
scalar polarization tensor

\varepsilon_{S}^{\mu\nu}\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{j})\!=\!p_{j}^{\mu}p_{j}^{\nu}/\mathbb{M}_{n_{j}}^{2}
(with
j\!=\!1,2,3
). Thus, each leading energy term of
O(E^{8})
is given by picking up the leading energy term
\varepsilon_{S}^{\mu\nu}

from all 3 external longitudinal polarization tenors (
\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt
) which have contractions with the
two-momentum-factors in of the trilinear KK graviton vertex. These momenta will form up 4 pairs of bilinear
contraction (
\,p_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j}
) which can be all converted into certain combinations of
squared KK-masses by using the momentum conservation
\hskip 0.85358ptp_{1}\!+\!p_{2}\!+\!p_{3}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

and the on-shell condition of each external momentum
p_{j}^{2}\!=\!-M_{n_{j}}^{2}
. Hence, all the leading energy terms of
O(E^{8})
are reduced to
O(E^{0})
in this way. The remaining contributions include to replace one, two, and three external longitudinal polarization tensors
(
\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt
) by the residual tensor
\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}\!\!=\!O(E^{0})
respectively. For those external momentum factors contracting among themselves, they will be converted into squared KK-masses
due to the momentum conservation and the on-shell conditions on all the external momenta. Hence, a given external momentum
p_{j}^{\mu}
could contribute a nontrivial energy factor

E^{1}
to the 3-point KK graviton amplitude only if it contracts with a residual tensor

\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!O(E^{0})
from Eq.(
106
). The terms having maximal number of such contractions arise from picking up two

\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}
tensors from two of the external KK graviton states via Eq.(
106
); so the
third external longitudinal polarization tensor contributes a leading momentum term of
\varepsilon_{S}^{\mu\nu}

via Eq.(
106
) and the trilinear KK graviton vertex contributes the leading coupling factors
with two external momenta; thus all the 4 external momenta may contract with the two external
\tilde{v}^{\mu\nu}
tensors
[via Eq.(
106
)] and thus makes the leading energy contributions of
O(E^{4})
. On the other hand, the tree-level amplitude of 3 gravitational
KK Goldstone bosons
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[3\phi_{n}]
arises from trilinear KK Goldstone vertex
whose coupling factor contains products of two external momenta

\,p_{i}\cdot p_{j}
. So the naive power counting shows that this KK Goldstone amplitude
would have leading energy dependence of
E^{2}
in agreement with Eq.(
76a
). But the momentum conservation and the on-shell condition of each external state convert
all the bilinear momentum products (
\,p_{i}\cdot p_{j}
) in the trilinear Goldstone vertex coupling
into squared KK-masses, so the 3-point KK Goldstone amplitude actually has energy dependence
E^{0}
. Then, we can apply the GRET (
71
) and note that on its right-hand side the corresponding 3-point
KK Goldstone amplitude has the actual leading energy dependence
E^{0}
. Hence, the GRET further ensures a large energy cancellation of
\hskip 0.85358ptE^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptE^{0}\hskip 0.85358pt

in the 3-point longitudinal KK graviton amplitude.

Then, we can explicitly compute the 3-point scattering amplitudes of pure longitudinal KK gravitons

\mathcal{M}[3h_{n}^{L}]
and
of the corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone bosons
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[3\phi_{n}]
. Under high energy expansion, we derive
these two KK amplitudes down to
O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt
:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{M}\big[h^{L}_{n_{1}}\!h^{L}_{n_{2}}\!h^{L}_{n_{3}}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{~{-\kappa}\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hat{r}_{12}^{2}\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}~}{24\sqrt{6\,}}\hskip-0.85358pt\big(\!-\!6698\!+\!7210\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{31}^{2}\!-\!20400\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{2}\!-\!18940\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{4}\!+\!14830\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{2}r_{31}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\!26471\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{31}^{4}\!-\!5520\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{6}\!+\!7310\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{4}\hat{r}_{31}^{2}\!-\!2950\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{2}\hat{r}_{31}^{4}\!+\!362\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{31}^{6}\!-\!186\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{8}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\!458\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{6}\hat{r}_{31}^{2}\!-\!375\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{4}\hat{r}_{31}^{4}\!+\!114\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{2}\hat{r}_{31}^{6}\!-\!11\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{31}^{8}\big),

(107a)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\big[\phi_{n_{1}}\!\phi_{n_{2}}\!\phi_{n_{3}}\big]

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\,}\kappa\Big[\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\omega}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}^{2}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\big(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}_{21}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}_{31}^{2}\big)\!+\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.6793pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\omega$}}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.6793pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\omega$}}_{n_{2}n_{3}n_{1}}\!\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.6793pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\omega$}}_{n_{3}n_{1}n_{2}}\Big],

(107b)

where we have defined the mass ratio
\hat{r}_{ij}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{i}}/\mathbb{M}_{n_{\!j}}\hskip-0.85358pt
,
the trilinear KK graviton coupling
\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}
and KK Goldstone couplings

(\tilde{\omega}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.10205pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\omega$}}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}})

are defined in Eq.(
340
). We see that both the above KK scattering amplitudes receive nonzero contributions at
O(E^{0})
. Even though the power counting rule (
76a
) suggests that the 3-point
gravitational KK Goldstone amplitude (
107b
) would scale like
E^{2}
,
we find that its actual leading energy dependence is only of
O(E^{0})

due to the momentum conservation and
the on-shell condition for each external momentum, which agrees with our discussion
in the previous paragraph.
7
7

7

From the viewpoint of double-copy (cf. Sec.
4.1
),
this is also related to the fact that the trilinear KK Goldstone vertex

A_{5}^{an}
-
A_{5}^{bm}
-
A_{5}^{c\ell}
vanishes identically in the compactified KK Yang-Mills gauge theories
as shown in Eq.(
89
).
According to the GRET (
71
) together with Eq.(
74
),
the residual term contains an amplitude having two (spin-0) KK Goldstone states (
\phi_{n}
and
\phi_{m}
)
and one external KK state
v_{\mu}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}_{\ell}
(with helicity
\pm 1
), which also contributes to
O(E^{0})
. This explains why the two KK amplitudes (
107a
) and (
107b
)
do not simply equal despite that they are both of
O(E^{0})
.

3.2
Warped GAET and GRET:
from 3-Point to 4-Point Amplitudes

In Section
3.1
we have explicitly established the GAET for the 3-point
KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes and the GRET for the 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitudes
in the high energy limit. Using the GAET and GRET for the basic 3-point KK scattering amplitudes,
we will further prove explicitly in this subsection that the GAET and GRET hold for the 4-point
KK scattering amplitudes in the high energy limit. This is realized by proving that the
mass-coupling identities (sum rules) ensuring the basic 3-point-level GAET and GRET can further
guarantee the validity of the GAET and GRET for the 4-point KK scattering amplitudes. With these we also conjecture that this proof can be extended through iterations
to the general
N
-point (
N\hskip-0.85358pt\!\geqslant\!5
) KK scattering amplitudes in the high energy limit.

3.2.1
Warped GAET:
from 3-Point to 4-Point KK Amplitudes

In this section, we first compute the 4-point elastic scattering amplitude of longitudinal KK gauge bosons

\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\rightarrow\!A_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]
and the corresponding KK Goldstone boson scattering amplitude

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bn}\!\rightarrow\!A_{5}^{cn}A_{5}^{dn}]
at tree level in the ’t Hooft-Feynman gauge (
\xi_{n}\!=\!1
). Under high energy expansion, we derive the leading-order KK amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\big(\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{K}_{s}^{0}+\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{K}_{t}^{0}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{K}_{u}^{0}\big),

(108a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{5}^{cn}A_{5}^{dn}]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\big(\mathcal{C}_{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{0}+\mathcal{C}_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{0}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{0}\big),

(108b)

where the non-Abelian group factors
(\mathcal{C}_{s},\,\mathcal{C}_{t},\,\mathcal{C}_{u})
are defined as

\big(\,\mathcal{C}_{s},\,\mathcal{C}_{t},\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\,\big)=\big(f^{abe}f^{cde}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,f^{ade}f^{bce}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,f^{ace}f^{dbe}\big),

(109)

and they satisfy the color Jacobi identity:

\mathcal{C}_{s}\!+\mathcal{C}_{t}\!+\mathcal{C}_{u}=0\,.

(110)

In Eqs.(
108a
)-(
108b
), both the leading-order KK scattering amplitudes
are of
O(E^{0})
under the high energy expansion, as required by the KK GAET (
26
)
which enforces the energy cancellation of
E^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!E^{0}
for the 4-point
longitudinal KK scattering amplitude
\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

as shown in Eq.(
78
). The exact cancellations of
O(E^{4})
and
O(E^{2})
terms in
longitudinal KK amplitude
\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

impose the following sum-rule-type conditions respectively:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!a_{nnj}^{2}

\displaystyle=a_{nnnn}\,,

(111a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}M_{j}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnj}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\,3\,}\hskip 0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}a_{nnnn}\,.

(111b)

The above conditions (
111a
) and (
111b
) are proved respectively
in Eq.(
349a
) and Eq.(
366
) of Appendix
D
. Combining the above two conditions, we can eliminate the quartic coupling
a_{nnnn}
and deduce the
following sum rule on the trilinear coupling coefficient
a_{nnj}
alone:

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\!\frac{\hskip 0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt}{3}-r_{j}^{2}\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\!a_{nnj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt=\,0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(112)

In Eq.(
108
),
the leading-order longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitudes
for each channel are given by

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{K}_{s}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{c_{\theta}}{\,4\,}\Bigg[4\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\big(r_{j}^{2}\!+\!2\big)^{\!2}a^{2}_{nnj}\Bigg],

(113a)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{K}_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,(1\!+\!c_{\theta})\,}\bigg\{\!(6\!-\!19c_{\theta}\!-\!10c_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{3\theta})\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}^{2}+\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\Big[\!\big(3r_{j}^{4}\!-\!10r_{j}^{2}\!+\!6\big)\!-\!\big(r_{j}^{4}\!-\!8r_{j}^{2}\!+\!19\big)c_{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\hskip-0.85358pt2\big(r_{j}^{2}\!-\!5\big)c_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{3\theta}\Big]a_{nnj}^{2}\!-(2\!+\!c_{\theta}\!-\!2c_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{3\theta})\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnnn}\bigg\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(113b)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{K}_{u}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta})\,}\bigg\{\!(6\!+\!19c_{\theta}\!-\!10c_{2\theta}\!+\!c_{3\theta})\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}^{2}+\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\Big[\!\big(3r_{j}^{4}\!-\!10r_{j}^{2}\!+\!6\big)\!+\!\big(r_{j}^{4}\!-\!8r_{j}^{2}\!+\!19\big)c_{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\hskip-0.85358pt2\big(r_{j}^{2}\!-\!5\big)c_{2\theta}\!+\!c_{3\theta}\Big]a_{nnj}^{2}\!-(2\!-\!c_{\theta}\!-\!2c_{2\theta}\!+\!c_{3\theta})\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnnn}\bigg\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(113c)

and the leading-order KK Goldstone boson amplitudes for each channel are given by

\hskip-14.22636pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{0}=-c_{\theta}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,\quad~\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{0}=\frac{\,3\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,\quad~\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{0}=-\frac{\,3\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}{\,1\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(114)

where we define the abbreviations

(s_{n\theta},\hskip 0.85358ptc_{n\theta})\!=\!(\sin\hskip-0.85358ptn\theta,\hskip 0.85358pt\cos\hskip-0.85358ptn\theta)

with
\theta
being the scattering angle. In the above the KK quartic coupling coefficient
a_{nnnn}
is defined
in Eq.(
334
) of Appendix
C
.

The KK GAET (
26
) requires the equivalence between the two leading-order KK scattering amplitudes (
108a
) and (
108b
). To prove this explicitly, we compute the difference between the leading-order sub-amplitudes (
113
) and (
114
) for each scattering channel:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{0}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,c_{\theta}\,}{4}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}r_{j}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(7\!+\!r_{j}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnj}^{2}\!-\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\Bigg],

(115a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{0}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{\,4\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\!c_{\theta})}\!\Bigg\{\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\big[7\!-\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr_{j}^{2}\!+\!(7\!+\!r_{j}^{2})\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\big]\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnj}^{2}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358pt(3\!-\!c_{\theta})\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\!\Bigg\},

(115b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{0}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,4\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\!c_{\theta})}\!\Bigg\{\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\big[7\!-\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr_{j}^{2}\!-\!(7\!+\!r_{j}^{2})\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\big]\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnj}^{2}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358pt(3\!+\!c_{\theta})\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\!\Bigg\},

(115c)

where we have utilized Eq.(
111
) to eliminate the quartic coupling coefficient
\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnnn}\hskip 0.85358pt

and Eq.(
112
) to eliminate the constant coefficient of
a_{nnj}^{2}
. Inpsecting the three difference expressions (
115a
)-(
115c
)
and the sum rule (
112
),
we find that they can be equal to each other provided the following sum rule condition
is satisfied:

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\!1\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{2}r_{j}^{2}\!\right)^{\!\!2}\!a_{nnj}^{2}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(116)

This condition (
116
) can be inferred directly from the identity
(
84
) by squaring its two sides and then summing over KK index
j\hskip 0.85358pt
. Note that Eq.(
84
) was proved to hold the GAET (
82
)
[in the case of
(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)
]
for the most fundamental 3-point KK scattering amplitudes at the leading order. Hence, the validity of the GAET at 4-point-level of KK scattering amplitudes is guaranteed
by the fundamental 3-point-level GAET (
82
).

With these we can further simplify the amplitude-difference in each channel of
Eq.(
115
) and derive the following:

\mathcal{K}_{i}^{0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{0}\,=\,-2\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}a_{nnj}^{2}\,,

(117)

where the index
\hskip 0.85358pti\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}
denotes the kinematic channel under consideration. Hence, we sum up the contributions of three kinematic channels and apply
the color Jacobi identity (
110
)
to prove the equivalence between the two leading-order KK scattering amplitudes
(
108a
) and (
108b
):

\mathcal{T}_{0}[4A^{an}_{L}]\!-\!\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[4A^{an}_{5}]=-2\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}a_{nnj}^{2}\!\times\!{(\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip-0.85358pt+\mathcal{C}_{u})}=0\,.

(118)

In summary, based upon the GAET (
82
)
for the most fundamental 3-point KK scattering amplitudes,
we have derived the GAET for leading-order
4-point KK gauge scattering amplitudes (
108a
)-(
108b
)
as the outcome:

\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{5}^{cn}A_{5}^{dn}]\,.

(119)

The above analysis demonstrates that the GAET for 3-point KK scattering amplitudes
is the most fundamental formulation of the GAET, with which we can further deduce the validity of the GAET for 4-point KK scattering amplitudes. Hence, we conclude that

the validity of GAET (
119
) for 4-point KK scattering amplitudes can be reduced to
the validity of GAET (
82
) for 3-point KK scattering amplitudes

(which we have already proved in Sec.
3.1.1
). Extending this result, we would conjecture that
the GAET for
N
-point KK scattering amplitudes (
N\!\!\geqslant\!5
) can be also reduced to
the validity of the GAET for the 3-point KK scattering amplitudes.

As a consistency check, we examine the flat 5d limit by taking the warped parameter
k\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. Substituting the flat-space couplings of Table
1
(Appendix
C
) into
the above amplitudes (
113
)-(
114
),
we obtain the following leading-order amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,11\,}{2}c_{\theta}\,,\hskip-42.67912pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,3\,}{2}c_{\theta}\,,

(120a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,5\!-\!11c_{\theta}\!-\!4c_{2\theta}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})}\,,\hskip-42.67912pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,3\hskip 0.85358pt(3\!-\!c_{\theta})}{\,2\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\,}\,,

(120b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{0}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{\,5\!+\!11c_{\theta}\!-\!4c_{2\theta}}{2(1-c_{\theta})}\,,\hskip-42.67912pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{0}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\frac{\,3(3\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\,}{\,2\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\,}\,,

(120c)

which agree with the previous results for the compactified flat 5d
Yang-Mills gauge theories (under
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
)
as given in Refs.
[
16
]
[
17
]

and also Refs.
[
12
]
[
49
]
. Moreover, in this flat 5d limit,
the elastic scattering amplitude of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and
of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons differ by the same amount in each channel:

\mathcal{K}_{i}^{0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{0}\,=\hskip 0.85358pt-4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(121)

with
\,i\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}
. They have vanishing contribution to the full amplitude
due to the color Jacobi identity (
110
).

Next, we consider two inelastic scattering processes,

(n,n)\!\!\rightarrow\!\!(m,m)
with
n\!\neq\!m\!\!>\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
, and

(n,m)\!\rightarrow\!(\ell,q)
with
n\!\neq\!m\!\neq\!\ell\!\neq\!q\!>\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. For the inelastic scattering process
(n,n)\!\!\rightarrow\!\!(m,m)
,
we compute the 4-point leading-order scattering amplitudes
of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{L}^{cm}A_{L}^{dm}]\,

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\big({\mathcal{C}_{s}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{t}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{u}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big),

(122a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{5}^{cm}A_{5}^{dm}]\,

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\big({\mathcal{C}_{s}}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{t}}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{u}}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big).

(122b)

In Eqs.(
122a
)-(
122b
),
both the leading-order inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
are of
O(E^{0})
under the high energy expansion, as required by the KK GAET (
26
)
which enforces the energy cancellations of
E^{4}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!E^{0}
for the 4-point
longitudinal KK scattering amplitude
\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{cm}A_{L}^{dm}]

as shown in Eq.(
78
). The exact cancellations of
O(E^{4})
and
O(E^{2})
terms in the inelastic
longitudinal KK amplitude
\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{cm}A_{L}^{dm}]

impose the following sum-rule-type conditions:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{nnj}a_{mmj}

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{nmj}^{2}=a_{nnmm}\,,

(123a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}a_{nmj}^{2}

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(1\!+\!r^{2})\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt}$}r_{j}^{2}\Big]a_{nnj}a_{mmj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(123b)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\frac{\,(1\!-\!r^{2})^{2}\,}{r_{j}^{2}}a_{nmj}^{2}

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(1\!+\!r^{2})\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{\hskip 0.85358pt3\hskip 0.85358pt}\,}{2}$}\hskip-0.85358ptr_{j}^{2}\Big]a_{nnj}a_{mmj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(123c)

where
(r,\,r_{j})
denote mass ratios and are defined as
\hskip 0.85358ptr\!=\!M_{m}/M_{n}
and
r_{j}\!=\!M_{j}/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
. In the above, the inelastic condition (
123a
) ensures to the energy cancellations at
O(E^{4})

and is mass-independent, whereas the inelastic conditions (
123b
)-(
123c
)
guarantees the energy cancellations at
O(E^{2})
. We prove the three inelastic conditions respectively in Eqs.(
349a
), (
369
)
and Eqs.(
371
)-(
374
) of Appendix
D
.

In Eq.(
122a
), for each kinematic channel,
the 4-point leading-order scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{c_{\theta}}{\,4\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\,}\bigg\{\!2\hskip 0.85358pt(5\!-\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr^{4})a_{nn0}a_{mm0}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\Big[r_{j}^{4}\!+\!4r_{j}^{2}\!+\!2\hskip 0.85358pt(5\!-\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr^{4})\Big]a_{nnj}a_{mmj}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt-2\hskip 0.85358pt(3\!-\!2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!r^{4})\hskip 0.85358pta_{nnmm}\Big\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(124a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,8\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}(1\!+\!c_{\theta})\,}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\bigg\{2(3\!-\!c_{\theta})r_{j}^{4}\!+\!\Big[4(1\!+\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2})c_{\theta}\!-\!(3\!+\!r^{2})(5\!-\!c_{2\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\Big]r_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt+\Big[(15\!-\!6\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!+\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr^{4})\!-\!(2\!+\!32\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr^{4})c_{\theta}\!-\!(11\!+\!10\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!-\!r^{4})c_{2\theta}\!-\!2\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt+(1\!-\!r^{2})^{2}\big[(5\!-\!r^{2})\!+\!8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!(3\!+\!r^{2})c_{2\theta}\big]r_{j}^{-2}\Big\}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nmj}^{2}-\frac{1}{\,8\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\,}\Big[2(3\!-\!2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!-\!r^{4})c_{\theta}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt+4\hskip 0.85358pt(3\!-\!2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!c_{2\theta})\big]a_{nnmm}\,,

(124b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\,8\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})\,}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\bigg\{2(3\!+\!c_{\theta})r_{j}^{4}\!-\!\Big[4(1\!+\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2})c_{\theta}\!+\!(3\!+\!r^{2})(5\!-\!c_{2\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\Big]r_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt+\Big[(15\!-\!6\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!+\!3\hskip 0.85358ptr^{4})\!+\!(2\!+\!32\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr^{4})c_{\theta}\!-\!(11\!+\!10\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!-\!r^{4})c_{2\theta}\!+\!2\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt+(1\!-\!r^{2})^{2}\big[(5\!-\!r^{2})\!-\!8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!+\!(3\!+\!r^{2})c_{2\theta}\big]r_{j}^{-2}\Big\}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nmj}^{2}+\frac{1}{\,8\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\,}\Big[2(3\!-\!2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\!-\!r^{4})c_{\theta}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.95013pt-4\hskip 0.85358pt(3\!-\!2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!c_{2\theta})\big]a_{nnmm}\,,

(124c)

with the mass ratios
\hskip 0.85358ptr\!=\!M_{m}/M_{n}
and
r_{j}\!=\!M_{j}/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

For the corresponding 4-point leading-order inelastic scattering amplitude (
122b
) of KK Goldstone bosons, the sub-amplitudes in each kinematic channel are given by

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\!=-c_{\theta}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nnj}\tilde{a}_{mmj}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\!=\frac{~3\!-\!c_{\theta}~}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nmj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\!=-\frac{~3\!+\!c_{\theta}~}{1\!-\!c_{\theta}}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nmj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(125)

Then, we find that in each channel, the 4-point inelastic scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons differ by the same amount:

{\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}}\,=-\frac{c_{\theta}}{\,2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\,}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\Big[(3\!+\!r^{2})\hskip 0.85358ptr_{j}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt2(1\!-\!r^{4})\Big]a_{nnj}a_{mmj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(126)

provided that the following sum rule conditions hold:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}\tilde{a}_{mmj}\,

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\hskip-0.85358pt1\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}r_{j}^{2}\right)\!\!\left(\hskip-0.85358pt1\!-\!\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}$}\bar{r}_{j}^{2}\right)\!a_{nnj}a_{mmj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(127a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nmj}^{2}\,

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{a}_{nnj}\tilde{a}_{mmj}=\tilde{a}_{nnmm}\,,

(127b)

where we have defined the mass ratios
\,r_{j}\!=\!M_{j}/M_{n}
and
\,\bar{r}\!=\!M_{j}/M_{m}
,
and the quartic KK Goldstone coupling coefficient

\tilde{a}_{nnmm}\!=\!\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n}\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n}\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{m}\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{m}\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt
. We note that the above inelastic condition (
127a
) can be directly inferred
from Eq.(
84
) by squaring its two sides followed by summing over the KK index
j\hskip 0.85358pt
. [We recall that Eq.(
84
) is the condition to hold the GAET (
82
)
at the level of 3-point KK amplitudes.] The condition (
127b
) holds because it is just the special case of the basic relations
(
348a
) which rely on the completeness condition (
347a
).

Eq.(
126
) demonstrates that the inelastic scattering amplitudes
(
122a
) and (
122b
) are equal
due to the color Jacobi identity (
110
). This explicitly establishes the GAET for 4-point inelastic KK scattering amplitudes:

\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{L}^{cm}A_{L}^{dm}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bn}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{5}^{cm}A_{5}^{dm}]\,.

(128)

According to our discussion below Eq.(
127
), we stress that

the validity of the inelastic GAET (
128
) is guaranteed by the validity of
the most fundamental GAET (
82
) at the level of 3-point KK amplitudes.

As a consistency check, we take the flat 5d limit
k\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

and derive the following inelastic KK scattering amplitudes for each kinematic channel:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-c_{\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(r^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt-\!r^{-2})c_{\theta}\,,\hskip-42.67912pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-c_{\theta}\,,

(129a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,3\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}+(r^{2}\!-\!r^{-2})c_{\theta}\,,\hskip-42.67912pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,3\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\,,

(129b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,3\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}{\,1\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(r^{2}\!-\!r^{-2})c_{\theta}\,,\hskip-42.67912pt

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,3\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}{\,1\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}\,.

(129c)

Thus, the inelastic scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and
of the KK Goldstone bosons differ by the same amount in each kinematic channel:

{\mathcal{K}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\,=\,(r^{2}\!-\!r^{-2})\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(130)

where each individual channel is marked by the kinematic index
\,i\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}
. Hence, the difference (
130
) gives zero contribution to the inelastic KK scattering amplitudes (
122a
)-(
122b
) due to the color Jacobi identity (
110
).

Then, we study the general inelastic scattering process
(n,m)\!\!\rightarrow\!\!(\ell,q)

with all the external KK indices
(n,m,\ell,q)
unequal. Under the high energy expansion,
we compute its 4-point leading-order inelastic scattering amplitudes
of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons,
which take the following form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bm}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{L}^{c\ell}A_{L}^{dq}]\,

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\big({\mathcal{C}_{s}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{t}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{u}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big),

(131a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bm}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{5}^{c\ell}A_{5}^{dq}]\,

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\big({\mathcal{C}_{s}}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{t}}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{u}}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big).

(131b)

In each kinematic channel,
we derive the leading-order scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\,4M_{n}M_{m}M_{\ell}M_{q}\,}\bigg\{\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\Big[M_{j}^{-2}(M_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{m}^{2})(M_{\ell}^{2}\!-\!M_{q}^{2})(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})

\displaystyle-c_{\theta}M_{j}^{4}\Big]a_{nmj}a_{\ell qj}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta}\Big[M_{\ell}^{4}\!+\!M_{q}^{4}\!+\!(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2})(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})

\displaystyle+\!M_{\ell}^{2}(M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{n}^{2}\!+\!2M_{q}^{2})\Big]a_{nm\ell q}\bigg\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(132a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\,16M_{n}M_{m}M_{\ell}M_{q}\,}\bigg\{\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\Big[\!-\!4M_{j}^{4}\!+\!2(5\!+\!c_{\theta})M_{j}^{2}(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})

\displaystyle-\hskip-0.85358pt2(1\!-\!c_{\theta})M_{j}^{-2}(M_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{q}^{2})(M_{m}^{2}\!-\!M_{\ell}^{2})(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})\Big]a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j}

\displaystyle+\frac{16}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2}\!-\!M_{j}^{2})(M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!-\!M_{j}^{2})a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j}

\displaystyle-\!2\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!4M_{m}^{2}\!+\!4M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta}(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle+(M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2})^{2}\Big]a_{nm\ell q}\bigg\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(132b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\,16M_{n}M_{m}M_{\ell}M_{q}\,}\bigg\{\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\Big[4M_{j}^{4}\!-\!2(5\!-\!c_{\theta})M_{j}^{2}(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt2(1\!+\!c_{\theta})M_{j}^{-2}(M_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{\ell}^{2})(M_{m}^{2}\!-\!M_{q}^{2})(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})\hskip-0.85358pt\Big]a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj}

\displaystyle-\frac{16}{\,1\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!-\!M_{j}^{2})(M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2}\!-\!M_{j}^{2})a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj}

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt2\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2})(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!4M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!4M_{q}^{2})\!+\!c_{\theta}(M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2})^{2}\Big]a_{nm\ell q}\bigg\}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(132c)

We further compute the corresponding 4-point leading-order scattering amplitude of KK Goldstone bosons in each channel,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-c_{\theta}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nmj}\tilde{a}_{\ell qj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(133a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=\frac{~3\!-\!c_{\theta}~}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nqj}\tilde{a}_{m\ell j}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(133b)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{~3\!+\!c_{\theta}~}{1\!-\!c_{\theta}}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{n\ell j}\tilde{a}_{mqj}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(133c)

Then, we demonstrate that in each kinematic channel the above inelastic scattering amplitudes of
the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the KK Goldstone bosons differ by the same amount:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}\!-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}=\frac{~M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2}~}{4M_{n}M_{m}M_{\ell}M_{q}}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!M_{j}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(1\!+\!c_{\theta})a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j}\!-\!(1\!-\!c_{\theta})a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj}\Big],

(134)

where we have used Eq.(
83
) as the crucial condition
[which holds the GAET (
82
) for the basic 3-point KK amplitudes]. In the above, we have also imposed the following sum rule conditions:

\displaystyle a_{nm\ell q}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{nmj}a_{\ell qj}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!a_{nqj}a_{\ell mj}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(135a)

\displaystyle a_{nm\ell q}=\frac{1}{~M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2}~}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!M_{j}^{2}(a_{nmj}a_{\ell qj}\!+\!a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj}\!+\!a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(135b)

\displaystyle(M_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{m}^{2})(M_{\ell}^{2}\!-\!M_{q}^{2})\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\frac{\,a_{nmj}a_{\ell qj}\,}{M_{j}^{2}}\,=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!M_{j}^{2}(a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j}\!-\!a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(135c)

\displaystyle(M_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{\ell}^{2})(M_{m}^{2}\!-\!M_{q}^{2})\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\frac{\,a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj}\,}{M_{j}^{2}}\,=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!M_{j}^{2}(a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j}-a_{nmj}a_{\ell qj})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(135d)

\displaystyle(M_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{q}^{2})(M_{\ell}^{2}\!-\!M_{m}^{2})\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\frac{\,a_{nqj}a_{m\ell j}\,}{M_{j}^{2}}\,=\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!M_{j}^{2}(a_{nmj}a_{\ell qj}\!-\!a_{n\ell j}a_{mqj})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(135e)

The relations (
135a
) can be readily proved as in Eq.(
348a
)
which relies on the completeness condition (
347a
). The above formula (
135b
) is derived
as in Eq.(
365
) of Appendix
D
and
the last three conditions of (
135
)
are derived around Eqs.(
375
)-(
376
). With the above, we explicitly prove the GAET for the general 4-point inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
with all external KK indices being different:

\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bm}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{L}^{c\ell}A_{L}^{dq}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A_{5}^{an}A_{5}^{bm}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A_{5}^{c\ell}A_{5}^{dq}]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(136)

Here we find again that the validity of the GAET (
136
)
for the 4-point inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
is guaranteed by the validity of the fundamental 3-point-level GAET (
82
).

Taking the flat 5d space limit
k\!\!\rightarrow\!\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
, we examine the inelastic KK scattering process

(n,2n)\!\rightarrow\!(3n,4n)
with
\hskip 0.85358ptn\!>\!0
for instance. The relevant scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons
and its corresponding KK Goldstone bosons can be derived from Eqs.(
132
)-(
133
)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{32}(35\!+\!43c_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{s}=-\frac{1}{2}c_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(137a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{~451\!+\!468c_{\theta}\!+\!145c_{2\theta}~}{64(1\!+\!c_{\theta})}\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{t}=\frac{~3\!-\!c_{\theta}~}{\,2(1\!+\!c_{\theta})\,}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(137b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{u}

\displaystyle=-\frac{~31\!-\!48c_{\theta}\!+\!145c_{2\theta}~}{64(1\!-\!c_{\theta})}\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{u}=-\frac{~3\!+\!c_{\theta}~}{\,2(1\!-\!c_{\theta})\,}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(137c)

From the above, we explicitly compute the difference between
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}

which gives the same amount for all three channels:

\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{i}=\frac{5}{\,32\,}(21\!+\!29\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(138)

where the index
i\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}
denotes the three kinematic channels. We have verified that the above difference (
138
) fully agrees with the flat 5d limit
(
k\!\rightarrow\!0
) of Eq.(
134
) for the choice of external KK indices

(n,m,\ell,q)=(n,2n,3n,4n)
.

Finally, we analyze the inelastic KK scattering channel
(0,0)\!\!\rightarrow\!\!(n,n)
. Under high energy expansion, we compute the leading-order scattering amplitudes of the
KK gauge bosons and of the KK Goldstone bosons as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A^{a0}_{\pm}A^{b0}_{\mp}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{L}A^{dn}_{L}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\big({\mathcal{C}_{s}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{s}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{t}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{t}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{u}}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{u}\big),

(139a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A^{a0}_{\pm}A^{b0}_{\mp}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{5}A^{dn}_{5}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\big({\mathcal{C}_{s}}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{s}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{t}}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{t}\!+{\mathcal{C}_{u}}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{u}\big).

(139b)

In the above, the sub-amplitudes
\{\mathcal{K}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
and

\{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
for each kinematic channel are derived as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=0\,,

(140a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=-(1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{f}_{0}^{2}\,,

(140b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{f}_{0}^{2}\,,

(140c)

which establish the equality between the two leading-order inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
(
139a
) and (
139b
). Hence, we have GAET for the 4-point inelastic
KK scattering amplitudes of
(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)\hskip 0.85358pt
:

\mathcal{T}_{0}[A^{a0}_{\pm}A^{b0}_{\mp}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{L}A^{dn}_{L}]\,=\,-\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A^{a0}_{\pm}A^{b0}_{\mp}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{5}A^{dn}_{5}]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(141)

As a consistency check, we can take the flat 5d limit
k\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
and obtain:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=0\,,

(142a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=-(1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(142b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(142c)

These agree with the previous inelastic KK scattering amplitudes derived
for the compactified flat 5d space with orbifold
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

[
28
]
.

3.2.2
Warped GRET:
from 3-Point to 4-Point KK Amplitudes

In this subsection, we first prove the wraped KK gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET)
at leading order for the elastic process
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)

with all the external KK gravitons being longitudinally polarized,
namely,
\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h^{n}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]
. Then, we analyze two inelastic scattering processes
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)
and
(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)

with the external KK gravitons having helicities
\pm 1\hskip 0.85358pt
. We will prove the GRETs for these two inelastic processes respectively,
