\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!2h^{m}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2\phi_{n}\!\rightarrow\!2\phi_{m}]
and

\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}\!\rightarrow\!2h^{n}_{\pm 1}]=-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}\!\rightarrow\!2\mathcal{V}^{n}_{\pm 1}]
.

\blacklozenge
Elastic Scattering Process
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)
:

We first derive the 4-point elastic scattering amplitude of
the spin-0 gravitational KK Goldstone bosons
\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358pt\phi_{n}\phi_{n}

for the warped 5d GR under the
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
compactification. We use the Feynman rules given in Appendix
C.2
and compute explicitly
the KK Goldstone boson amplitude to the leading order of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s\hskip 0.85358pt(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\!\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{64}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}^{2}\,,

(143)

where the leading-order scattering amplitude

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]\!\equiv\!\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358pt\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

is of
O(E^{2})
. To explicitly demonstrate the GRET for 4-point KK scattering amplitudes,
we compare the leading-order scattering amplitude
(
143
) of the gravitational KK Goldstone bosons with
the corresponding longitudinal KK graviton amplitude

\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h_{L}^{n}]\!\equiv\!\mathcal{M}_{0}[h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358pth_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}]

[
55
]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h_{L}^{n}]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s\hskip 0.85358pt(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\!\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{2304}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\big(\hat{r}_{j}^{6}\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\!+\!11\big)\alpha_{nnj}^{2}\,,

(144)

where the KK mass ratio
\hat{r}_{j}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{j}/\mathbb{M}_{n}
. To hold the KK GRET (
75
) requires the equality between the two leading-order
scattering amplitudes (
144
) and (
143
),

\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h_{L}^{n}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]\,,

(145)

which gives the following nontrivial sum-rule condition:

\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\big(\hat{r}_{j}^{6}\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\!+\!11\big)\alpha_{nnj}^{2}=36\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}^{2}\,.

(146)

To prove the above condition (
146
) for the 4-point GRET (
145
), we make use of the important
fundamental 3-point GRET condition (
100
) to replace the coupling

\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}
by
\alpha_{nnj}
and convert Eq.(
146
)
to the following form:

\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\big(8\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{6}\!-\!40\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{4}\!+\!59\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\!-\!36\big)\alpha_{nnj}^{2}=\hskip 0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(147)

Then, the rest of the proof is to justify the condition (
147
)
which can be achieved by using the properties of the 5d wavefunctions and the resultant relations. To prove the condition (
147
),
we first derive the following sum rule identities:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\alpha_{nnj}^{2}

\displaystyle={\alpha_{nnnn}}\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad

(148a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nnj}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\,4\,}{3}\alpha_{nnnn}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(148b)

where the quartic KK coupling coefficient
\alpha_{nnnn}
is defined
in Eq.(
342a
) of Appendix
D
. The identities (
148a
) and (
148b
) are proved in
Eqs.(
349b
) and (
368
) respectively. Combining the identities (
148a
) and (
148b
), we eliminate the
quartic KK coupling coefficient
\alpha_{nnnn}
and obtain:

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\!1\!-\!\frac{3}{\,4\,}\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\!\right)\!\alpha_{nnj}^{2}=0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(149)

Using Eq.(
149
),
we can further simplify the identity (
147
) as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\!\hat{r}_{j}^{6}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt5\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{4}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!\frac{\,16\,}{3}\!\right)\!\alpha_{nnj}^{2}=0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(150)

which is proved in Eq.(
382
) of Appendix
D

by using the equations of motion (
289
)-(
290
). This completes the proof of the identity (
147
) and thus establishes the GRET
(
145
) for the 4-point gravitational KK scattering amplitudes.

\blacklozenge
Inelastic Scattering Process
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)
:

In this part, we compute the inelastic amplitudes for the longitudinal KK graviton scattering
process
h^{n}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{m}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{m}_{L}

and for corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone boson scattering

\phi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{m}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{m}
,
with which we explicitly prove the corresponding GRET.

Using the Feynman rules given in Appendix
C.2
, we compute explicitly
the KK gravitational Goldstone amplitude to the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{m}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{m}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\,\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts_{0}\,}{\,128\sin^{2}\!\theta\,}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left[128\tilde{\beta}_{nmj}^{2}\!-\!(29\!-\!28c_{2\theta}\!-\!c_{4\theta})\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}\tilde{\beta}_{mmj}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}\,}{\,64\sin^{2}\!\theta\,}\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,.

(151)

Then, we compute the corresponding KK graviton scattering amplitude at the leading order
of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{n}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{m}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{m}_{L}]=\frac{\kappa^{2}s_{0}}{~6912s_{\theta}^{2}r^{4}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})~}\bigg\{\!\!-\!\Big\{513(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{8})\!+\!577\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})+429\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4}

\displaystyle+\!2\!\left[87(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{8})\!+\!118\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})\!+\!96\hat{r}^{4}\right]\!c_{2\theta}\!+\!15\!\left[\hskip-0.85358pt1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{8}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt3\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})\right]\!c_{4\theta}\Big\}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}

\displaystyle-\!\frac{1}{~3456s_{\theta}^{2}r^{4}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})~}\Big\{223(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{8})\!+\!7\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\!-\!131\hat{r}^{4}\!+\!2\!\left[31(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{8})\!-\!14\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})\!-\!56\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4}\right]\!c_{2\theta}

\displaystyle+\!3\!\left[\hskip-0.85358pt1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{8}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt7\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})\!+\!17\hat{r}^{4}\right]\!c_{4\theta}\Big\}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}\!+\!\frac{\,(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})(7\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})^{2}\,}{576s_{\theta}^{2}\hat{r}^{4}}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{4}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}

\displaystyle+\!\frac{\,(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt3c_{2\theta})\,}{3456\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4}}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{8}\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!-\!\frac{\,(37\!+\hskip-0.85358pt11c_{2\theta})\,}{1728\hskip 0.85358pts_{\theta}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4}}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\hat{r}_{j}^{8}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}\bigg\},

(152)

where we have defined the KK mass ratios
\hat{r}=\mathbb{M}_{m}/\mathbb{M}_{n}
and
\hat{r}_{j}=\mathbb{M}_{j}/\mathbb{M}_{n}
.

To prove GRET for the inelastic scattering amplitudes
(
152
) and (
151
),
we use the fundamental 3-point GRET condition (
99
)
to convert the trilinear KK coupling
\tilde{\beta}_{nmj}
to
\alpha_{nmj}

as follows:

\left[(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}_{j}^{2})^{2}\!+\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}\right]\!\alpha_{nmj}=6\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{nmj}\,.

(153)

Then, we derive the following sum rules which ensure the energy cancellations in the
longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitude
\mathcal{M}[h^{n}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{m}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{m}_{L}]

from
O(E^{10})
to
O(E^{2})
under high energy expansion. These sum rules include

\displaystyle\alpha_{nnmm}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\alpha_{nmj}^{2}\,,

(154)

for the
O(E^{10})
to
O(E^{8})
cancellations,

\displaystyle 2(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\alpha_{nnmm}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}(\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!+\!2\alpha_{nmj}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(155)

for the
O(E^{8})
to
O(E^{6})
cancellations,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{4}(\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!-\!\alpha_{nmj}^{2})=\left[2(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\!+\!\frac{(1\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})^{2}}{\,2(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\,}\right]\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}(\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!-\!\alpha_{nmj}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(156)

for the
O(E^{6})
to
O(E^{4})
cancellations, and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{6}\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}

\displaystyle=\frac{\hskip 0.85358pt5\hskip 0.85358pt}{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\!\sum_{j=1}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{4}\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\alpha_{nnmm}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt-6\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!-\!4\hat{r}^{2}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}\,,

(157a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{6}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\hskip 0.85358pt5\hskip 0.85358pt}{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})\!\sum_{j=1}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{4}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}\!+\!\frac{1}{2}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt6\hat{r}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})\alpha_{nnmm}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt-(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{4})\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nnj}\alpha_{mmj}\!-\!2(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\hat{r}^{2})^{2}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!\hat{r}_{j}^{2}\alpha_{nmj}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(157b)

for the
O(E^{4})
to
O(E^{2})
cancellations. We prove these sum rule relations in Appendices
D
and
E
,
as given by Eqs.(
349b
), (
370
), (
388c
) and (
390
). With these, we can establish the GRET for the inelastic scattering channel as follows:

\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{n}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{n}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!h^{m}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pth^{m}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}\!\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{m}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{m}]\,.

(158)

\blacklozenge
Inelastic Scattering Process
(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)
:

Next, we consider a mixed inelastic KK graviton scattering process

h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\rightarrow\!h^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-1}_{n}

and its corresponding KK Goldstone boson scattering channel

h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}
,
where the initial state includes two massless zero-mode gravitons with helicities
\pm 2\hskip 0.85358pt
, and
both the final state KK gravitons and KK vector Goldstones have helicities
\pm 1\hskip 0.85358pt
. By explicit calculation, we derive the following KK scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}[h^{+2}_{0}h^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!h^{+1}_{n}h^{-1}_{n}]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}(1\!+\!c_{\theta})(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{3}(12M_{n}^{2}\!+\!s_{0})s_{0}~}{16(4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}^{2}M_{n}^{2}\!+\!s_{\theta}^{2}s_{0})}\alpha_{000}\alpha_{nn0}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(159a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\rightarrow\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}(1\!+\!c_{\theta})(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{3}(3s_{0}\!+\!2M_{n}^{2})(s_{0}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})~}{48(4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}^{2}M_{n}^{2}\!+\!s_{\theta}^{2}s_{0})}\alpha_{000}\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(159b)

Making high energy expansion, we derive the leading-order KK scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}h^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!h^{+1}_{n}h^{-1}_{n}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}s_{0}\,}{\,16\,}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\alpha_{000}\alpha_{nn0}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(160a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}h^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]

\displaystyle=+\frac{\,\kappa^{2}s_{0}\,}{\,16\,}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\alpha_{000}\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(160b)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{000}\!=\!\alpha_{nn0}\!=\!\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\!=\!\mathsf{u}_{0}

and
\mathsf{u}_{0}
is given by Eq.(
295a
). We see that the above KK graviton scattering amplitude equals
the corresponding KK Goldstone scattering amplitude at the leading order
after including an overall factor

(-\text{i})^{2}\!=\!-1
from the (tree-level) modification factor (
58a
)
on its right-hand side,

\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}h^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!h^{+1}_{n}h^{-1}_{n}]=-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}h^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(161)

This agrees with the GRET (
59
).

In summary, we stress that in the proof of the condition (
146
)
for the 4-point GRET (
145
), the basic 3-point
GRET condition (
100
) plays a key role. Hence, we conclude that our proof of the most basic 3-point GRET identity (
98
)
or its equivalent sum rule condition (
100
) does automatically guarantee the validity
of the 4-point GRET identity (
145
) or its equivalent sum rule condition
(
146
). We stress that
a major goal of the present work is to demonstrate that the validities of the GAET and GRET
for the 4-point KK scattering amplitudes can be proved from the corresponding GAET and GRET
identities (sum rule conditions) at the level of the 3-point KK scattering amplitudes.

Finally, it is natural to conjecture that the validity of general
N
-point (
N\!\!\geqslant\!4
) GAET and GRET
can be all reduced to that of the 3-point GAET and GRET. This is because all the 4-point gauge (gravity) couplings can be reduced to
the summed products of 3-point gauge (gravity) couplings as shown in Eq.(
348
)
(due to the completeness conditions of 5d wavefunctions),
and there is no more independent new gauge (gravity) couplings for
N\!\!\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
.
8
8

8

It is also known that all the
N
-point (
N\!\!\geqslant\!4
) nonlinear gravitational self-couplings
are reducible and can be induced precisely as having 3-point trilinear graviton couplings
[
59
]
.
Hence, we would expect that for the general
N
-point (
N\!\!\geqslant\!4
) KK amplitudes the GAET and GRET
cannot impose extra new conditions on the 3-point KK couplings (to avoid over-constraint and inconsistency),
so that the validities of the
N
-point GAET and GRET should be reduced to that of the 3-point GAET and GRET. We note that a rigorous general proof on this reduction is more nontrivial,
especially because each massive scattering amplitude of longitudinal KK gauge bosons (KK gravitons)
involves large energy cancellations. This interesting task is beyond the current scope
and we would leave it to our future work.

4
Extended Double-Copy for Warped KK
Equivalence Theorems

In this section, we study the double-copy construction of massive gravitational KK scattering amplitudes
at tree level from the corresponding massive KK gauge scattering amplitudes
under the warped 5d compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. This is nontrivial and challenging
because the warped 5d compactification leads to highly nonlinear KK mass-spectrum
and the scattering amplitude contains in each kinematic channel the sum over
infinite number of KK-pole-exchanges, which is in contrast to the flat 5d case. It was proved
[
28
]

that the double-copy construction for
N
-point (
N\!\!\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt\!4
) massive gauge/gravity
KK scattering amplitudes can directly work out
only for toroidal compactification with flat extra dimensions,
which has linear mass-spectrum proportional to KK index and
exhibits single-pole exchange in each kinematic channel. Because the KK gauge/gravity theories under warped 5d compactification do not satisfy these conditions,
they cannot hold double-copy for
N
-point (
N\!\!\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt\!4
) KK amplitudes
exactly or at the sub-leading orders. Nevertheless, we will demonstrate in Section
4.1

that the double-copy can be realized for 3-point (
N\!\!=\!3
)
KK gauge/gravity scatttering amplitudes without high energy expansion. Then, in Section
4.2
we construct the double-copy
for 4-point KK gauge/gravity scatttering amplitudes
at the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion
which is the key to derive the GRET from GAET. We further study in Section
4.3
the extension to

N
-point (
\!N\!\!\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
) KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes
at the LO and their LO double-copy construction. Finally, we give a prescription for realizing the warped double-copy
at the LO of high energy expansion.

4.1
Double-Copy Construction of 3-Point Amplitudes and Warped GRET

We have computed the most general 3-point on-shell scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons in Eq.(
79
),
which takes the following form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\{\epsilon_{j}\}]

\displaystyle=g\hskip 0.85358ptf^{abc}\mathcal{N}[\{\epsilon_{j}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(162a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}[\{\epsilon_{j}\}]

\displaystyle=-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\left[(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})\right]\hskip-0.85358pt,

(162b)

where
\{\epsilon_{j}\}\!=\!\{\epsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu}\}
denotes the polarization vectors for the three external
KK gauge bosons
\{A^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn_{j}}_{\lambda_{j}}\}
with
j\!=\!1,2,3
. In parallel, we can reexpress the most relevant 3-point scattering amplitudes (
81
) of KK Goldstone bosons
with gauge bosons:

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[A_{5}^{an_{1}}\!A_{5}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{\pm}^{cn_{3}}]=gf^{abc}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}[\epsilon_{3}]\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}[\epsilon_{3}]=-\text{i}\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\hskip 0.85358pt{\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(163)

where the cubic KK Goldstone-gauge coupling coefficient
\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!

is given by Eq.(
334
) of Appendix
C.1
.

The conventional BCJ double-copy method
[
25
]
[
26
]
applies to
N
-point scattering amplitudes
of massless gauge bosons and gravitons with
N\!\!\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
. For the case of
N\!\!=\!3\hskip 0.85358pt
, the massless double-copy can be directly realized
after imposing on-shell conditions on the 3-point gauge/gravity amplitudes. For the present study, we first extend it to the 3-point (
N\!=\!3
) massive KK scattering amplitudes of
the warped gauge/gravity theories. This is much more nontrivial as the mass spectra of the KK gauge bosons and
KK gravitons also differ from each other. Since each on-shell 3-point gauge amplitude (
162
) or (
163
)
only has a single color factor and numerator without internal propagator,
we may formulate the 3-point CK correspondence by using the following double-copy relations
and the gauge/gravity coupling correspondence as follows:

f^{abc}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{N}[\{\epsilon_{j}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 17.07164ptf^{abc}{\longrightarrow\,}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}[\epsilon_{3}]\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 17.07164ptg{\,\longrightarrow\,-\frac{\,\kappa\,}{4}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(164)

Moreover, at each KK level-
n
, we set up the KK-mass correspondence (replacement),

M_{n}\!\rightarrow\!\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
which means to replace each mass-eigenvalue
M_{n}
of KK gauge bosons
[determined by Eq.(
275
)] by mass-eigenvalue
\mathbb{M}_{n}
of KK gravitons
[determined by Eq.(
294
)].

With the above, we make the following double-copy construction of
the on-shell 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitude from the product of
the corresponding on-shell 3-point KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes (
162
):

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}[\{e_{j},\hskip 0.85358pt\epsilon_{j}\}]=-\frac{\,\kappa\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\,}{~4\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}\,}\frac{1}{8}\Big(\mathcal{N}[\{e_{j}\}]\!\times\!\mathcal{N}[\{\epsilon_{j}\}]+\{e_{j}\!\leftrightarrow\!\epsilon_{j}\}\Big),

(165)

where

\{e_{j}\}\!=\!\{e_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu}\}
denotes another set of polarization vectors in parallel to the polarization vecors

\{\epsilon_{j}\}\!=\!\{\epsilon_{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}}^{\mu}\!\}
,
and the overall factor
1\hskip-0.85358pt/8\!=\!1\hskip-0.85358pt/2^{3}
arises from symmetrizing the two
polarization vectors associated with each external KK graviton polarization. The two polarization vectors
\{e_{j},\hskip 0.85358pt\epsilon_{j}\}
should compose the
polarization tensor
\varepsilon_{j}^{\mu\nu}\!=\!\varepsilon_{\hat{\lambda}_{j}}^{\mu\nu}
of the external KK graviton-
j\hskip 0.85358pt
according to Eq.(
262
). Also, because the graviton polarization tensor is traceless
\varepsilon_{\hat{\lambda}_{j}}^{\mu\nu}\eta_{\mu\nu}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
,
we have the corresponding products vanish:

\hskip 0.85358pte_{j}^{\pm}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{j}^{\pm}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~\hskip 0.85358pte_{j}^{\pm}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{j}^{L}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~e_{j}^{\pm}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{j}^{\mp}+e_{j}^{L}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{j}^{L}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(166)

This helps to simplify our explicit results of double-copy. Using the polarization tensors Eq.(
262
) for the three external KK graviton states, we can express the
double-copy formula of the 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitude as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[h_{n_{1}}^{\hat{\lambda}_{1}}h_{n_{2}}^{\hat{\lambda}_{2}}h_{n_{3}}^{\hat{\lambda}_{3}}\big]=\sum_{\lambda_{j},\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\!\!\hskip-0.85358pt\Big(\!\prod_{j}\!\hskip-0.85358ptC_{\hskip-0.85358pt\lambda_{j}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}^{\hat{\lambda}_{j}}\Big)\mathcal{M}\big[\{e_{i}\}^{\lambda_{j}}\!,\{\epsilon_{i}\}^{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt=-\frac{~\kappa\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\,}{~4\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}\,}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{j},\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\!\!\Big(\!\prod_{j}\!\hskip-0.85358ptC_{\hskip-0.85358pt\lambda_{j}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}^{\hat{\lambda}_{j}}\Big)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[\{\hskip-0.85358pte_{\lambda_{j}}\!\}]\hskip-0.85358pt\!\times\!\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[\{\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{\hskip-0.85358pt\lambda_{j}^{\prime}}\!\}]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt\equiv\frac{~\kappa\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\,}{~4~}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{j},\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\!\!\Big(\!\prod_{j}\!\hskip-0.85358ptC_{\hskip-0.85358pt\lambda_{j}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}^{\hat{\lambda}_{j}}\Big)\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 3.74997pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}^{\mathrm{dc}}\,,

(167)

where the helicity index

\,\hat{\lambda}_{j}\!=\!\{\pm 2,\pm 1,0\}\!=\!\{\pm 2,\pm 1,L\}\,

labels the 5 helicity states of each external massive KK graviton. The polarization tensors of the external KK graviton states are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}_{\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\lambda}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}}=\sum_{\lambda_{j},\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\!\!C_{\hskip-0.85358pt\lambda_{j}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\lambda}_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}}\hskip 0.85358pt\epsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu}\hskip 0.85358pt\epsilon_{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}^{\nu}\,,

(168a)

\displaystyle C_{\pm 1,\pm 1}^{\pm 2}\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt,\quad C_{\pm 1,0}^{\pm 1}\!=C_{0,\pm 1}^{\pm 1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt,\quad C_{\pm 1,\mp 1}^{\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!=\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{6\,}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt,\quad C_{0,0}^{\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!=\!\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\hskip 0.85358pt}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(168b)

Then, we use the double-copy method to compute the on-shell 3-point scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons and derive the following formula:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 3.74997pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}^{\mathrm{dc}}=(e_{1}\epsilon_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}p_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})+(e_{1}e_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})+(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3})

\displaystyle+(e_{1}\epsilon_{2})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}p_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3})+(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})+(e_{1}e_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})

\displaystyle+(e_{1}\epsilon_{2})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})-2(e_{1}\epsilon_{2})(e_{2}\epsilon_{1})(e_{3}p_{2})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})-2(e_{1}e_{2})(e_{3}p_{2})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle+(e_{1}e_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{1})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})+(e_{1}e_{2})(e_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})-(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{3})(e_{3}p_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle-(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{1})(e_{3}p_{2})(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})-(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{1})(e_{3}\epsilon_{2})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})-(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}e_{3})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle+(e_{1}\epsilon_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})-(e_{1}\epsilon_{2})(e_{2}\epsilon_{3})(e_{3}p_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})-(e_{1}e_{2})(e_{3}p_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})

\displaystyle-(e_{1}\epsilon_{2})(e_{2}e_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})-(e_{1}e_{2})(e_{3}\epsilon_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{2}\epsilon_{3})-2(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})

\displaystyle-2(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}e_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})+(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})+(e_{1}p_{3})(e_{2}e_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3})

\displaystyle+(e_{1}e_{2})(e_{3}p_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3})+(e_{1}\epsilon_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{1})(e_{3}p_{2})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})-2(e_{1}\epsilon_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(e_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle-\!2(e_{1}e_{3})(e_{2}p_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{3})\!+\!(e_{1}\epsilon_{3})(e_{2}e_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})\!+\!(e_{1}e_{3})(e_{2}\epsilon_{3})(p_{3}\epsilon_{1})(p_{3}\epsilon_{2})\,,

(169)

where we have used the shorthand notation
(ab)\!\equiv\!(a\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptb)
, and
we also make the replacement
M_{n}\!\rightarrow\hskip-0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
for each KK mass.

We compare the above double-copy formula (
169
) of the general 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitude
with the explicit calculation result of the same KK graviton scattering amplitude (
346
) in
Appendix
C.2
. We find that they fully agree with each other after
imposing the correspondence (
164
).

With the above, we then analyze the 3-point on-shell scattering amplitude of KK gravitons
at the leading order of high energy expansion
and establish the double-copy construction of the KK gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET)
from the KK gauge-theory equivalence theorem (GAET)
at the level of the most fundamental 3-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes. We consider a 3-point KK graviton scattering amplitude
containing two external KK gravitons with helicities
\pm 1

and one external KK gravition with helicities
\pm 2
,
or, including two external longitudinal polarization states (with helicity-0)
plus one transverse polarization state (with helicities
\pm 2
). Using the KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes (
162
) and (
167
),
we can construct two types of 3-point KK graviton amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion. One type of the KK graviton scattering amplitudes have helicities
(\pm 1,\hskip 0.85358pt\pm 1,\hskip 0.85358pt\pm 2)
,
and we derive the following:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]={-\frac{~c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa\,}{~4}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Big(\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[A^{\pm}_{n_{1}}A^{\pm}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\,\mathcal{N}[A^{L}_{n_{1}}A^{L}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]+\!\mathcal{N}[A^{\pm}_{n_{1}}A^{L}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\mathcal{N}[A^{L}_{n_{1}}A^{\pm}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]+O(E^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(170a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]={-\frac{~c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa\,}{~4}}\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{\pm}_{n_{1}}A^{\pm}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{L}_{n_{1}}A^{L}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]

\displaystyle={c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}}\!\!\frac{\,\kappa\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\!\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!-\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2})\,}{\,2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23}\,}\!\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{1}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262ptp_{1})^{2}\!+\!(\epsilon_{2}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262ptp_{1})\!+\!(\epsilon_{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262ptp_{1})\hskip-0.85358pt\Big],

(170b)

where
c_{3}
is a normalization factor of couplings and will determined shortly. As before we denote the mass ratio
\hat{r}_{ij}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{i}}/\mathbb{M}_{n_{j}}
. We note that on the right-hand side of Eq.(
170a
)
the second amplitude product has only subleading contributions
of
O(E^{0})
, instead of the leading-order contributions of
O(E^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Another type of KK graviton scattering amplitudes have helicities
(L,\hskip 0.85358ptL,\hskip 0.85358pt\pm 2)
,
where
L
stands for longitudinal polarization with helicity-0. We construct its exact tree-level
scattering amplitude
\mathcal{M}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}h^{L}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

and derive its leading-order amplitude

\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}h^{L}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]
as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}h^{L}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle={-\frac{~c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa\,}{~4}}\frac{1}{3}\Big\{2(\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{L}_{n_{1}}\!A^{L}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}])^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{+}_{n_{1}}\!A^{-}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{-}_{n_{1}}\!A^{+}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{+}_{n_{1}}\!A^{+}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{-}_{n_{1}}\!A^{-}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]+2\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{+}_{n_{1}}\!A^{L}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{-}_{n_{1}}\!A^{L}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\Big\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(171a)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}_{0}}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}h^{L}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle={-\frac{~c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa\,}{~12}}\!\left\{\hskip-0.85358pt2(\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{L}_{n_{1}}\!A^{L}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}])^{2}\!+\!\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{+}_{n_{1}}\!A^{-}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{-}_{n_{1}}\!A^{+}_{n_{2}}\!A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\right\}

\displaystyle={c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}}\frac{\,\kappa\hskip 0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-1.42262ptp_{1})^{2}\,}{6}\!\!\left[2+\!\left(\!\!\frac{\,1\!-\!\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!-\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2}\,}{\hat{r}_{13}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23}}\!\!\right)^{\!\!\!2}\,\right]\!,

(171b)

where the KK mass ratios are defined as

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}\!/\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}
and

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt/\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}
. We note that under the high energy expansion and on the righ-hand side of Eq.(
171a
),
the first two terms in the braces give leading-order contributions and the last two terms are
subleading.

Then, for the 3-point leading-order KK gravitational amplitudes (
92b
) and (
97b
),
we can construct them by double-copy of the corresponding two 3-point KK gauge boson amplitudes:

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle={-\frac{~c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa\,}{~4}}\mathcal{N}_{0}[A^{\pm}_{n_{1}}A^{\pm}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}[A^{5}_{n_{1}}A^{5}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}]

\displaystyle={\kappa\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3}\hskip 0.85358pta_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}}\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})^{2}\!+\!(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{3})(\epsilon_{1}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 96.73918pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{2}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1})\Big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(172a)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[\phi_{n_{1}}\phi_{n_{2}}h_{n_{3}}^{\pm 2}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle={-\frac{~c_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa\,}{~4}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}[A^{5}_{n_{1}}A^{5}_{n_{2}}A^{\pm}_{n_{3}}])^{2}}={\kappa\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}}\left(\epsilon_{3}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{1}\right)^{2}.

(172b)

By comparing the gravitational KK scattering amplitudes (
170
)-(
172
)
(obtained via double-copy construction)
with the same type of amplitudes (
92a
)-(
92b
)
and (
97a
)-(
97b
)
(obtained via explicit Feynman-diagram approach),
we find that the two sets of KK scattering amplitudes share exactly
the same kinematic structure
and
can equal to each other so long as we require that
the KK gauge boson mass be replaced by the corresponding KK graviton mass at each KK level and
the KK gauge coupling factor are replaced by the corresponding KK gravitational coupling factor
[cf. Eq.(
164
)] together with the following replacements of the trilinear coupling factors:

\displaystyle c_{3}\,a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}\!\!\rightarrow\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},\quad c_{3}\,a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\!\rightarrow\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},\quad c_{3}\,\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}\!\!\rightarrow\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}.

(173)

In general, such replacements are nontrivial because the coupling raitos
(such as the ratio
\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\hskip-0.85358pt/a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}
) do not equal one. This means that for the double-copy of KK gauge/gravity theories with warped 5d space, the coupling
replacements (
173
) are necessary and nontrivial. We note that in the flat 5d limit of
\,k\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
, these ratios take simple values (cf. Table
1
). For instance, choosing
\hskip 0.85358pt(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)\,
with
n\!>\!0
,
we have

c_{3}=\frac{\,\alpha_{nnj}\,}{a^{2}_{nnj}}=\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{nnj}}{\,a_{nnj}\tilde{a}_{nnj}}=\frac{\,\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}\,}{\tilde{a}^{2}_{nnj}\,}=\left\{\!\!\begin{array}[]{cl}1\hskip 0.85358pt,&\quad j\!=\!0\,,\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\quad j\!=\!2\hskip 0.85358ptn\,,\end{array}\right.

(174)

whereas for the case of
\hskip 0.85358pt(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptm,\hskip 0.85358pt|n\hskip-0.85358pt\pm\hskip-0.85358ptm|)\,
with
n\!\neq\!m

in the flat 5d limit, we have

c_{3}=\frac{\,a^{2}_{nm|n\pm m|}\,}{\,\alpha_{nm|n\pm m|}\,}=\frac{\,a_{nm|n\pm m|}\tilde{a}_{nm|n\pm m|}\,}{\tilde{\alpha}_{nm|n\pm m|}}=\frac{\,\tilde{a}^{2}_{nm|n\pm m|}\,}{\,\tilde{\beta}_{nm|n\pm m|}\,}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\,.

(175)

In fact, as we have checked, for the flat 5d space under the
S^{1}
toroidal compactification
without orbifold
[
28
]
, all the above coupling ratios will become
c_{3}\!=\!1
.

Finally, using the coupling replacements (
173
),
we compare the leading-order KK graviton amplitudes
(
170b
) and (
171b
)
with the corresponding gravitational KK Goldstone boson amplitudes
(
172a
) and (
172b
) respectively. We find that they share exactly the same kinematic structures and obey the GRET,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[h^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}h^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle=-{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{1}}\mathcal{V}^{\pm 1}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(176a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}_{0}}[h^{L}_{n_{1}}h^{L}_{n_{2}}h^{\pm 2}_{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt]

\displaystyle={\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}}[\phi_{n_{1}}\phi_{n_{2}}h_{n_{3}}^{\pm 2}\hskip 0.85358pt]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(176b)

provided the following conditions hold respectively,

\displaystyle(\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!+\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2}\!-\!1)\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}

\displaystyle\!=\hskip 0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\hat{r}_{23}\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},

(177a)

\displaystyle\left[\hskip-0.85358pt(1\!-\!\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\!-\!\hat{r}_{23}^{2})^{2}\!+\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\hat{r}_{23}^{2}\right]\!\hskip-0.85358pt\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}

\displaystyle\!=\hskip 0.85358pt6\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}^{2}\hat{r}_{23}^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},

(177b)

where the mass ratios

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{13}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{1}}\!/\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}
and

\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{r}_{23}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{n_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt/\mathbb{M}_{n_{3}}
. Comparing the above conditions (
177a
)-(
177b
)
with the conditions (
94
) and (
99
)
(which we obtained by explicit Feynman diagram approach), we find that the
identities (
177a
) and (
94
) are identical, and
the same is true for the two identities (
177b
) versus (
99
). This establishes our successful double-copy constructions of the GRET (
176a
) and
(
176b
) (in warped KK gravity theory) from the GAET
(
82
) (in the warped KK gauge theory).

In summary, we have established the double-copy construction from the GAET to GRET
for the 3-point leading-order (LO) KK scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(3)}[h^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{~\scalebox{.9}{\text{GRET}}~}}~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(3)}[\phi_{n}]

(178)

\displaystyle{\left\uparrow\vbox to14.22636pt{}\right.\kern-1.2pt}\scalebox{.9}{$\text{DC}_{3}$}

\displaystyle\scalebox{.9}{$\text{DC}_{3}$}{\left\uparrow\vbox to14.22636pt{}\right.\kern-1.2pt}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(3)}[A^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{~\scalebox{.9}{\text{GAET}}~}}~\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{(3)}[A^{n}_{5}]

In Eq.(
178
), along the horizontal directions,
the lower (upper) equality presents the GAET (GRET) of 3-point scattering amplitudes
of KK gauge bosons (KK gravitons). Along the direction of a given vertical arrow, each correspondence is established
by the double-copy (DC
3
) under the extended massive color-kinematics dualities (
164
) and (
173
). The left vertical arrow represents the double-copy (DC
3
)
construction of the 3-point LO KK graviton
amplitude
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(3)}[h^{n}_{L}]
from the corresponding LO KK gauge boson amplitude
\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(3)}[A^{n}_{L}]
. The right vertical arrow represents the double-copy (DC
3
)
construction of the 3-point LO gravitational
KK Goldstone boson amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(3)}[\phi_{n}]
from the corresponding
(gauge theory) LO KK Goldstone boson amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{(3)}[A^{n}_{5}]
. Finally, this picture also holds if we replace the two amplitudes

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(3)}[h^{n}_{L}]
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(3)}[\phi_{n}]
in the top row by

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(3)}[h^{n}_{\pm 1}]
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(3)}[\mathcal{V}_{\pm 1}^{n}]
respectively.

4.2
Double-Copy Construction of 4-Point Amplitudes and Warped GRET

In this subsection, we study double-copy construction for the 4-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes
and for their corresponding 4-point KK Goldstone boson scattering amplitudes
at the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion. As we will demonstrate, under high energy expansion, the leading-order KK amplitudes no longer contain
summation of infinite number of KK mass-poles. By construction they can exhibit an effective single massless-pole in each channel
and the associated numerators obey the kinematic Jacobi identity, which enable the realization of double-copy
at the leading order. In the subsections
4.2.1
and
4.2.2
, we study the double-copy constructions for
the elastic and inelastic scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons respectively.

4.2.1
Construction of 4-Point Elastic KK Graviton Amplitudes

At the leading order of high energy expansion, we can reexpress the 4-point scattering amplitudes
(
108a
)-(
108b
)
of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and of the KK Goldstone bosons as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[4A^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(179a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[4A^{n}_{5}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(179b)

where the Mandelstam variables
(s_{0},t_{0},u_{0})
are defined in Eq.(
261
)
of Appendix
A
and obey the condition

s_{0}\!+\!t_{0}\!+\!u_{0}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. In the above, the kinematic numerators
\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0},\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0},\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0}\big)

and
\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0}\big)
are connected to the
subamplitudes of Eqs.(
108a
)-(
108b
) and Eqs.(
113
)-(
114
)
via the folllowing relations:

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0},\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0},\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{s}^{0},\,t_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{t}^{0},\,u_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{u}^{0}\big),

(180a)

\displaystyle\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{0},\,t_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{0},\,u_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{0}\big),

(180b)

Using the definitions (
180
) and the difference relation (
117
), we derive the generalized gauge transformations between the numerators of elastic scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of the KK Goldstone bosons:

\mathcal{N}^{0}_{j}\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}+s_{0j}\Theta\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\Theta=\!-2\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\!r_{i}^{2}a_{nni}^{2}\,,

(181)

where
s_{0j}\!\in\!\{s_{0},\hskip 0.85358ptt_{0},\hskip 0.85358ptu_{0}\}
denotes the massless Mandelstam variables. Inspecting the leading-order numerators (
180a
) and (
180b
)
for elastic scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of KK Goldstone bosons,
we find that they satisfy the kinematic Jacobi identities:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}+\Theta\sum_{j}\!s_{0j}=0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(182a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(182b)

where the index
j\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}
. We can readily prove the above second identity (
182b
) by using
the formulas (
180b
) and (
114
),
whereas the first identity (
182a
) holds because of
s_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptt_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptu_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt=0\hskip 0.85358pt
.

We extend the conventional color-kinematics duality
for massless gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes
[
25
]
[
26
]

to the massive scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons
and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons in Eq.(
179
). We formulate the massive color-kinematics duality by using the following double-copy relations:

\mathcal{C}_{j}{\,\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219pt\mathcal{C}_{j}{\,\longrightarrow\,}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219ptg^{2}{\,\longrightarrow\,}-\!(\kappa/4)^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(183)

Thus, we construct the corresponding scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gravitons
and of the gravitational KK Goldstone bosons, to the leading-order contributions of
\mathcal{O}(E^{2})
under the high energy expansion. At each KK level-
n
, we also set up the KK-mass correspondence (replacement)
from KK gauge bosons to KK gravitons,
M_{n}\!\rightarrow\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Next, we make double-copy construction of the 4-point scattering amplitudes of (helicity-zero) longitudinal KK gravitons and
of the KK gravitational Goldstone bosons from the corresponding KK gauge/Goldstone boson scattering amplitudes. Applying the double-copy correspondence (
183
) to the 4-point scattering amplitudes
(
179a
)-(
179b
) of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons
and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons at the leading order of high energy expansion,
we derive the following 4-point scattering amplitudes of
the longitudinal KK gravitons and of the KK gravitational Goldstone bosons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4h_{L}^{n}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,c_{4}\kappa^{2}\,}{\,16\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\Bigg],

(184a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,c_{4}\kappa^{2}\,}{\,16\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\Bigg],

(184b)

where the normalization factor
c_{4}
will be determined by taking the flat 5d limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
). We already established
[
28
]
the exact double-copy formula
for the flat 5d space under orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
which leads to
\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4}\!=\!2/3\hskip 0.85358pt

[
28
]
.

Applying the generalized gauge transformation (
181
) to
the numerators of the longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitude
(
184a
) and making use of the Jacobi identity (
182b
),
we can directly prove the equivalence between the leading-order scattering
amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gravitons and of the gravitational KK Goldstone bosons:

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4h_{L}^{n}]\,=\,\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4\phi_{n}]\,.

(185)

This is a proof of the KK gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET) from the
KK gauge-theory equivalence theorem (GAET) by using the double-copy approach
at the level of 4-point scattering amplitudes.

Based on the GRET relation (
185
), we can compute its either side to obtain the same
4-point leading-order gravitational KK scattering amplitude. Since the KK Goldstone numerators

\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\}
[as in Eqs.(
180b
)(
114
)] are much simpler than
the KK graviton numerators
\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\}
[as in Eqs.(
180a
)(
113
)],
we can use the KK Goldstone numerators
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\}
to explicitly compute the leading-order
amplitudes (
184
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4h_{L}^{n}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4\phi_{n}]=-\frac{\,c_{4}\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt}{\,16\,\hskip 0.85358pt}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\!\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{~c_{4}\kappa^{2}s_{0}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{64}\bigg(\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}\!\bigg)^{\!\!2}

\displaystyle=\frac{~c_{4}\kappa^{2}s_{0}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{64}\tilde{a}^{2}_{nnnn}\,,

(186)

where in the last step we have made use of the sum rule (
349c
)
for converting the trilinear Goldstone coupling (
\tilde{a}_{nnj}^{2}
)
to the quartic Goldstone coupling (
\tilde{a}_{nnnn}
). Then, using the explicit Feynman diagram calculations shown in
Eqs.(
143
)-(
144
),
we have the following 4-point elastic scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gravitons and
of KK gravitational Goldstone bosons at the leading order of high energy expansion, which are equal to
each other due to the GRET (
145
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h_{L}^{n}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s_{0}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{2304}\!\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\!r_{j}^{2}\big(r_{j}^{6}\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358ptr_{j}^{2}\!+\!11\big)\alpha_{nnj}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s_{0}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\!\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{64}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s_{0}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\!\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{64}\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\,,

(187)

where the mass ratio
\hskip 0.85358ptr_{j}\!=\!\mathbb{M}_{j}/\mathbb{M}_{n}
, and in the last step above we have used
the sum rule (
349d
) with the trilinear and quartic gravitational Goldstone couplings

\tilde{\beta}_{nnj}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}

defined respectively in Eqs.(
340c
) and (
342b
).

With the above, we compare the double-copied scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons (KK Goldstone bosons)
in Eq.(
186
) with the corresponding KK graviton
(KK Goldstone boson) amplitudes by explicit Feynman diagram calculations in Eq.(
187
). They have exactly the same kinematic structure except the difference
between the two types of quartic coupling coefficients:

\frac{~\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4h_{L}^{n}]~}{\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h_{L}^{n}]\,}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4\phi_{n}]~}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~c_{4}\tilde{a}_{nnnn}^{2}\,}{~\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\,}\,.

(188)

As a consistency check, we take the flat 5d space limit
k\!\rightarrow\!0

and find that the above ratio of KK amplitudes approaches one as expected:

\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4h_{L}^{n}]~}{\mathcal{M}_{0}[4h_{L}^{n}]\,}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4\phi_{n}]~}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[4\phi_{n}]}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~c_{4}\tilde{a}_{nnnn}^{2}\,}{~\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\,}=\frac{~2/3\!\times\hskip-0.85358pt\!(3\hskip-0.85358pt/2)^{2}~}{3\hskip-0.85358pt/2}=\hskip-0.85358pt1\hskip 0.85358pt,

(189)

where the flat-space coupling coefficients are given by

\tilde{a}_{nnnn}\!=\!\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\!=\!3/2\hskip 0.85358pt
,
as summarized in Table
1
.

Hence, according to Eqs.(
188
)-(
189
), we complete our
double-copy construction of the 4-point leading-order KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes
by further imposing the following replacements,

c_{4}\tilde{a}_{nnnn}^{2}{\,\longrightarrow\,}\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\,,

(190)

in addition to the operations in Eq.(
183
). With these, we can reexpress the leading-order double-copied gravitational KK amplitudes
(
186
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4h_{L}^{n}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[4\phi_{n}]=-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\,}{\,16\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\alpha}_{nnnn}^{2}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\!\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s_{0}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{64}\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\,,

(191)

which agrees with Eq.(
187
).

4.2.2
Construction of 4-Point Inelastic KK Graviton Amplitudes

In this subsection, we study double-copy construction of the leading-order gravitational KK scattering amplitudes
for inelastic processes. This includes the inelastic channels
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)
(with
n\!\neq\!m
),

(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)
, and
(n,m)\!\rightarrow\!(\ell,q)
(with unequal KK indices of
n,m,\ell,q
).

\blacklozenge
Inelastic Scattering Process
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)\hskip 0.85358pt
:

We first consider the inelastic scattering process
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)
with
\hskip 0.85358ptn\!\neq\!m\hskip 0.85358pt
and

n,m\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
. Making high energy expansion, we can reexpress the leading-order inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
(
122a
)-(
122b
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[2A^{n}_{L}2A_{L}^{m}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(192a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[2A^{n}_{5}2A_{5}^{m}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(192b)

where the leading-order kinematic numerators

\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)

and
\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)
are given by

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,t_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,u_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big),

(193a)

\displaystyle\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,t_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,u_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big).

(193b)

Using Eq.(
193
) and the difference relation
(
126
), we derive the generalized gauge transformations between
the kinematic numerators of inelastic scattering amplitudes, analogous to Eq.(
117
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{j}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}+s_{0j}\Theta_{\mathrm{in}}\,,

(194a)

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{c_{\theta}}{\,2\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2}\,}\!\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\!\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(3\!+\!r^{2})\hskip 0.85358ptr_{i}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt2(1\!-\!r^{4})\Big]a_{nni}\hskip 0.85358pta_{mmi}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(194b)

where the mass ratios
\hskip 0.85358ptr\!=\!M_{m}/M_{n}
and
r_{i}\!=\!M_{i}/M_{n}
. Then, we sum up the leading-order kinematic numerators (
193a
) and (
193b
)
respectively and deduce the following:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+\!(s_{0}\!+\!t_{0}\!+\!u_{0})\Theta_{\mathrm{in}}\!=0\,,

(195a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pts_{0}c_{\theta}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{a}_{nmi}^{2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nni}\tilde{a}_{mmi})=0\,,

(195b)

where the index
\hskip 0.85358ptj\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}
, and we have used the kinematic identity
s_{0}\!+\!t_{0}\!+\!u_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt

and the sum rule condition (
349c
). Eqs.(
195a
) and (
195b
)
demonstrate that the two sets of numerators
\{\mathcal{N}_{j}\}
and
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}\}
satisfy
the kinematic Jacobi identities respectively.

Thus, with the massive CK duality based on the kinematic Jacobi identities
(
195a
)-(
195b
),
we formulate the following double-copy correspondence for
the inelastic scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons and
of the KK Goldstone bosons in Eq.(
192
):

\mathcal{C}_{j}{\,\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 19.91692pt\mathcal{C}_{j}{\,\longrightarrow\,}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 19.91692ptg^{2}{\,\longrightarrow\,}-(\kappa/4)^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(196)

Applying the double-copy correspondence (
196
)
to the inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
(
192a
)-(
192b
) of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons
and of the KK Goldstone bosons at the leading order of high energy expansion,
we derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h_{L}^{n}2h_{L}^{m}]=-\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}\,}{\,16\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\Bigg],

(197a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]=-\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}\,}{\,16\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\Bigg],

(197b)

where the normalization factor
c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}
can be determined
by taking the flat 5d limit
k\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. For the flat 5d compactification with orbifold
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
, this factor is given by

\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

[
27
]
[
28
]
.

Since the kinematic numerators of KK Goldstone bosons
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
are much simpler than that of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons
\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
, with the GRET relation (
185
) we can use the kinematic numerators of KK Goldstone bosons to explicitly compute the leading-order KK scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{n}_{L}2h^{m}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]=-\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt}{\,16\,\hskip 0.85358pt}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\!\Bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}s_{0}~}{\,32\,}\!\left[2c_{\theta}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt-\!\frac{\,(3\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\!-\!\frac{\,(3\!+\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,}{\,1\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}\!\right]\!\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}~}{64\sin^{2}\!\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}\,,

(198)

where in the second line we have utilized the sum rule (
349c
)
to convert the trilinear coupling into the quartic coupling. In the above, the normalization factor
c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}

may be determined by taking the flat 5d space limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
)
which gives
c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\!=\!1

[
27
]
[
28
]
. Moreover, using the explicit Feynman diagram approach, we derive the 4-point
inelastic scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gravitons and
of KK gravitational Goldstone bosons at the leading order of high energy expansion. They are equal to each other due to the GRET (
158
) and we present them as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h^{n}_{L}2h^{m}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}\,}{\,64\sin^{2}\!\theta\,}\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,,

(199)

where the sum rule (
349d
) is used in the last step.

With these, we compare the double-copied amplitudes of KK gravitons (KK Goldstone bosons)
in Eq.(
198
) with the corresponding KK graviton (KK Goldstone bosons) amplitudes
by explicit Feynman diagram calculations in Eq.(
199
). We find that they have exactly the same kinematic structure except the difference
between the two types of quartic coupling coefficients:

\frac{~\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h_{L}^{n}2h_{L}^{m}]~}{\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h_{L}^{n}2h_{L}^{m}]\,}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]~}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}\,}{~\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,}\,.

(200)

Taking the flat 5d space limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
),
the above ratio of KK amplitudes approaches one as expected:

\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h_{L}^{n}2h_{L}^{m}]~}{\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h_{L}^{n}2h_{L}^{m}]\,}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]~}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}\,}{~\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,}=\frac{~1\!\times\!1^{2}\,}{1}=1\hskip 0.85358pt,

(201)

where values of the flat 5d coupling coefficients

\tilde{a}_{nnmm}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}
are given in Table
1

of Appendix
C
.

Accordingly, based on Eqs.(
200
)-(
201
),
we impose the following correspondence for the
double-copy construction of leading-order inelastic KK gauge/gravity amplitudes,

c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}{\,\longrightarrow\hskip 0.85358pt\,}\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,,

(202)

alongside the operations in Eq.(
196
). With these, we can reexpress the double-copied leading-order gravitational KK amplitudes
(
198
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h_{L}^{n}2h_{L}^{m}]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2\phi_{n}2\phi_{m}]=-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,}{\,16\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\!\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}~}{64\sin^{2}\!\theta}\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\,,

(203)

which agrees with Eq.(
199
).

\blacklozenge
Inelastic Scattering Process
(n,m)\!\rightarrow\!(\ell,q)\hskip 0.85358pt
:

Consider the general inelastic scattering of the longitudinal KK gauge bosons
A^{n}_{L}A^{m}_{L}\!\rightarrow\!A^{\ell}_{L}A^{q}_{L}

and of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons
A^{n}_{5}A^{m}_{5}\!\rightarrow\!A^{\ell}_{5}A^{q}_{5}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Under high energy expansion, we can reexpress the leading-order inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
(
131a
) and (
131b
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A^{n}_{L}A_{L}^{m}A_{L}^{\ell}A_{L}^{q}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(204a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A^{n}_{L}A_{L}^{m}A_{L}^{\ell}A_{L}^{q}]

\displaystyle\,=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)\!,

(204b)

where the kinematic numerators

\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)

and
\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0},\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)
are given by

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,t_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,u_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big),

(205a)

\displaystyle\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(s_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,t_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip-0.85358pt,\,u_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\big).

(205b)

Then, we derive the generalized gauge transformations which connect the numerators of KK gauge bosons
to that of the KK Goldstone bosons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}+s_{0j}\Theta_{\mathrm{in}}\,,

(206a)

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=\frac{~M_{n}^{2}\!+\!M_{m}^{2}\!+\!M_{\ell}^{2}\!+\!M_{q}^{2}~}{4M_{n}M_{m}M_{\ell}M_{q}}\!\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\!M_{i}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(1\!+\!c_{\theta})a_{nqi}a_{m\ell i}\!-\!(1\!-\!c_{\theta})a_{n\ell i}a_{mqi}\Big].

(206b)

With these, we compute the sum of each set of the leading-order kinematic numerators

\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
and
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,s_{0}\,}{2}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left[2c_{\theta}\tilde{a}_{nmj}\tilde{a}_{\ell qj}\!+\!(3\!-\!c_{\theta})\tilde{a}_{nqj}\tilde{a}_{m\ell j}\!-\!(3\!+\!c_{\theta})\tilde{a}_{n\ell j}\tilde{a}_{mqj}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{s_{0}}{2}\left[2c_{\theta}+(3-c_{\theta})\!-\!(3\!+\!c_{\theta})\right]\tilde{a}_{nm\ell q}=0\,,

(207a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+\!(s_{0}\!+\!t_{0}\!+\!u_{0})\Theta_{\mathrm{in}}\!=0\,.

(207b)

This proves the kinematic Jacobi identities for the leading-order inelastic KK scattering amplitudes:

\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}=0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(208)

Next, using the extended massive double-copy we construct the inelastic scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons and of gravitational KK Goldstone bosons at the leading order of
high energy expansion, which also obey the GRET:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[h^{n}_{L}h^{m}_{L}h^{\ell}_{L}h^{q}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[\phi_{n}\phi_{m}\phi_{\ell}\phi_{q}]=-\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}\,}{\,16\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Bigg[\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{s_{0}}\!+\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{t_{0}}\!+\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0})^{2}\,}{u_{0}}\!\Bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}s_{0}~}{\,32\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\left[2c_{\theta}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt-\!\frac{\,(3\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,}{\,1\!+\!c_{\theta}\,}\!-\!\frac{\,(3\!+\!c_{\theta})^{2}\,}{\,1\!-\!c_{\theta}\,}\!\right]\!\tilde{a}_{nm\ell q}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}~}{64\sin^{2}\!\theta}\tilde{a}_{nm\ell q}^{2}=\frac{~\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}~}{64\sin^{2}\!\theta}\tilde{\beta}_{nm\ell q}\,,

(209)

where in the second equality the completeness relation (
348b
) is applied
and in the last equality we have used the conversion relation (
212
)
which is established at the end of this paragraph. In the above, the normalization factor
c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}

may be determined by taking the flat 5d limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
)
which gives
c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\!=\!2\hskip 0.85358pt

[
27
]
[
28
]
. On the other hand, using the explicit Feynman diagram approach, we compute the inelastic scattering amplitudes
of gravitational KK Goldstone bosons (KK gravitons) at the leading order of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{n}_{L}h^{m}_{L}h^{\ell}_{L}h^{q}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[\phi_{n}\phi_{m}\phi_{\ell}\phi_{q}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}s_{\theta}}{32}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\!\left[\!(15\!+\!c_{2\theta})\tilde{\beta}_{nmj}\tilde{\beta}_{\ell qj}\!-\!\frac{\,4(5\!+\!2c_{\theta}\!+\!c_{2\theta})\,}{1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta}}\tilde{\beta}_{nqj}\tilde{\beta}_{m\ell j}\!-\!\frac{\,4(8\!+\!7c_{\theta}\!+\!c_{3\theta})\,}{1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta}}\tilde{\beta}_{n\ell j}\tilde{\beta}_{mqj}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}s_{0}~}{64\sin^{2}\!\theta}\tilde{\beta}_{nm\ell q}\,.

(210)

As a consistency check, we take the limit of flat 5d space (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
)
and find that the ratio of KK scattering amplitudes approaches one:

\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\tilde{a}_{nm\ell q}^{2}\,}{~\tilde{\beta}_{nm\ell q}\,}=\frac{~2\!\times\!(1\hskip-0.85358pt/2)^{2}~}{1\hskip-0.85358pt/2}=1\hskip 0.85358pt,

(211)

where the values of couplings
\tilde{a}_{nm\ell q}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{nm\ell q}

in the flat space limit are summarized in
Table
1
.
Hence, we set up the following double-copy correspondence for
the above leading-order inelastic KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes,

c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nm\ell q}^{2}{\,\hskip 0.85358pt\longrightarrow~}\tilde{\beta}_{nm\ell q}\,,

(212)

together with Eq.(
196
).

\blacklozenge
Inelastic Scattering Process
(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)\hskip 0.85358pt
:

Finally, we analyze the mixed 4-point inelastic scattering amplitudes of
\hskip 0.85358pt(0,\hskip 0.85358pt0)\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn)\hskip 0.85358pt
. According to Eq.(
140
), we verify that the kinematic Jacobi identities for the numerators

\{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
and
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\}
hold. Applying the massive double-copy correspondence (
196
), we construct the
double-copied gravitational KK scattering amplitudes at the leading order:

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2h^{n}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2\phi_{n}]=\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}}{32}s_{0}(1\!-\!c_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pta_{0nn}^{4}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(213)

where
a_{0nn}\!\!=\!\mathsf{f}_{0}
is given by Eq.(
341e
). On the other hand, using the explicit Feynman diagram approach and the GRET identity (
145
),
we compute the 4-point inelastic gravitational KK scattering amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion:

\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2h^{n}_{L}]=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2\phi_{n}]=\frac{\,\kappa^{2}}{\,32\,}s_{0}(1\!-\!c_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{0nn}^{2}\,,

(214)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{0nn}\!\!=\!\mathsf{u}_{0}
is given by Eq.(
341b
). We see that the two types of KK scattering amplitudes (
213
) and (
214
)
exhibit the same kinematic structure, and thus the mixed double-copy construction (
213
) is
successful. As a consistency check, we take the flat 5d limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
) and find that
the ratio of the two KK amplitudes approaches unit as expected:

\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2h^{n}_{L}]~}{\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2h^{n}_{L}]\,}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2\phi_{n}]~}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2\phi_{n}]}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{0nn}^{4}\,}{~\alpha_{0nn}^{2}\,}=\frac{1\times 1^{4}}{1^{2}}=1\hskip 0.85358pt,

(215)

where the values of the coupling coefficients
a_{0nn}
and
\alpha_{0nn}
in the flat 5d limit
are summarized in Table
1
. Hence, for the mixed inelastic KK scattering process of
\hskip 0.85358pt(0,\hskip 0.85358pt0)\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn)\hskip 0.85358pt
,
we set up the following double-copy correspondence for the leading-order inelastic KK gauge/gravity
scattering amplitudes,

c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{0nn}^{4}{\,\hskip 0.85358pt\longrightarrow~}\alpha_{0nn}^{2}\,,

(216)

together with Eq.(
196
).

For a further demonstration, we study the double-copy constructions of the gravitational
KK scattering amplitudes for another type of mixed processes

h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!h^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-1}_{n}
and

h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}
. For this purpose, we compute the following 4-point mixed amplitudes
for the scattering of two zero-mode gauge bosons into two final-state KK gauge bosons
(with helicities
\pm 1
or
0
) or into two final-state KK Goldstone bosons at the leading order
of high energy expansion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A^{a0}_{+}A^{b0}_{-}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{+}A^{dn}_{-}]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{~{\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\,}{s_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{~{\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}~}{t_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{~{\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}~}{u_{0}}\!\right)

\displaystyle=-g^{2}s_{0}\!\left[\!\frac{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{t}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358ptt_{0}}-\frac{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{u}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358ptu_{0}}\!\right]\!a_{000}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}\,,

(217a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A^{a0}_{+}A^{b0}_{-}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{L}A^{dn}_{L}]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)

\displaystyle=g^{2}s_{0}\!\left[\!-\frac{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{t}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358ptt_{0}}+\frac{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{u}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358ptu_{0}}\!\right]\!a_{000}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}\,,

(217b)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}[A^{a0}_{+}A^{b0}_{-}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!A^{cn}_{5}A^{dn}_{5}]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)

\displaystyle=g^{2}s_{0}\!\left[\!\frac{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{t}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358ptt_{0}}-\frac{\,(1\!-\!c_{\theta}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{u}\,}{2\hskip 0.85358ptu_{0}}\!\right]\!a_{000}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{nn0}\,,

(217c)

where the coupling coefficients

a_{000}\!=\!a_{nn0}\!=\!\tilde{a}_{nn0}\!=\!\mathsf{f}_{0}

according to Eq.(
341
). We see that the above leading-order KK scattering amplitudes
(
217b
) and (
217c
) obey
the GAET (
26
) as expected.

With the mixed leading-order gauge boson scattering amplitudes (
217
),
we construct the double-copied graviton scattering amplitude and the corresponding
scattering amplitude with gravitational KK Goldstone bosons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!h^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-1}_{n}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}\,}{16}\!\left(\!\!\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{s_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{t_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{\,\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}s_{0}~}{16}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}a_{000}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}^{2}\,,

(218a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{dc}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}\,}{16}\!\left(\!\!\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{s}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}{\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\,}{s_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}{\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\,}{t_{0}}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{u}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}{\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\prime\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}}\,}{u_{0}}\!\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\kappa^{2}s_{0}~}{16}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}a_{000}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}\tilde{a}_{nn0}\,,

(218b)

where the coupling coefficients

a_{000}\!=\!a_{nn0}\!=\!\tilde{a}_{nn0}\!=\!\mathsf{f}_{0}

according to Eq.(
341
). From the above, we can verify the equality between the two scattering amplitudes
(
218a
) and (
218b
):

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{dc}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!h^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-1}_{n}]\,=\,-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{dc}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]\,.

(219)
