Then, we explicitly compute the above leading-order amplitudes by using the Feynman diagram approach:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!h^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-1}_{n}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}s_{0}~}{16}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\alpha_{000}\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{nn0}\,,

(220a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}s_{0}~}{16}(1\!-\!c_{\theta})^{2}\alpha_{000}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\,,

(220b)

where the coupling coefficients are given by

\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha_{000}\!=\!\alpha_{nn0}\!=\!{\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}}\!=\!\mathsf{u}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt

according to Eq.(
341
). This also establishes the equivalence between the leading-order scattering amplitudes
(
220a
) and (
220b
):

\mathcal{M}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!h^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-1}_{n}]\,=\,-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[h^{+2}_{0}\hskip 0.85358pth^{-2}_{0}\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\mathcal{V}^{+1}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{n}]\,,

(221)

in full agreement with the double-copied GRET (
219
). As a consistency check, we take the flat 5d limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
) and find that
the ratio of the two gravitational amplitudes (
218a
) and (
220a
)
[or, (
218b
) and (
220b
)]
approaches one as expected:

\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2h^{n}_{\pm 1}]~}{\mathcal{M}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2h^{n}_{\pm 1}]\,}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2\mathcal{V}^{n}_{\pm 1}]~}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}[2h^{0}_{\pm 2}2\mathcal{V}^{n}_{\pm 1}]}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\lim_{k\rightarrow 0}\!\frac{~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{000}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}\tilde{a}_{nn0}\,}{~\alpha_{000}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\,}=1\hskip 0.85358pt,

(222)

where the values of all coupling coefficients in the flat 5d limit
are summarized in Table
1
. Comparing the leading-order scattering amplitudes
(
218a
)-(
218b
)
and (
220a
)-(
220b
),
we see that they share exactly the same kinematic structure and can be equal under the following
correspondence:

\displaystyle c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{000}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}^{2}

\displaystyle{\,\,\longrightarrow~}\alpha_{000}\hskip 0.85358pt{\alpha}_{nn0}\,,

(223a)

\displaystyle c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{000}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pta_{nn0}\tilde{a}_{nn0}

\displaystyle{\,\,\longrightarrow~}\alpha_{000}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\,.

(223b)

In summary, we have established the double-copy construction from the GAET to the GRET
for the 4-point leading-order (LO) KK scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(4)}[h^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{~\scalebox{.9}{\text{GRET}}~}}~\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(4)}[\phi_{n}]

(224)

\displaystyle{\left\uparrow\vbox to14.22636pt{}\right.\kern-1.2pt}\scalebox{.9}{$\text{DC}_{4}$}

\displaystyle\scalebox{.9}{$\text{DC}_{4}$}{\left\uparrow\vbox to14.22636pt{}\right.\kern-1.2pt}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(4)}[A^{n}_{L}]

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{~\scalebox{.9}{\text{GAET}}~}}~\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{(4)}[A^{n}_{5}]

In parallel to Eq.(
178
), we discuss the meaning of
the above Eq.(
224
). Along the horizontal directions,
the lower (upper) equality presents the GAET (GRET) at the level of 4-point KK scattering amplitudes. On the other hand, along the direction of each vertical arrow, the correspondence
is established by the double-copy (DC) under the extended massive color-kinematics dualities
and the KK gauge/gravity coupling correspondences
for both the elastic scattering [as shown in Eqs.(
183
)(
190
)]
and the inelastic scattering [as shown in Eqs.(
196
)(
202
)(
212
)(
216
)(
223
)]. The left vertical arrow represents the double-copy (DC
4
)
construction of the 4-point LO KK graviton
amplitude
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(4)}[h^{n}_{L}]
from the corresponding LO KK gauge boson amplitude
\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(4)}[A^{n}_{L}]
. The right vertical arrow represents the double-copy (DC
4
)
construction of the 4-point LO gravitational
KK Goldstone boson amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(4)}[\phi_{n}]
from the corresponding
(gauge theory) LO KK Goldstone boson amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{(4)}[A^{n}_{5}]
. Finally, this picture also holds if we replace the two KK scattering amplitudes

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(4)}[h^{n}_{L}]
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(4)}[\phi_{n}]
in the top row by
the other two KK scattering amplitudes

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(4)}[h^{n}_{\pm 1}]
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(4)}[\mathcal{V}_{\pm 1}^{n}]
respectively.

4.3
Extension of Warped LO Double-Copy with

N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt

So far, we have explicitly demonstrated that the warped double-copy can be realized
for 3-point and 4-point KK scattering amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion. In this subsection, we discuss the extension of the warped LO double-copy to the

N
-point KK gauge/gravity amplitudes with
N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
. Using the GAET [cf. Eq.(
24
)] and GRETs [cf. Eqs.(
57
) and (
71
)], we find that it is more convenient to analyze the
LO KK Goldstone boson amplitudes.

For a given
S
-matrix element
\mathbb{S}
with
N
external states in (3+1)d spacetime,
its mass-dimension is determined by the spacetime dimension
d\!=\!4
and the number of
external states
N

[
58
]
[
16
]
:

D_{\mathbb{S}}\,=\,4-N\hskip 0.85358pt.

(225)

Then, we first consider the pure KK Goldstone amplitude having even number of external states,
with
N\hskip-0.85358pt\!\!\geqslant\!4
and
N\!\!\in\!\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}\,
. For the KK gauge theory, the leading energy-power dependence of the
N
-point KK Goldstone boson

(A^{an}_{5})
amplitude
\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}\,
is given in the second equation of Eq.(
78a
)
with
\bar{V}_{3}^{\min}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!N_{v}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the mass-dimension of the amplitude
\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}\,
and
its leading energy-power dependence are equal:

D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}(N\!A_{5}^{an})=D_{E}(N\!A_{5}^{an})=\hskip 0.85358pt4\!-\!N\hskip 0.85358pt.

(226)

Hence, Eq.(
226
) indicates that we can express the LO scattering amplitudes of
KK Goldstone bosons as follows:

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\big[A^{a_{1}n_{1}}_{5},\cdots\!,A^{a_{N}n_{N}}_{5}\big]\,=\,\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\hskip 0.85358ptg^{N-2}\!\sum_{i=1}^{(2N-5)!!}\!\!\!\frac{~\mathcal{C}_{i}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{0}\,}{\mathcal{D}_{i}}\,,

(227)

where
\mathcal{D}_{i}
denotes the products of denominators of propagators. The effective
N
-point coupling coefficients

\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}

is dimensionless because the 4d gauge coupling is dimensionless,
[g]\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. Moreover, by taking the Del Duca-Dixon-Maltoni (DDM) color decomposition
[
60
]
,
we can express scattering amplitude
(
227
) in terms of Kleiss-Kuijf basis
[
61
]
,
which corresponds to diagrams with the half-ladder (multi-peripheral) structure
shown in Fig.
2
and contributes the leading energy-dependence. Thus, we can readily write down the
N
-point KK coupling coefficient

\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{N-3}}\hskip-11.38109pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{\sigma(2)}j_{1}}\tilde{a}_{j_{2}n_{\sigma(3)}j_{1}}{\tilde{a}_{j_{2}n_{\sigma(4)}j_{3}}}\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{j_{N-4}n_{\sigma(N-2)}j_{N-3}}\tilde{a}_{n_{\sigma(N-1)}n_{N}j_{N-3}}

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{f}_{n_{1}}\!\cdots\tilde{f}_{n_{N}}\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\hskip 0.85358pt,

(228)

where the completeness relation (
347
)
has been imposed, and the brackets
\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt
are defined in
Eq.(
364
) of Appendix
D
. In the above, the external states 1 and
N
are fixed, and

\sigma\!=\!\{\sigma(2),\cdots\!,\sigma(N\!-\!1)\}\!\in\!S_{N-2}

represents a permutation of the remaining
(N\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!2)
external states. Every color-ordered amplitude shares the same KK coupling coefficient,
because
\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptn_{N}}\!
is just the integration of 5d wavefunctions
\{\tilde{f}_{n_{i}}\hskip-0.85358pt\}
associated with each external state
\{A^{a_{i}n_{i}}_{5}\hskip-0.85358pt\}

and thus the KK indices of
\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\!

are symmetric and independent of the color ordering. We also note that the LO KK Goldstone numerators
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{0}\}
in Eq.(
227
)
should still obey the kinematic Jacobi identities for
N
-point amplitude since they
are fully determined by kinematics (similar to the flat 5d case)
and the effect of the warped 5d compactification only
affects the KK coupling coefficients
\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}
.

Figure 2
:
N
-point half-ladder (multi-peripheral) diagrams for the scalar KK Goldstone boson scattering at tree level
(
N\!\in\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}\,)
which contribute the leading energy-dependence
in the compactified 5d gauge theory. Here the external states 1 and
N
are fixed, and

\sigma\!=\!\{\sigma(2),\cdots\!,\sigma(N\!-\!1)\}\!\in\!S_{N-2}
represents
a permutation of the remaining
(N\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\!2)
external states.

As for the KK gravity theory, we first consider the scattering amplitudes of the gravitational
KK scalar Goldstone bosons (
\phi_{n}
). Based on the extended CK duality and using Eq.(
227
), we can construct
the
N
-point LO amplitudes of gravitational KK scalar Goldstone bosons
via double-copy (DC) as follows:

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}\big[\phi_{n_{1}}\!\cdots\phi_{n_{N}}\big]=-\,c_{N}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\!\cdots n_{N}}^{2}\!\!\left(\frac{\kappa}{4}\right)^{\!\!N-2}\sum_{i=1}^{(2N-5)!!}\!\!\frac{~(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{0})^{2}\,}{\mathcal{D}_{i}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(229)

Then, we can derive the leading energy-power dependence of the KK gravitational Goldstone amplitude
(
229
):

D_{E}(N\!A_{5}^{an})~\xrightarrow{~\mathrm{DC}~}~D_{E}(N\!\phi_{n})=2(4\!-\!N)+2(N\!-\!3)=2\,.

(230)

This confirms our power counting rule Eq.(
76a
) at tree level
(
L\!=\!0
). Moreover, based on the double-copy correspondence, we make the following replacement:

c_{N}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}^{2}\,\longrightarrow\,~\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}.

(231)

Thus, the
N
-point KK scalar gravitational Goldstone scattering amplitude can be expressed as follows:

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[\phi_{n_{1}}\ldots\phi_{n_{N}}\big]=-\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left(\frac{\kappa}{4}\right)^{\!\!N-2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{(2N-5)!!}\!\!\frac{\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\ell}^{0})^{2}\,}{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}\,,

(232)

where the KK Goldstone coupling coefficient
\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\!

is dimensionless. To understand the dimensionlessness of

\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\hskip-0.85358pt
, we compute the difference between
the mass-dimension of the KK Goldstone amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}(N\!\phi_{n})
and its leading energy-dependence
via Eqs.(
225
) and (
230
), which yields:

\Delta D(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0})=D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}(N\!\phi_{n})\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptD_{E}(N\!\phi_{n})=2\hskip-0.85358pt-\!N=[\kappa^{N-2}]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(233)

Eq.(
233
) shows that the residual mass-dimension of the LO amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}

fully comes from the gravitational coupling constant
\kappa

which has mass-dimension
[\kappa]\!=\!-1
. The LO KK gravitational Goldstone amplitude (
232
) is mass-independent
(resembling a “massless” amplitude) and is contributed by the trilinear vertices of

\phi_{n_{1}}\!
-
\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n_{2}}\!
-
\hskip 0.85358pth^{\mu\nu}_{n_{3}}

type (containing two partial derivatives). Hence, we deduce that the KK coupling coefficient
\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\!

is dimensionless and takes the following form:

\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}=\sum_{j_{1},\cdots,j_{N-3}=0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}j_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\beta}_{n_{N-1}n_{N}j_{N-3}}\!=\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{w}_{n_{1}}\cdots\mathsf{w}_{n_{N}}\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt.

(234)

This shows that the coupling coefficient
\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}
only depends on the
5d wavefunction
\{\mathsf{w}_{n_{i}}\}
associated with the external states
\{\phi_{n_{i}}\}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Then, we consider the case for odd number of external states
with
N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
and
N\!\in\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}+\hskip-0.85358pt1
,
the mass-dimension of
N
-point KK Goldstone amplitude in the KK gauge theory
and its leading energy-power dependence are given by

D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}(N\!A_{5}^{n})=4\!-\!N,\qquad D_{E}(N\!A_{5}^{n})=3\!-\!N.

(235)

Thus, the difference
D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptD_{E}\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

for the odd number
N
. Actually, this difference is due to the fact that the
N
-point KK Goldstone amplitude (with odd
N
)
must contain a non-derivative cubic vertex
A_{5}^{an_{1}}
-
A_{\mu}^{bn_{2}}
-
A_{\nu}^{cn_{3}}

in Eq.(
333c
). Hence, its KK coupling coefficient has mass-dimension

\,[\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}]\!=\![(M_{n_{2}}^{2}\!\!-\!M_{n_{3}}^{2})/M_{n_{1}}]\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
,
which can make up the full mass-dimension
of the KK Goldstone amplitude,
D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}\!=\!4\!-\!N\hskip 0.85358pt
. This means thar for the
N
-point KK scattering amplitude (with odd
N
),
the leading KK Goldstone amplitude contains one power of KK mass
and has its leading energy dependence

D_{\!E}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt3\hskip 0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptN
, which is lower than the leading energy dependence of
the mass-independent KK Goldstone amplitude with even number
N
. For such massive amplitudes with odd number
N
, there is no systematic way to realize
the BCJ-type double-copy for the warped KK gauge/gravity theories.

Next, we consider the double-copy construction for the
N
-point scattering amplitudes of
KK vector Goldstone bosons (
\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\mu}
). The dimensional analysis including the counting
of leading energy dependence is similar to the aforementioned conclusion
on the
N
-point amplitudes of the KK scalar Goldstone bosons (
\phi_{n}
). For the
N
-point KK scattering amplitudes (with even number
N
),
we obtain the following scattering amplitude:

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[\mathcal{V}_{n_{1}}^{\pm 1},\cdots\!,\mathcal{V}_{n_{N}}^{\pm 1}\big]

\displaystyle=-\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}\!\!\left(\!\frac{\kappa}{4}\right)^{\!\!N-2}\!\sum_{\ell=1}^{\,(2N-5)!!}\!\!\!\!\frac{1}{\,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}\,}\sum_{j=0}^{N}\sum_{i=0}^{\binom{N}{j}}\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\ell,i}^{0}[(N\!\!-\!j)A_{5}^{an};jA_{\pm}^{an}]

\displaystyle\quad~\times\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\ell,i}^{0}[\hskip 0.85358pt(N\!\!-\!j)A_{\pm}^{an};jA_{5}^{an}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(236)

where for every possible product of two numerators in the sums
we have replaced the relevant product of KK gauge coupling coefficients
by the corresponding common gravitational KK coupling coefficient:

c_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\mathsf{f}_{n_{k}}\!\!\!\cdots\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n_{k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\hskip-0.85358pt\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n_{k}}\!\!\!\cdots\mathsf{f}_{n_{k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\,\longrightarrow~\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdots\hskip 1.42262ptn_{N}}\,,

(237)

where the
N
-point KK gauge coupling coefficient
\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\mathsf{f}_{n_{k}}\!\!\!\cdots\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n_{k^{\prime}}}\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt

contains
(N\!-\!j)
wavefunctions associated with
A_{5}^{an}

and
j
wavefunctions associated with
A_{\pm}^{an}
,
whereas the
N
-point KK gauge coupling coefficient
\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n_{k}}\!\!\!\cdots\mathsf{f}_{n_{k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt

includes
j
wavefunctions associated with
A_{5}^{an}
and
(N\!-\!j)

wavefunctions associated with
A_{\pm}^{an}\hskip 0.85358pt
. The bracket notation
\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt
for defining the KK coupling coefficient
as an integral of 5d wavefunctions over the 5th coordinate
is given in Eq.(
364
) of Appendix
D
. In Eq.(
237
),
c_{N}
is a normalization factor and can be determined
by taking the flat 5d limit (
k\!\rightarrow\!0
). The effective
N
-point coupling coefficient

\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptn_{N}}\!
is dimensionless and can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptn_{N}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{N-3}=0}^{\infty}\hskip-11.38109pt\tilde{\rho}_{n_{1}n_{2}j_{1}}\tilde{\rho}_{j_{2}n_{3}j_{1}}\!\tilde{\alpha}_{j_{2}n_{4}j_{3}}\!\cdots{\tilde{\alpha}_{j_{N-4}n_{N-2}j_{N-3}}}\!\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{N-1}n_{N}j_{N-3}}\!

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{v}_{n_{1}}\!\!\cdots\mathsf{v}_{n_{N}}\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\hskip 0.85358pt.

(238)

In the above, we have chosen the case with 2
\tilde{\rho}_{nm\ell}
’s and
(N\!\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4)

\tilde{\alpha}_{nm\ell}
’s as an example, whereas the results computed for other combinations of
\tilde{\rho}_{nm\ell}

and
\tilde{\alpha}_{nm\ell}
will give the same coupling coefficient

\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{v}_{n_{1}}\!\!\cdots\mathsf{v}_{n_{N}}\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt

as shown in Eq.(
238
). This is because, regardless of the numbers and positions of
\tilde{\rho}_{nm\ell}
and
\tilde{\alpha}_{nm\ell}
, the internal lines (indices)
can be summed over by using the completeness relation (
347
). The final result only depends on the 5d eigenfunctions

\{\mathsf{v}_{n_{i}}\hskip-0.85358pt\}
associated with the external states
\{\mathcal{V}_{n_{i}}^{\mu}\hskip-0.85358pt\}
. Similarly, for the scattering amplitudes containing
N
external states (with odd number
N
),
the KK scattering amplitudes will be proportional to KK masses
and thus have lower energy-power dependence. So there is no clear evidence that the double-copy construction would hold
for these massive amplitudes.

We can further prove the
N
-point LO double-copy relation by utilizing
the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) formalism
[
62
]
. We start by considering the
(4+\delta)
-dimensional spacetime with

\delta
extra spatial dimensions under the toroidal compactification. (Since we will study the KK scattering amplitudes at the leading order (LO) only,
the current discussion under toroidal compactification
will give the same results for the case of
orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
at the LO of high energy expansion.) For a momentum
\hat{p}_{a}
living in the
6d spacetime, we can decompose it in the following form:

\hat{p}_{a}^{M}=\left(p_{a}^{\mu}\hskip 0.85358pt;M_{n_{a}}\!,\,0\right),\qquad a\!\in\!\{1,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,N\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(239)

where
\,p^{\mu}_{a}\,
denotes the momentum in
the
(3\!+\!1)
d spacetime and
M_{n_{a}}\!
is the momentum-component in
the 5th dimension (realized as discretized KK mass-eigenvalues). For each polarization vector, we have two choices, depending on
whether it is restricted to the extra dimension or not. Thus, we can define the polarizations for each KK gauge boson and
its corresponding KK Goldstone boson as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{M}_{a,\mathrm{g}}=(\epsilon^{\mu}_{a}\hskip 0.85358pt;\,0\hskip 0.85358pt,\,0)\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad~~\epsilon^{M}_{a,\mathrm{s}}=(\vec{0}\hskip 0.85358pt;\,0\hskip 0.85358pt,\,1)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(240)

where the subscripts “g” and “s” stand for
the KK guage boson and KK Goldstone boson respectively. The LO scattering amplitude
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{0}
in either gauge theory or gravity theory
can be re-written as the expression of a contour integral:

\mathcal{A}_{N}=\oint_{\Gamma}\!z_{ij}^{2}z_{jk}^{2}z_{ki}^{2}\!\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=1\\
(a\neq i,j,k)\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\!\!\!\!\frac{\,\text{d}z_{a}\,}{\mathbb{E}_{a}}\hskip 0.85358ptI_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{a}=\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
(b\neq a)\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\!\!\frac{~p_{a}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{b}\!+\!M_{n_{a}}\!M_{n_{b}}\,}{z_{a}\!-\!z_{b}}\,,

(241)

where the contour
\Gamma
encloses all the solutions
for the scattering equations of
\mathbb{E}_{a}

and
I_{N}
is the theory-dependent integrand. Under high energy expansion, the above formulations
can be further derived at the leading order:

\mathcal{A}_{N}^{0}=\oint_{\Gamma}\!z_{ij}^{2}z_{jk}^{2}z_{ki}^{2}\!\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=1\\
(a\neq i,j,k)\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\!\!\!\!\frac{\,\text{d}z_{a}\,}{\mathbb{E}_{a}^{0}}\,I_{N}^{0}\,,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{a}^{0}=\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
(b\neq a)\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\!\frac{~p_{a}\!\cdot\!p_{b}~}{z_{a}\!-\!z_{b}}\,.

(242)

We note that in the high energy limit and for the KK Goldstone boson sector,
the 5d KK Yang-Mills gauge theory is reduced to a 4d KK Yang-Mills-Scalar (YMS) theory,
whereas the 5d KK Einstein gravity theory is reduced to
a 4d KK Einstein-Maxwell-Scalar (EMS) theory. According to the dimensional compactification of the CHY formalism
[
63
]
,
we can express the scalar integrands
for the YMS (single flavor) amplitude and the EMS amplitude as follows:

\displaystyle I_{N}^{0,\mathrm{YMS:s}}

\displaystyle=\,\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}{\mathcal{C}[\alpha]}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{PT}[\alpha](\mathrm{Pf}\hskip 0.85358pt{{\mathbb{X}}_{N}})(\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}\hskip-1.42262pt{{\mathbb{A}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(243a)

\displaystyle I_{N}^{0,\mathrm{EMS:s}}

\displaystyle=\,{c_{N}}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}^{2}(\mathrm{Pf}\hskip 0.85358pt{{\mathbb{X}}_{N}})^{2}(\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}\hskip-1.42262pt{{\mathbb{A}}_{N}})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(243b)

where the symbols Pf and
\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}
denote the Pfaffian and reduced Pfaffian respectively.
The matrix quantities
{\mathbb{X}}_{N}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{N}
are given by

({\mathbb{X}}_{N})_{ab}=\left\{\!\!\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\,z_{a}\!-\!z_{b}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\hskip 11.38109pt(\mathrm{for}~a\!\neq\!b),\\[2.84526pt]
0\hskip 0.85358pt,~\hskip 8.53581pt&\hskip 11.38109pt(\mathrm{for}~a\!=\!b),\end{aligned}\right.\hskip 28.45274pt({\mathbb{A}}_{N})_{ab}=\left\{\!\!\begin{aligned} \frac{p_{a}\!\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{b}}{\,z_{a}\!-\!z_{b}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\hskip 11.38109pt(\mathrm{for}~a\!\neq\!b),\\[2.84526pt]
0\hskip 0.85358pt,~\hskip 8.53581pt&\hskip 11.38109pt(\mathrm{for}~a\!=\!b).\end{aligned}\right.

(244)

In Eq.(
243a
), the color factor
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}[\alpha]
is decomposed into
the DDM basis and contains
(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2)!\hskip 0.85358pt
elements, as defined below:

\mathcal{C}[\alpha]\,=\sum_{e_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358pte_{N-3}}\hskip-11.38109ptf^{a_{1}a_{\alpha(2)}e_{1}}f^{e_{1}a_{\alpha(3)}e_{2}}\cdots f^{e_{N-3}a_{\alpha(N-1)}a_{N}}\,,

(245)

where the 1st and
N
-th labels are fixed in the color ordering
\alpha\hskip 0.85358pt
, i.e.,

\alpha\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\![1,\alpha(2),\cdots\!,\alpha(N\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!1),N]\!\hskip-0.85358pt\in\!S_{N-2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. The symbol
\mathrm{PT}[\alpha]
stands for the Parke-Taylor factor and is given by

\mathrm{PT}[\alpha]=\frac{1}{~(z_{1}\!-\!z_{\alpha(2)})\cdots(z_{\alpha(N-1)}\!-\!z_{N})(z_{N}\!-\!z_{1})~}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(246)

The above CHY formulation works for even number
N
, whereas for the odd number
N

the Pfaffian is trivially zero. This is consistent with the conclusions we reached earlier in this subsection. Namely,
in the case of
N
-point scattering (with even number
N
),
the leading-order double-copy holds. But, for the
N
-point scattering (with odd number
N
),
the KK GAET and GRET (which are connected by double-copy) will take the trivial form of

0\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt
at the leading order of high energy expansion. Note that the scattering equations are the same for both the KK YMS theory and the KK EMS theory. So the above LO double-copy relation (
243b
) holds under the replacement

\,c_{N}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptn_{N}}^{2}\!\!\!\!\longrightarrow\!\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptn_{N}}
. Using Eqs.(
242
)-(
243
) and the fundamental BCJ relations,
we derive the extended KLT-type double-copy formula for the
N
-point
LO scattering amplitude of gravitational KK Goldstone bosons:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}\big[\phi_{n_{1}}\!,\hskip-0.85358pt\cdots\!,\phi_{n_{N}}\big]=-\,c_{N}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptn_{N}}^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\kappa\,}{4}\right)^{\!\!N-2}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}\in S_{N\!-\!3}}\hskip-11.38109pt\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\big[\hskip-0.85358ptA^{a_{\alpha(i)}n_{\alpha(i)}}_{5}\big]\mathcal{K}[\alpha|\beta]\,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\big[\hskip-0.85358ptA^{a_{\beta(i)}n_{\beta(i)}}_{5}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(247)

where
\mathcal{K}[\alpha|\beta]
is the KLT kernel. Finally, we note that the CHY double-copy construction of the
N
-point LO scattering amplitude
of the vector-type gravitational KK Goldstone bosons

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\mathrm{dc}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{n_{1}}^{\pm 1},\!\cdots\!,\mathcal{V}_{n_{N}}^{\pm 1}\big]

can be similarly realized.

4.4
Prescription for Warped Double-Copy Construction at Leading Order

Our previous work
[
28
]
proved that for the compactified KK gauge/gravity theories,
only the toroidal compactification of flat extra dimensions can satisfy the mass spectrum condition
and directly realize extended double-copy of massive KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes. It was also proved
[
27
]
that
under the toroidal compactification of flat extra dimensions such massive KK double-copies can be
derived as the field theory limit of the massive KLT relations of KK open/closed string amplitudes. But we note that the compactified warped 5d gauge/gravity theories
with orbifold
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
(such as the RS1 model
[
19
]
)
clearly do not meet this criterion. This is because such warped KK gauge/gravity theories have highly nonlinear mass spectra
and the mass spectrum of KK gauge bosons differ from that of the KK gravitons due to the 5d warped metric,
whereas coupling coefficients of the KK gauge bosons and of the KK gravitons also differ significantly. Moreover, the
S^{1}\hskip-0.85358pt/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold compactification spoils the KK number conservation and
the KK scattering amplitudes do not exhibit single-pole structure in each channel. Nevertheless, as demonstrated in Sections
4.1
-
4.2
, for tree-level KK gauge/gravity
scattering amplitudes, we find proper ways (or restriction) to evade these problems. These include:
(i)
the 3-point double-copied KK graviton scattering amplitudes (from that of the KK gauge bosons)
exhibit exactly the same kinematic structure as the original 3-point KK grviton amplitudes even without
high energy expansion except that we need to set up a proper prescription for the correspondence
between the double-copied trilinear KK gauge couplings and the original trilinear KK graviton couplings,
whereas under such prescription
the double-copy works for the corresponding 3-point gravitational KK Goldstone
amplitudes only at the leading-order of high energy expansion;
9
9

9

We found previously
[
28
]
that for the gauge/gravity
KK Goldstone scattering amplitudes with toroidal compactification of flat extra dimensions,
the double-copy construction also works only at the leading order of high energy expansion.

(ii)
at the leading-order of high energy expansion,
the numerators of 4-point scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of KK Goldstone bosons
obey the kinematic Jacobi identities;

(iii)
the double-copied leading-order KK graviton scattering amplitudes
(constructed from that of the KK gauge bosons)
exhibit exactly the same kinematic structure as the original leading-order amplitudes of
KK grvitons except that we need to set up a proper prescription for the correspondence
between double-copied KK gauge couplings and the original KK graviton couplings.

Based upon the above observation and the double-copy analysis shown in Sections
4.1
-
4.2
,
we summarize the prescriptions for successful double-copy constructions of the 3-point and 4-point KK
graviton scattering amplitudes in the warped 5d gauge/gravity theories under orbifold compactification
of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
:

1).

For the present double-copy construction of
N
-point warped KK graviton scattering amplitudes from the corresponding
N
-point warped KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes, we first replace the 4d gauge coupling
g
by the 4d gravitational coupling
\kappa
as follows:

g^{N\hskip-0.85358pt-2}~\longrightarrow~\!\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-1.42262pt\left(\frac{\kappa}{4}\right)^{\!\!N\hskip-0.85358pt-2}\,.

(248)

For instance, this gives
\hskip 0.85358ptg\!\rightarrow\!-\kappa/4\,
for
N\!=\!3

and
\hskip 0.85358ptg^{2}\!\rightarrow\!-\kappa^{2}/16
for
N\!=\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
. This double-copy replacement (
248
) is the similar to what we established for the compactified flat 5d gauge/gravity theories
[
27
]
[
28
]
. The difference is that in the present analysis, due to an overall factor of
i
of the

N
-point (for
N\!=\hskip 0.85358pt
odd) gauge amplitude, the corresponding double-copied gravitational
amplitude has an overall factor of
-1\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the factor
(-)^{N+1}
in Refs.
[
27
]
[
28
]
will be replaced by
-1
in this study.

2).

Then, for the scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons (KK Goldstone bosons),
we apply the extended massive color-kinematics duality
and make the following group factor replacements (double-copy relations)
as in Eq.(
164
) for the 3-point KK amplitudes, or as in Eqs.(
183
)(
196
)
for the 4-point KK amplitudes:

\begin{array}[]{rlrll}f^{abc}&{\longrightarrow~}\mathcal{N}[\{\epsilon_{j}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt,&f^{abc}&{\longrightarrow~}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}[\epsilon_{3}]\hskip 0.85358pt,&\text{(3-point~amplitudes),}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{C}_{j}&{\longrightarrow~}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\mathcal{C}_{j}&{\longrightarrow~}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{0}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\text{(4-point~elastic~amplitudes),}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{C}_{j}&{\longrightarrow~}\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\mathcal{C}_{j}&{\longrightarrow~}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}\hskip 0.85358pt0}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\text{(4-point~inelastic~amplitudes).}\end{array}

(249)

3).

At each KK level-
n
, we replace the mass-eigenvalue
M_{n}
of KK gauge bosons
[determined by Eq.(
275
)] by mass-eigenvalue
\mathbb{M}_{n}
of KK gravitons
[determined by Eq.(
294
)], i.e.,
M_{n}\!\rightarrow\hskip-0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

4).

At each KK level-
n
, we further replace the involved KK gauge couplings
by the corresponding KK gravitational couplings as in Eq.(
173
) for the 3-point KK amplitudes
and as in Eqs.(
190
)(
202
)(
216
) for the 4-point KK amplitudes:

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747ptc_{3}a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}\!\!\rightarrow\alpha_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},\quad c_{3}a_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}\!\!\rightarrow\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},\quad c_{3}\tilde{a}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}}^{2}\!\!\rightarrow\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}n_{2}n_{3}},

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747ptc_{4}\tilde{a}_{nnnn}^{2}\!\rightarrow\tilde{\beta}_{nnnn}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\tilde{a}_{nnmm}^{2}\!\rightarrow\tilde{\beta}_{nnmm}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}\hskip 0.85358pta_{0nn}^{4}\!\rightarrow\alpha_{0nn}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747ptc_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}a_{000}^{2}a_{nn0}^{2}\!\rightarrow\alpha_{000}\alpha_{nn0}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~c_{4}^{\mathrm{in}}a_{000}^{2}a_{nn0}\tilde{a}_{nn0}\!\rightarrow\alpha_{000}\tilde{\alpha}_{nn0}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(250)

where the overall coefficient
c_{3}
or
c_{4}
is the relevant normalization factor and
its determination is given in Sections
4.1
-
4.2
. Finally, in Section
4.3
, we studied the double-copy construction
for the
N
-point (
N\!\!\geqslant\!4
) LO KK Goldstone boson amplitudes. Thus for the
N
-point LO double-copy, we impose the following correspondence (replacement)
between the KK gauge and gravity coupling coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\mathsf{f}_{n_{k}}\!\!\!\cdots\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n_{k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\hskip-0.85358pt\hskip 0.85358pt\llbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\tilde{\mathsf{f}}_{n_{k}}\!\!\!\cdots\mathsf{f}_{n_{k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\cdots\hskip 0.85358pt\rrbracket\hskip 0.85358pt\,\longrightarrow~\tilde{\alpha}_{n_{1}\hskip-1.42262pt\cdots\,n_{N}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(251a)

\displaystyle c_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt\tilde{a}_{n_{1}\!\cdots\,n_{N}}^{2}\,\longrightarrow~\tilde{\beta}_{n_{1}\cdots n_{N}}.

(251b)

5
Conclusions

In this work, we conducted comprehensive analyses on the structure of scattering amplitudes of massive Kaluza-Klein (KK) states in the compactified 5-dimensional warped gauge and gravity theories. We presented systematic formulations of the gauge theory equivalence theorem (GAET) and the gravitational
equivalence theorem (GRET) within the
R_{\xi}
gauge and up to loop level. Under high energy expansion, the GAET quantitatively connects the scattering amplitudes of longitudinal KK gauge bosons to that of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons, whereas the GRET connects
the scattering amplitudes of massive KK gravitons of helicity-0 (helicity-1) to that of the corresponding
gravitational KK scalar (vector) Goldstone bosons. A key point of our work is
to take the GAET of KK Yang-Mills gauge theories as the
truly fundamental ET formulation,
from which
the GRET of
the corresponding KK gravity theories can be reconstructed
by using double-copy
at the leading order (LO) of high energy expansion. We systematically studied the extended double-copy construction of
3-point and 4-point massive KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes at tree level. We proved that under color-kinematics correspondence and gauge-gravity coupling
correspondence, the double-copy of 3-point physical KK gauge-boson/graviton scattering amplitudes
can be realized without high energy expansion,
and for the corresponding 3-point gauge/gravity KK Goldstone
scattering amplitudes the double-copy can be achieved at the leading order (LO)
of high energy expansion. Moreover, we demonstrated that the double-copy can be realized for 4-point massive
scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of KK gravitons
(as well as their corresponding KK Goldstone bosons) to the
leading order of high energy expansion. In addition, we can reduce the validity of 4-point-level GAET and GRET
to the validity of 3-point-level GAET and GRET. Hence, the GAET and GRET formulations
at the level of 3-point KK scattering amplitudes are the most fundamental formulations,
from which the GAET and GRET formulations at 4-point-level of KK scattering amplitudes
can be inferred. We derived the GRET from GAET by LO double-copy constructions for
3-point and 4-point KK scattering amplitudes. We further extended this LO double-copy construction
to the general
N
-point gauge/gravity KK scattering amplitudes with
N\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
. A more elaborated summary of our results and conclusions in each sections are presented as below,
which is followed by a schematic summary shown in the last paragraph of this section
together with Fig.
3
.

In Section
2
, we proved the formulations of the GAET and GRET
within the
R_{\xi}
gauge and up to loop level. The GAET formulation was presented in Eqs.(
24
)-(
25
) and
Eq.(
26
), whereas the GRET formulations were given
in Eqs.(
71
)(
75
) for KK gravitions with helicity-0 (called type-I)
and in Eqs.(
57
)(
59
)
for KK gravitions with helicity-1 (called type-II). In essence, the GAET and GRET reflect the geometric “Higgs” mechanism through the KK compactifications,
which hold not only for the compactified flat extra dimensions
[
12
,
13
]
[
16
,
17
]
,
but also for the compactified warped extra dimensions as proved in Sections
2.2
-
2.3
. They determine the high energy behaviors of massive KK graviton scattering amplitudes and
ensure the nontrivial interlancing cancellations among contributions of different KK levels
as happened in the scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of KK gravitons.

In Section
3
, we analyzed the structure of 3-point and 4-point massive scattering amplitudes of
KK gauge bosons and of KK gravitons (as well as the corresponding scattering amplitudes of
KK Goldstone bosons), including the interconnections between the 3-point and 4-point KK amplitudes. In Section
3.1
,
we explicitly proved the warped GAET and GRET
for the fundamental 3-point massive scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of KK gravitons. We found that the nontrivial realization of GAET is given by the 3-point KK gauge boson amplitude
with two longitudinal external states and one transverse external state (of
LLT
type),
as shown in Eq.(
82
). We proved that to hold this 3-point-level GAET (
82
)
requires the condition (
83
) which is directly proved in Eq.(
352
)
of Appendix
D
. Then, we computed the 3-point KK graviton amplitude
10
10

10

We derived the most general 3-point scattering amplitude of KK gravitons in Eq.(
346
)
of Appendix
C
.

of helicities
(\pm 1,\pm 1,\pm 2)
and its corresponding KK Goldstone amplitude
in Eqs.(
91a
)-(
91b
)
and Eqs.(
92a
)-(
92b
). To hold the 3-point-level GRET (
93
) imposes the condition (
94
)
which we proved in Eq.(
354
) of Appdenix
D
. We further computed the 3-point KK graviton amplitude of helicities

(0,\hskip 0.85358pt0,\hskip 0.85358pt\pm 2)
and its corresponding KK Goldstone amplitude in
Eqs.(
97a
)-(
97b
). To hold the 3-point-level GRET (
98
) leads to the nontrivial condition
(
99
) which we proved in Eq.(
360
) of Appdenix
D
. We also computed the mixed 3-point KK graviton amplitude of helicities
(\pm 1,\pm 1,\hskip 0.85358pt0)

and its corresponding KK Goldstone amplitude in Eq.(
101
). To hold the GRET (
103
) imposes the condition (
104
)
which was proved in (
362
) of Appendix
D
.

In Section
3.2
, we further demonstrated that the validity of the warped GAET and GRET for
4-point massive KK scattering amplitudes can be effectively reduced to the validity of these theorems
for the 3-point massive KK scattering amplitudes. For the 4-point massive elastic and inelastic scattering amplitudes
of KK gauge bosons and KK Goldstone bosons,
we explicitly established the warped GAET as in Eqs.(
119
),
(
128
), and (
136
) respectively. We proved that the validity of the
4-point-level GAET in these cases is ensured by the validity of the fundamental
3-point-level GAET (
82
). Then, we analyzed the 4-point massive scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons and gravitational KK Goldstone bosons. We explicitly established the warped GRET as in Eq.(
145
) for the 4-body elastic scattering channel. The validity of the 4-point-level GRET (
145
) relies on the
sum rule condition (
146
), while the proof of Eq.(
146
)
requires the sum rule condition (
100
) to play the key role, where
the condition (
100
) just ensures the validity of the 3-point-level
GRET (
98
) in the case of
(n_{1}{},n_{2},n_{3})\!=\!(n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358ptj)
. This shows that validity of the 4-point-level GRET (
145
) is reduced to the validity
of the 3-point-level GRET (
98
). We further computed the mixed 4-point inelastic scattering amplitudes
of KK gravitons and of KK Goldstone bosons
as in Eqs.(
159a
)-(
159b
). Under high energy expansion, we found that the leading-order amplitudes
(
160a
)-(
160b
) are simple enough in this case. Thus, we explicitly established the 4-point-level GRET (
161
)
without a need of additional sum rule condition.

In Section
4
, we studied the double-copy construction of the massive gravitational
KK scattering amplitudes from the corresponding massive KK gauge scattering amplitudes
for the warped 5d gauge and gravity theories with the orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. This is highly nontrivial and challenging since it was proved
[
28
]

that the direct construction of double-copy for
N
-point massive gauge/gravity KK
scattering amplitudes (with
N\!\!\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt\!4
) can directly work out
only for toroidal compactifications with flat extra dimensions. Nevertheless, we could realize double-copy construction
for the warped massive gauge/gravity theories
with proper restrictions and prescriptions so as to evade the previous conditions
[
28
]
. In Section
4.1
, we newly proved that the double-copy can be constructed for
the 3-point full scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons at tree level
for warped massive gauge/gravity theories. We set up the 3-point color-kinematics (CK) correspondence and
the gauge-gravity coupling correspondence as in Eq.(
164
),
and the KK mass replacement
M_{n}\!\rightarrow\hskip-0.85358pt\mathbb{M}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
at each KK level. With these, we first presented the general 3-point doubel-copy formulas (with any
polarization tensors of physical KK graviton states) as in

at the level of 3-point KK scattering amplitudes.

In Section
4.2
,
we demonstrated that the extended double-copy construction can be achieved
for the 4-point KK gauge/gravity scatttering amplitudes
at the leading-order (LO) of high energy expansion. For the 4-point elastic scattering
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)\hskip 0.85358pt
, the LO amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and
of KK Goldstone bosons have their effective numerators connected by the
generalized gauge transformations (
181
) and obey the kinematic Jacobi identities
(
182
). The double-copies of these two LO amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and of
KK Goldstone bosons give the corresponding LO amplitudes of KK gravitons
and of the gravitational KK Goldstone bosons as in
Eqs.(
184
) and (
186
). Using the coupling conversion (
190
), we derived the double-copied LO gravitational
KK amplitudes (
191
) which agree with the same amplitudes (
187
)
(by explicit Feynman diagram approach). In parallel, we studied the double-copy constructions for the LO inelastic KK gauge/gravity
scattering amplitudes, including the processes
(n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)
(with
n\!\neq\!m
),

(n,m)\!\rightarrow\!(\ell,q)
(with
n\!\neq\!m\!\neq\!\ell\!\neq\!q
), and
(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)

(with
n\!>\!0
). We found that their effective numerators satisfy the kinematic Jacobi identity
respectively, as shown in Eqs.(
195
), (
207
)-(

),
and so on. For these inelastic processes, we presented the double-copied inelastic amplitudes
of KK gravitons and of gravitational KK Goldstone bosons
as in Eqs.(

), and
(
213
)(

). In Section
4.3
, we further established that this LO double-copy construction
can be extended to the general
N
-point KK scattering amplitudes with
N\!\!\geqslant\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
. In Section
4.4
, we summarized a set of well-defined prescriptions for
realizing the extended double-copy constructions in the warped massive KK gauge/gravity theories,
which include the double-copy constructions for 3-point full KK gauge/gravity amplitudes
and the double-copy constructions for the 4-point LO KK gauge/gravity amplitudes.

Figure 3
:
Schematic Summary of the present analyses: Equivalence Theorems and Double-Copy Correspondences from 3-point scattering amplitudes to 4-point scattering amplitudes and from massive KK gauge scattering amplitudes to massive KK gravitational scattering amplitudes at the leading order of high energy expansion.

Finally, we present a schematic summary of the present analyses as in Fig.
3
. In this figure, we start from the horizontal bottom row in which all entries describe the
basic 3-point KK scattering amplitudes. From the left to right, the long equality sign linking
the first two entries gives the “GAET(3)” which connects the 3-point LO longitudinal KK
gauge boson amplitude
\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(3)}[A_{L}^{n}]

to the corresponding LO KK Goldstone boson amplitude
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{(3)}[A_{5}^{n}]
; then the long equality sign
linking the last two entries gives the “GRET(3)” which connects
the 3-point LO longitudinal KK graviton amplitude

\mathcal{M}_{0}^{(3)}[h_{L}^{n}]
to the corresponding LO gravitational KK Goldstone boson amplitude

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{(3)}[\phi_{n}]
; in the middle, the horizontal arrow indicates the extended LO double-copy “DC
3
”
which constructs the 3-point LO longitudinal KK graviton (KK Goldstone) amplitude from the
3-point LO longitudinal KK gauge boson (KK Goldstone) amplitude. After this, we see that all the entries and equality signs (or arrow) in the top row are doing the
same jobs as those in the bottom row except that all entries in the top row deal with
the 4-point KK gauge/gravity scattering amplitudes. Finally, the vertical arrows from bottom to top indicate the 4-point-level “GAET(4)” and “GRET(4)”
can be reduced to (reconstructed from) the fundamental “GAET(3)” and “GRET(3)” for the basic 3-point KK
gauge (gravity) amplitudes. Furthermore, along the horizontal top row
we can construct the “GRET(4)” from “GAET(4)” through the extended LO double-copy “DC
4
”.
